Press (Redburn 08-27): off-grid gas + batteries, ~$71bn campus capex.** ➤ **Two things for this page's ladders: (1) a FILED gross↔IT conversion — 1.37/2.0 = 0.685 — for a gas micro-grid campus (generation losses + cooling + auxiliaries), tighter than the PUE-only ratios this page has used; (2) 2 GW of turbines + BESS to be delivered by 1H28 against [[GEV]] sold out through 2030 and 2-3 yr turbine lead times (09-14 block) — the filing names no turbine supplier.** ⚠️ **Never divide Nscale's contract revenue by gross MW: on IT MW the Anthropic tranche is ~$16.2bn/GW/yr, on gross MW ~$11.1bn.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-17 — **THE CONSTRAINT ON FRONTIER AI *RESEARCH* IS NAMED FROM INSIDE A LAB, AND IT IS GPUs AND SERIAL EXPERIMENT TIME — A THIRD CALL ON COMPUTE THAT SITS ALONGSIDE TRAINING AND SERVING AND THAT NO CAPEX MODEL ON THIS WIKI BREAKS OUT.**

_(Source: Dwarkesh Podcast — *“Noam Brown – Agent swarms, alignment, & recursive self-improvement”*, **2026-09-17** (guest **Noam Brown**, research scientist at OpenAI). Digest + verbatim raw: [`_equity_calls/Overall/2026-09-17_Dwarkesh_Noam-Brown-agent-swarms-alignment-RSI.md`](../../_equity_calls/Overall/2026-09-17_Dwarkesh_Noam-Brown-agent-swarms-alignment-RSI.md). ⚠️⚠️ **NOT A DISCLOSURE, NOT RESEARCH, AND — IMPORTANT FOR THIS PAGE — IT CONTAINS NO GW, NO MW, NO POWER FIGURE AND NO SITE DATA OF ANY KIND.** It is filed here only because it speaks to what BINDS compute demand. No number on this page is superseded, confirmed or moved. Only claims the digest tags **[BROWN]** are used. Primary ingest on [[OPENAI]] / [tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md) / [ai-cybersecurity](ai-cybersecurity.md); compute-demand read-through on [[NVDA]].)_

- 🔴🔴 **THE QUOTE:** asked how much faster progress runs once AIs do AI research — *“I don’t think it’s an overnight intelligence explosion where we go 100x faster, because we do get bottlenecked by certain limitations that are **not bottlenecks of intelligence**. It’s running experiments. It’s running experiments **serially**… **It’s having the GPUs to run those experiments.**”* Point estimate **~3x**, with 50% possible and 10x *“unlikely… but possible.”* ➤ **This page tracks the constraint stack — power, shells, permitting, switchgear, transformers, turbines — as the thing that gates DEPLOYED capacity. Brown is describing a constraint one level up: even with capacity built, research throughput is gated by GPU availability and by the WALL-CLOCK of training runs. Serial experiment time is a bottleneck that more gigawatts do not relieve.**
- 🔴 **AND THE ELASTICITY, WHICH IS WHY IT BELONGS ON A CAPACITY PAGE AT ALL:** asked whether 100x less compute plus all the best people would mean 100x less progress — *“**No, not 100x less**”*, but *“I suspect it would be **less progress**… it would **definitely** be less.”* ➤ **Sublinear but strictly positive. Research compute is a real, persistent claim on capacity that is NOT captured by token-serving demand models — and unlike serving, it does not scale down when a product’s usage does. ⚠️ Entirely qualitative: no GPU count, no spend, no share of fleet, and “sublinear” is unquantified. Nothing here can be converted into GW.**
- ⚠️ **THE FORWARD-COMPUTE SHAPE BROWN ENDORSED, AND WHY IT IS *NOT* ADOPTABLE.** Host-constructed framing that Brown called *“pretty accurate”*: *“By the end of next year, OpenAI will have enough compute such that… let’s say you have 10,000 agents at the end of next year… Each of them will have enough compute to run a **GPT-3-sized experiment every single day**.”* ➤ **⚠️⚠️ HOST-ASSERTED, RESEARCHER-UNCONTESTED — NOT AN OPENAI CAPACITY GUIDE. Nothing is sized: no GW, no GPU count, no date beyond “end of next year”, and no definition of “GPT-3-sized”. It must NOT be chained into any capacity, capex or power estimate on this page. Logged as the SHAPE of the argument a lab researcher endorsed, nothing more.**
- ✅ **ONE ADDITIVE, NON-DISCRETIONARY LOAD THAT *IS* MECHANISTIC: Brown commits OpenAI to *“chain-of-thought monitoring during evaluations, during deployment, during training for **any frontier model**”*** ➤ **A permanent monitoring overhead on every served token for every frontier model — the concrete mechanism behind the framing this wiki carries that AI safety is *“another Compute workload”*. ⚠️ Unsized: no overhead %, no cost, no latency. Directional only.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-16 (/wiki-ingest 21h — UBS Taiwan Summit + BofA's AI Infra Summit) — **THE POWER CHAIN PUTS NUMBERS ON THE NEXT TWO YEARS: DELTA AND LITE-ON AT 70%+ POWER-SUPPLY GROWTH IN 2026 AND SUSTAINING INTO 2027, ASIC POWER RACKS FIRST AND NVIDIA TIMED TO RUBIN ULTRA, 800V A LATE-2027/2028 DRIVER — AND NVDA CLAIMS 40% MORE COMPUTE OUT OF THE SAME POWER ENVELOPE**

_(**UBS · Randy Abrams / Jerry Su / Diana Chang** — "2026 Taiwan Summit Summary and Day 3 Hardware Takeaways", 2026-09-16, meetings incl. Lite-On, Hon Hai, Quanta, Auras, Compeq · **BofA · Vivek Arya** — AI Infra Summit Day 1, 2026-09-15 · **@QQ_Timmy** relaying UBS's AI-power APAC work)_

- 🔴🔴 **THE SUPPLIER NUMBERS, FROM THE SUPPLIERS: **Delta and Lite-On both confident in 70%+ power-supply growth in 2026, sustaining into 2027**; **Lite-On's cloud segment is now 50% of sales, targeted at 70-80% y/y in 2026 and "could sustain 70%+ in 2027"**, with **initial ASIC power racks and 110kW shelves for Vera Rubin already ramping in Q3**. **Sequencing that matters: power growth is supported FIRST by ASIC power racks, with [[NVDA]] timed to Rubin Ultra "or at the latest Feynman"**, and **rising costs being passed on from Q4**.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE 800V TIMING QUESTION GETS AN ANSWER THAT CUTS AGAINST THE CONSENSUS: Lite-On is *"confident on 800V as a steeper driver LATE 2027 into 2028"*, and UBS's own AI-power model (via @QQ_Timmy) argues **the market is over-focused on an 800V migration that is running slow — 2027 growth comes from deployed GW and architectural upgrades, NOT from high-voltage going mainstream** (2027 drivers: accelerator die area ~+65% y/y plus Rubin-class platform content). **UBS's bottom-up AI-server POWER TAM: US$91.1bn by 2030 on a 69% CAGR from 2025, front-loaded on volume and back-loaded on content-per-watt.** ⚠️ **$/watt HAZARD: that is a component-REVENUE TAM per server power, not a build cost and not revenue-per-GW — do not chain it to the $30-60bn/GW build basis or to NVDA's $18/25/40bn revenue-per-GW.
- 🔴 **THE EFFICIENCY LEVER, WITH A VENDOR NUMBER: [[NVDA]] showcased **DSX MaxLPS, which it says extracts ~40% more compute/throughput/revenue from the same DC power envelope**, and BofA cites the **8GW OpenAI / SB Energy / NVDA Ohio site** as the co-design template. **BofA ranks power as the SECOND binding constraint after memory and ahead of interconnect, with all three "tighter into 2027".** **If a 40% power-efficiency uplift is real at rack level, it is simultaneously bullish deployable-GW and bearish the linear "GW → revenue" extrapolations this page has to keep policing.
- 🔭 **Content read-through carried for the component names: **Auras expects new ASIC programs ramping from 4Q26 with ASIC revenue potentially ~40% of 2027 sales and SURPASSING its NVIDIA exposure in 2H27, driven by higher content per rack and full-rack liquid cooling**; **Hon Hai targets 40% AI-server share adding neo-clouds while holding OpM >3.2%**; **Quanta sees triple-digit AI-server growth with Vera Rubin sampling in Q4**. **The liquid-cooling and power-rack content story is being underwritten by ASIC programs first and by NVIDIA platforms second — the reverse of 2025.

- 🔴🔴 **THE CROSS-NAME STATEMENT, AND IT IS THE CLEANEST MULTI-COMPANY CORROBORATION THIS PAGE HAS OF ITS OWN CENTRAL CLAIM: _"NVIDIA, Broadcom, Microsoft, CoreWeave and Nebius all described demand above available capacity into 2027-2028, with POWER, SHELLS AND PERMITTING REPLACING CHIPS AS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT"_** (GS Communacopia 2026 — conference takeaways, 2026-09-14). ➤ **This page has assembled that conclusion since August out of analysts, credit desks and one-off management remarks — UBS on Broadcom's ramp being tied to powered-shell readiness, Morgan Stanley's October-2026 contract deadline, JPM's 8-to-12-18-month build stretch, Hood on nothing coming online inside 12 months. Here it is FIVE MANAGEMENTS across four layers of the stack saying it in the same week.** ⚠️ **It is a note-taker's synthesis ACROSS sessions, not a single disclosure — no GW, no dollars, no dates — but as corroboration it is the strongest item of its kind on the page.**
- 🔴 **[[MSFT]] — THE STAGING LADDER AND WHY THE CHEAP END IS BOUGHT FIRST: LAND then COLD SHELL then POWERED SHELL then GPUs/CPUs, where LAND AND POWER ARE CHEAP TO CARRY AND BOUGHT AHEAD while COMPUTE IS LATE-BINDING and needs customer, workload and silicon visibility; today's decisions primarily affect FY28, FY29, FY30 and beyond, and the FY27 lever is pulling kit-deployment dates forward and accelerating the fill of existing facilities** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Microsoft small group, 2026-09-14). ✅ **CONFIRMS the 09-09 Microsoft IR meeting and the 09-11 powered-shells-continue-to-be-our-constraint fireside already on this page. What is added is the ORDER OF COMMITMENT stated as policy: the option value sits in land and power, and the kit is the strike — which is why a capex line can rise years before a capacity line moves.** ⚠️ **No acreage, no MW, no dollars.**
- 🔴🔴 **[[CRWV]] — THE POLITICAL CONSTRAINT PRICED, NOT JUST OBSERVED: CoreWeave has MORE THAN 1GW OF CONTRACTED POWER OUTSIDE THE UNITED STATES, and management expects political opposition to SHIFT WHERE CAPACITY IS BUILT AND RAISE ITS COST — NOT TO REDUCE DEMAND** (GS Communacopia 2026 — CoreWeave broadcast, 2026-09-14). ➤ **The ex-US gigawatt figure is already on [[CRWV]] from the Q2 call; the NET-NEW half is the COST clause. This page has carried siting backlash as a TIMING risk — the ERCOT audits, the Texas halt, the Vineland fight, the October-2026 signing deadline for year-end-2027 energisation. Management is now saying it is also a PRICE risk, and that the industry PAYS it rather than shrinking to fit.** 🔴 **AND THE COMPETITIVE COROLLARY, WHICH IS THE SENTENCE TO KEEP: management expects INDUSTRY CONSOLIDATION, distinguishing ANNOUNCING several gigawatts from actually CONSTRUCTING AND OPERATING THEM, which favours operators with proven execution and financing.** ⚠️ **No names, no capacity, no timeframe — and it is an incumbent arguing that capital access is the filter. Read it against the offtake-spread scoreboard already on this page (CoreWeave-offtake project bonds ~300-600bp wider than Amazon-offtake paper on physically comparable assets), which says CoreWeave itself is not on the hyperscaler side of that filter.**
- 🔴 **[[NBIS]] — PERMITTING AS A SCHEDULED INPUT RATHER THAN AN EVENT RISK, PLUS THE FIRST EXPLICIT REGULATION-IS-A-MARGIN-TAILWIND FRAME ON THIS PAGE: management says permitting timelines are ALREADY EMBEDDED IN DEPLOYMENT SCHEDULES, providing flexibility if further hurdles emerge, and confirmed a FAVOURABLE ZONING OUTCOME at the Vineland (NJ) site; the Bloom Energy partnership is framed as lower-emission power that improves project economics, COMMUNITY ACCEPTANCE and future site development** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Nebius broadcast, 2026-09-14). ✅ **The Vineland item CONFIRMS the Bloom-fuel-cell approval already logged on [[NBIS]]; the embedded-schedule claim is net-new and is an ASSERTION, not an outcome.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE FRAME THAT MUST NOT BE ADOPTED SILENTLY: Goldman's own read from the same session is that regulatory challenge is an INDUSTRY-WIDE issue that could WORSEN SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS AND THEREFORE LIFT PRICING AND MARGINS, with Nebius relatively well positioned on geographic diversification, execution buffers and community engagement. That converts a risk into a tailwind by assuming the company sits on the right side of it — which is exactly what the Vineland fight has been testing. Logged as the HOST HOUSE's framing, NOT as this page's view.** ➤ **The structural point holds either way, and it is the one this page should carry forward: if permitting is the binding constraint and it binds UNEVENLY, it is a SHARE mechanism between operators before it is a growth mechanism for the industry — and there is still no measured elasticity anywhere on this page linking permitting delay to realised price.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/wiki-ingest, 09:40 window) — **THE BOTTLENECK IS RE-RANKED BY THE PEOPLE WHO SELL INTO IT: POWER SEMIS ARE *NOT* THE BINDING CONSTRAINT — SWITCHGEAR IS ~18 MONTHS AND TRANSFORMERS AND ON-SITE GAS TURBINES ARE 2-3 YEARS+. PLUS AN 800VDC SLIP THAT MOVES $500m-1bn OF TAM, AND THE TWO KOREAN MEMORY MAKERS REFUSING TO PREPAY $18.7bn FOR POWER**

🔴🔴🔴 **UBS · Francois-Xavier Bouvignies publishes the AI power-semis deep dive and the most valuable paragraph in it ARGUES AGAINST HIS OWN SECTOR: *"Power supply is tightening but is UNLIKELY THE KEY BOTTLENECK. While power semiconductor lead times are rising, we view ELECTRICAL INFRASTRUCTURE as an EARLIER bottleneck to AI buildout. Lead times for BACKUP POWER SYSTEMS AND SWITCHGEAR extend to ~18 MONTHS and BEYOND 2-3 YEARS for UTILITY SCALE TRANSFORMERS AND ON-SITE GAS TURBINES."*** ➤ **He then kills the efficiency consolation directly: *"power semis can improve efficiency but against that backdrop, a 5-10% improvement in power delivered to the GPU, while beneficial, is UNLIKELY TO MOVE THE NEEDLE MATERIALLY."*** ⚠️ **Solid-state transformers are named as the longer-term answer but *"meaningful adoption remains a POST-2028 story AT THE EARLIEST."*** ✅ **This is a sell-side analyst with a Buy-rated basket telling you his own products do not relieve the constraint — the highest-credibility form of a bottleneck claim, and it ranks the queue for this page: SWITCHGEAR AND BACKUP (18m) → TRANSFORMERS AND TURBINES (2-3yr+) → power semis (rising but not binding).**

🔴🔴 **THE 800VDC ARCHITECTURE SLIPPED A YEAR AND IT IS NOW SIZED: high-volume adoption of the 800VDC power SIDECAR moves to 2028 from 2027, which UBS says *"will likely impact the power semis TAM"* by $500m-1bn.** The sensitivity is published: UBS assumes **60% sidecar penetration on Rubin Ultra in 2028E**, and at 100% it would add **$400-500m** to TAM. ➤ **The content difference is the number worth keeping: Rubin Ultra WITH a power sidecar requires $170m/GW of power semis versus $119m/GW for INTEGRATED power racks — a ~43% content uplift riding on a single architecture decision, driven by the battery backup unit, protection, and the new high-voltage IBC.** ✅ **Corroborated from an unrelated desk the same morning: UBS Global Battery Supply Chain (Tim Bush) published *"Rubin moves batteries OUT OF THE TRAY"* — the battery relocation IS the sidecar.**

🔴 **TAM AND CONTENT-PER-GW, RAISED: AI power semis $13bn by 2028E (from $11bn) → $15bn by 2030, on a rebuilt BOM covering 30+ xPU generations. Content per GW $100-200m/GW, average rising $95m/GW (2024) → $130m/GW (2026E) → $150-170m/GW beyond.** ⚠️⚠️ **SEVENTH PER-GIGAWATT UNIT ON THIS PAGE. $m/GW of POWER-SEMI CONTENT is not $/GW BUILD COST, not $/GW REVENUE, and not $/W RENTAL. The page already warns it carries six mutually non-comparable per-gigawatt units; this is the seventh.** 🔴 Separately, UBS · Randy Abrams sizes the wider layer bottom-up: **AI SERVER POWER TAM at US$91.1bn by 2030, a 69% CAGR from 2025** — front-end loaded, with rising content/watt sustaining growth as the stack moves from integrated PSUs → dedicated power racks → 400/800V distribution → solid-state transformers. Named beneficiaries: **Delta (~40% share) and Lite-On**, plus MLCCs (Murata/TDK/Semco) and storage (Panasonic/Samsung SDI). ⚠️ **$91.1bn AI-SERVER-POWER is a different base from $13-15bn POWER-SEMIS — the latter is a component subset of the former.**

🔴🔴 **THE DEMAND-SIDE REFUSAL THAT MATTERS MOST, AND IT COMES FROM THE BUYERS: [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]] have REJECTED KEPCO proposal that they pay W25 TRILLION (~$18.7bn) UP FRONT to fund power infrastructure for Korea planned semiconductor mega-clusters — citing UNCERTAINTY OVER LONG-TERM SEMICONDUCTOR DEMAND DURABILITY (Reuters, from a document submitted to a lawmaker).** ➤ **A page that tracks who will finance power now has a case where the most informed possible counterparties DECLINED to, and said why. Logged as the producers refusing DURATION RISK on power, not as a demand-collapse call — a prepayment is a financing structure with its own logic.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/wiki-ingest) — **NVIDIA PUTS ITS OWN NUMBERS ON THE $/GW LADDER — $60bn TO BUILD A GIGAWATT, $50bn/YEAR OF RENTAL REVENUE OUT OF IT, "PAYBACK IN 15 MONTHS" — WHICH CORROBORATES TWO MARKS THIS PAGE HAD REFUSED TO ADOPT AND CONTRADICTS NVIDIA'S OWN FINANCING BEHAVIOUR; PLUS ANTHROPIC'S 14.8GW / $517bn AGGREGATE, WHICH CANNOT BE DIVIDED**
_(Jensen Huang at GS Communacopia, **2026-09-10**, relayed by Rothschild & Co Redburn US Riser **2026-09-11** and GS · James Schneider takeaways 2026-09-10 · The Information, **2026-09-13** · SpaceX CFO **Bret Johnsen** at GS Communacopia via The Information, **2026-09-11** · MS · **Adam Jonas**, "Prometheus Shrugged", 2026-09-11)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE FIRST-PARTY $/GW PAIR, FROM NVIDIA, AT A PUBLIC CONFERENCE:** *"NVDA claimed build costs of **$60bn / 1GW** generated **$50bn / year in rental revenues**, suggesting **payback in 15 months**."* ✅ **THE RENTAL LEG RECONCILES THREE PREVIOUSLY UNADOPTED MARKS. $50bn/GW/year is identical to [[NBIS]]'s $40-50M/MW/year and to [[GOOG]]'s $50/watt [[SPCX]] rental — and it is the same order as the $40-50M/MW figure @zephyr_z9 attributed to Anthropic/OpenAI on 09-09, which this page logged as ~3x the 22V $16M/MW mark and explicitly did NOT adopt. ➤ With NVIDIA's own number now on the record, the high end of that range has a first-party source and the 22V ~$16M/MW mark is the outlier that needs explaining, not the other way round.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **BUT THE PAYBACK CLAIM IS REFUTED BY NVIDIA'S OWN CONDUCT, AND THE ANALYST RELAYING IT SAYS SO IN ONE LINE:** Redburn — *"The 15 month payback claim will be DIFFICULT FOR INVESTORS TO RECONCILE WITH INCREASING NVDA FINANCING INVOLVEMENT."* ➤ **An asset that returns its cost in 15 months does not require the vendor to backstop its buyers. Either the payback is overstated, or the financing is doing something other than bridging economics. This is the cleanest one-sentence statement of the circular-financing debate the wiki has, and it comes from a Buy-rated analyst.** ⚠️ Note $60bn/GW sits at the TOP of this page's $30-60bn/GW build-cost band — the payback ratio would be better still at the bottom of it, which makes the 15 months a conservative-looking number built on an aggressive-looking cost.
- ⚠️⚠️⚠️ **UNIT TRAP LOGGED, BECAUSE THE DIGITS COLLIDE:** the same Jensen appearance disclosed per-chip ASPs of **Hopper ~$18,000 · Blackwell ~$25,000 · Vera Rubin ~$40,000** (rack $8.5m). These share the sequence 18 / 25 / 40 with this wiki's **$18bn / $25bn / $40bn revenue-per-GIGAWATT** ladder and are a DIFFERENT QUANTITY. A 1GW Hopper fleet at ~700W/chip is ~1.4M chips × $18k ≈ $26bn, not $18bn. ➤ **NEVER CHAIN THE TWO. Per-chip dollars and per-GW billions are not convertible without a power-per-chip and a utilisation assumption, neither of which was given.**
- 🔴🔴 **A SEVENTH BASIS ARRIVES AND IT IS THE ONE MOST LIKELY TO BE MISUSED: [[ANTHROPIC]]'s cloud agreements now total *"at least **14.8 GIGAWATTS** of compute capacity, costing as much as **$517 BILLION** over the next decade"* (The Information, 09-13; counterparties Google, SpaceX, Nscale, Rum Group).** ⚠️⚠️⚠️ **DO NOT DIVIDE. $517bn ÷ 14.8GW = ~$35bn/GW of TOTAL contract value over ~10 years, i.e. ~$3.5bn/GW/YEAR if 14.8GW were live on day one — versus the $40-50bn/GW/YEAR rental rate above, a ~14x gap. The gap is RAMP TIMING: 14.8GW is an end-state, not an average deployed base, and no deployment curve has been published. The pair is a contract-value aggregate and nothing else. NOT ADOPTED as a $/GW basis; logged here so the next reader does not do the division.**
- 🔴 **THE SHORTEST-DURATION CONTRACT DISCLOSURE ON THIS PAGE, AND IT REVALUES A LOT OF BACKLOG:** [[SPCX]]'s CFO says a new compute rental deal will pay *"about **$1.11 BILLION PER MONTH** starting on Dec. 1"* (~$13.3bn annualised), after the **$920m/month Google agreement** — but that this deal, *"like **ALMOST ALL** of SpaceX's agreements to provide computing capacity to customers, spans roughly **SIX MONTHS**."* ➤ **A ~$100bn annualised compute revenue run-rate assembled from six-month commitments is a materially different asset from [[ORCL]]'s $664bn RPO or a five-year take-or-pay. Renewal rate and renewal price become the whole question, and nobody has published either.**
- 🔴 **MS's SENSITIVITY GIVES THE PAGE A CLEAN EQUITY-VALUE-PER-GW CONVERSION:** for [[SPCX]], *"each incremental 1GW of nameplate compute draw (at **$50/watt**, assuming **70% incremental margin**), capitalized at **10x EBITDA**, could add **$27 to the stock**, roughly 20% of the current share price"* — with MS modelling **4.9GW at year-end FY27 against the company's ~10GW target**. ⚠️ The $50/watt is the same unit as the rental marks above (i.e. $50bn/GW/yr), NOT a build cost.

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-11 (/run-inbox) — **THE MISSING LEG OF EVERY CAPACITY MODEL ON THIS PAGE — *WHO UNDERWRITES THE CREDIT* — GETS ITS ARCHITECTURE: FIVE "GIGASCALERS" HAVE BEEN **GATEKEEPING THE INVESTMENT-GRADE CAPITAL THE WHOLE BUILD RUNS ON**, [[NVDA]]'s BACKSTOPS ARE A DELIBERATE COUNTER-ANCHOR TO THAT GATE, AND THE THREE STRUCTURES IT USES CARRY **OBLIGATION INTENSITIES PER GW THAT DIFFER BY ~6x**.**

_Source: **SemiAnalysis (Daniel Nishball, Oliver Kennon, Terence Ong) "Nvidia's Backstop Universe – Heads I Win, Tails Who Loses?", 2026-09-11** ([source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nvidias_Backstop_Universe_Heads_I_Win_Tails_Who_Loses_.html)). ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT RESEARCH HOUSE, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model in the equity sense.** Figures are SemiAnalysis's own *AI Compute, Capital and Markets Model* / *AI Datacenter Model* unless sourced to [[NVDA]]'s 10-Q or Hut 8's 8-K, and the forward paths are explicitly framed by the authors as a capacity test — *"this does not represent our base case or a definitive forecast"* — **NOT ADOPTED as forecasts.** ⚠️ **NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS SUPERSEDED: no facility-GW mark, IT-load-GW mark, build-cost anchor, $/MW rental price, project-bond yield, IRR, rating or PT already carried is replaced.**_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE THESIS THIS PAGE DID NOT HAVE, AND IT REFRAMES EVERY LEASE ON IT: THE CONSTRAINT ON THE BUILD IS NOT ONLY POWER, IT IS *WHO IS ALLOWED TO BORROW AGAINST IT*. Verbatim: the Gigascalers — Amazon, Microsoft, Google, Meta, Oracle — *"have been GATEKEEPING THE INVESTMENT GRADE CAPITAL that the AI buildout runs on and have benefitted tremendously from the HIGH SPREAD BETWEEN OFFTAKE PRICE THEY ARE BEING CHARGED, AND SPOT/SHORT-TERM PRICE THAT THEY ARE SELLING AT as well as services that sit on top."* In response [[NVDA]] has been *"MANUFACTURING ALTERNATIVE CREDIT ANCHORS so Neoclouds and Neolabs can raise money WITHOUT the Gigascalers"* — selling GPUs at full margin on day one and then flooring the buyer's revenue, guaranteeing the buyer's landlord, or signing the lease itself, *"all so that NON IG-RATED OPERATORS CAN EFFECTIVELY BORROW AT IG RATED PRICING."*** ➤ **The stated objective is a market-structure objective, not a financing one: SemiAnalysis's datacenter team reads it as *"to have TWENTY BUYERS AT SCALE RATHER THAN JUST FOUR."* Read against this page's own 08-29 finding that the SAME gigawatt earns a 10% or a 45% IRR depending on who you sell it to — the offtaker's credit rating is the variable behind both, and this is the first source here to name the rent it produces and who collects it.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE SCALE PUTS [[NVDA]] IN PROPORTION — THE GIGASCALER LEASE BOOK IS MULTIPLES OF THE BACKSTOP BOOK EVERYONE IS ARGUING ABOUT. [[MSFT]], [[META]], AWS ([[AMZN]]) and [[ORCL]] lease roughly **15GW of third-party capacity in 2026**, rising to **more than 35GW by 2028** (**~39GW including [[GOOG]]**), on leases that *"typically run FIFTEEN TO TWENTY YEARS, and developers BORROW AGAINST THEM WITH INVESTMENT GRADE PRICING."* Against that, NVDA is a tenant for only **~1.5GW** and backstops **~6.5GW**, *"equivalent to JUST UNDER 3% of the approximately **240GW OF NET GROWTH IN GLOBAL AI IT CAPACITY** forecast… between year-end 2025 and year-end 2030."* Even the authors' deliberately maximal expansion scenario — **~46.6GW gross, of which 28.2GW would sit under the capital partnership at only a 25% residual-value guarantee** — is *"LESS THAN ~20% OF TOTAL PLANNED AI IT CAPACITY ADDITIONS THROUGH CY2030."*** ➤ **Their conclusion is the one to carry: *"the AI industry is simply TOO BIG for Nvidia to fund the overall buildout through circular financing."*** The top-down denominator behind it: **~$11 TRILLION of cumulative AI capex CY24 through CY29.** ⚠️ **Model output, single house, no methodology published — logged as the note's own anchor, NOT adopted into this page's capex series.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT GUARD — THE THREE NUMBERS BELOW ARE A *THIRD CLASS* OF PER-CAPACITY UNIT AND BELONG TO NEITHER RUNNING TALLY ON THIS PAGE. They are **MAXIMUM CREDIT EXPOSURE PER UNIT OF CAPACITY ENABLED** — an obligation intensity. They are **NOT revenue per watt** (the $16M/MW/yr contract-revenue basis, the $18/25/40bn NVDA revenue/GW, the $40-50M/MW rental ladder) and they are **NOT build cost per GW** (~$30-60bn/GW, [[NBIS]] ~$50bn/GW). **NEVER net them against, subtract them from, or chain them into a series with either.** What they measure is how much guarantee [[NVDA]] must write to make one gigawatt financeable:**
  · **AI Cloud Partner (AICP) revenue floors — ~$59bn of obligation per GW enabled (~$58.6M/MW).** The program backstops GPU rental contracts for six years at *"approximately $2.35/hr/GPU on average for the GB300 vs the $4.50 to $4.60 range for 5y contracts"*; first disclosed at **$36bn** in the 2Q F1/27 10-Q, three deals announced (Firmus Batam 360MW = $21.1bn, SharonAI = $4.2bn, GMI ≈ $2.2bn), and **reportedly PAUSED** two weeks before publication.
  · **PORTS-Pike-style land/power/shell (LPS) guarantees — ~$25bn per GW (~$24.7M/MW)**, i.e. *"more than TWICE THE CAPACITY PER DOLLAR as AICP"* on a gross-commitment basis.
  · **Residual-value guarantee under the capital partnership — ~$9.4bn per GW**, which the authors call *"the most bang for buck"* — *"though the DATACENTER LEG STILL NEEDS TO BE SOLVED FOR."*
  ➤ **That last clause is the whole point for this page: the cheapest credit structure is the one that does NOT deliver a powered shell. Capital is elastic; the site is not.**
- 🔴🔴 **WHY THE DATACENTER LEG IS THE HARD ONE, AND THE MECHANISM HAS A NAME — *CREDIT SUBSTITUTION*:** *"Developers want BOTH AN INVESTMENT GRADE TENANT AND A LONG LEASE, making the DATACENTER LEG THE HARDEST LEG TO SOLVE FOR within the AI Project Trinity."* A datacenter is financed against NVDA **as an AA-rated offtaker**, and the lease is then handed to a neocloud. The worked example is on this page's own map: **Hut 8 funded the first Beacon Point building with $4.25bn of SENIOR SECURED NOTES at 6.129%, rated Baa2, amortising from 2030** — terms *"NO NEOCLOUD COULD PRICE ON ITS OWN NAME OR FOR THAT TERM"* — and **Hut 8's July 8-K shows TWO 352MW Beacon Point leases at $9.8bn each over fifteen years, $19.6bn in total lease payments.** ⚠️ **And the risk does not actually transfer on reassignment: SemiAnalysis believes NVDA *"likely stays PRIMARY OBLIGOR until the project significantly DE-LEVERS and/or clears a DSCR TEST"*, because *"lenders that bought Baa2 paper on an AA tenant WILL NOT CONSENT TO A B-RATED ONE."*** ➤ **This is the pricing complement to the 08-20 credit block already on this page (AMZN-offtake paper ~7.9-8.8% vs [[CRWV]]-offtake paper 8.2-14.2% on physically comparable data centres): there the bond market charged for offtaker quality; here a vendor guarantee BUYS the rating outright, at 6.129% on a Baa2 tranche. ✅ Beacon Point is already on this page from two other angles — the [[ANTHROPIC]]/Lambda 6yr/$35bn/~350MW contract at ~$16M/MW/yr (09-01) and the ERCOT-queue read that on-time energisation is *"most relevant/good for HUT"* (08-17). Hut 8 and Lambda have NO wiki page — plain text, read-through only.**
- 🔴 **PORTS-PIKE, MECHANICALLY, AND IT CORROBORATES THE 08-17 SIGNING RATHER THAN CHANGING IT: SB Energy develops the ~8GW Ohio campus through **17 BUILDING-LEVEL LANDLORD SPVs, each with a separate 20-YEAR TRIPLE-NET LEASE to an [[OPENAI]] affiliate** — *"these project SPVs give lenders a defined set of assets and lease receipts to underwrite."* NVDA guarantees **land, power and shell for ~4.25GW capped at $105bn**, support *"becoming effective IN PHASES as buildings reach ready for service, with the FIRST EXPECTED IN FISCAL 2029"*; exposure declines as OpenAI pays and **terminates altogether if OpenAI reaches a satisfactory credit rating**; and there is an **option on a further 3.8GW that would take the cap to roughly $200bn** — *"if Nvidia DECLINES to backstop this phase, then OpenAI has to find an ALTERNATIVE GUARANTOR WITH AN A- CREDIT RATING OR BETTER."*** ⚠️ **Basis check before anyone re-quotes the option: the note writes the second phase as "3.8 GW" in the deal description and "3.75 GW" in its own forecast paragraph; this page's 08-17 signing carried 3.75GW. Both as printed, neither reconciled, nothing changed.** ➤ **The A- fallback clause is the most investable sentence in the deal: it prices what the guarantee is actually worth — the campus is financeable ONLY with an IG name attached, whoever supplies it.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE "DURATION MISMATCH" IS REALLY A POWER ARGUMENT, AND IT IS THIS PAGE'S OWN THESIS ARRIVING FROM THE CREDIT SIDE. On the fifteen-year lease standing against a six-year offtake: *"gets described as a DURATION MISMATCH, but we would frame it as OPTIONALITY"* — at year six NVDA can re-tenant with upgraded GPUs and *"touch only the power equipment"*, or let the operator renew and run the existing hardware at spot. *"BOTH PATHS ARE WORTH MORE IN A WORLD WHERE INTERCONNECT QUEUES RUN FOUR TO SEVEN YEARS."* And the closing statement of the note is a straight scarcity claim: *"POWER IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT ON THE BUILDOUT, so EVERY POWERED SITE IS SCARCE and WHOEVER LOCKS THE SITE UP OWNS THAT CAPACITY FOR A GENERATION OF HARDWARE OR MORE."*** ➤ **A financing desk independently reaching the conclusion this page has built from utilities, turbines, permitting and labour — and it explains why the leases are fifteen-to-twenty years while the compute contracts are five-to-six: the LEASE is the instrument that captures interconnection scarcity, the offtake is not. Any model that treats the tail years as dead time is mispricing the asset in exactly the direction the queue is moving.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE CONCENTRATION RISK SITS UNDER THE WHOLE FINANCING STACK, NOT JUST UNDER [[NVDA]]: *"OPENAI IS 97% OF THE GUARANTEE CAP, and the agencies are worried about how much of it points at one customer."* NVDA can spread the next tranche, *"but it cannot diversify away the fact that TWO FRONTIER LABS ARE THE END PAYERS BEHIND ALMOST EVERY FLOOR, LEASE AND GUARANTEE. That means that A DEMAND SHOCK AT THE LABS HITS EVERYTHING, ALL AT ONCE."*** ➤ **Read directly against the 09-09 Bernstein tracker on this page — stranded capacity at a record 12% of a 404GW pipeline, of which only ~35% is judged credible. The stranding channel that note measures is REGULATORY; this is the FINANCIAL one, and it is correlated across every project at once rather than site by site.** ✅ **And the playbook is not NVDA-only: *"This playbook is not lost on GOOGLE AT $75.5BN OF GUARANTEES AND META AT $59.0BN OF OBLIGATIONS"* — two Gigascalers writing the same instrument they gatekeep.**
- ➤ **Cross-filed [[NVDA]], [[OPENAI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[MSFT]], [[META]], [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]], [[ORCL]], [[CRWV]], [hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md).**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-09 (/wiki-ingest, 21:00 window) — **A HYPERSCALER PPA IS NOW UNDERWRITING THE LIFE EXTENSION OF A EUROPEAN NUCLEAR PLANT; MICROSOFT'S CFO SAYS NEXT YEAR'S CAPEX *CANNOT* BECOME NEXT YEAR'S CAPACITY; LAM NAMES CLEAN-ROOM SPACE AS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT THROUGH 2027; AND NVIDIA's 800VDC TRANSITION TURNS OUT TO BE RETROFITTABLE RATHER THAN A FORKLIFT.**

_(Bloomberg / CNBC / @cazenove_uk / @zerohedge on Google-Fortum; **MANAGEMENT** — [[MSFT]] CFO **Amy Hood** @ GS Communacopia, [[LRCX]] CFO **Doug Bettinger** @ Citi and [[NBIS]] CEO **Arkady Volozh** @ Citi, all via TMT Breakout desk relay; @SemiAnalysis_ on 800VDC; Morgan Stanley "AI Sovereignty" webcast, 2026-09-09.)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE MECHANISM IS NEW AND IT IS NOT A NEW BUILD: the [[GOOG]]-FORTUM AGREEMENT IS "THE FIRST SUCH AGREEMENT OF ITS KIND IN EUROPE" AND "WILL HELP UNLOCK €1 BILLION OF INVESTMENT NEEDED TO KEEP A NUCLEAR POWER PLANT IN FINLAND RUNNING FOR DECADES TO COME" (Bloomberg, 09-09).** ➤ **Every nuclear datapoint on this page so far has been NEW capacity — SMRs, restarts, behind-the-meter. This one monetises EXISTING generation by extending its life, which is faster, cheaper per MW and politically easier than anything else on the page. ## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-09 (/wiki-ingest) — **THE PIPELINE PASSED 400GW AND THE SAME ANALYST SAYS ONLY ~35% OF IT IS CREDIBLE. STRANDED CAPACITY HIT A RECORD 12%. AND A SIXTH $/GW BASIS ENTERED CIRCULATION — THIS PAGE NOW HAS SIX MUTUALLY NON-COMPARABLE PER-GIGAWATT UNITS.**
_(Bernstein · **Chad Dillard** (August data-centre tracker), **2026-09-09**; Jefferies · **Jonathan Petersen** (REITs) roadshow feedback relayed by Larissa Utiyama, **2026-09-08**; Goldman Sachs · James Schneider (AVGO/Hock Tan), **2026-09-08**; BofA · Justin Post (META), **2026-09-09**; Morgan Stanley · Shane Brett (semicap) and Thomas Wigg (TMT sales), **2026-09-08/09**; Cantor via @cazenove_uk and @KobeissiLetter via @dnystedt, corpus, **2026-09-07/08**)_
- 🔴🔴 **THE BEST QUANTIFICATION OF PIPELINE-vs-REALITY THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED. Bernstein's August tracker: the project pipeline SURPASSED 400GW, growing 23GW MONTH-OVER-MONTH TO 404GW**, while **rolling TTM additions have STALLED at 246GW** and **stranded capacity continues to rise FASTER than construction.** Pipeline growth was led by **[[AMZN]] (~6GW of hyperscaler additions)**, plus **Fermi (10.5GW), Galaxy (4GW) and TECfusions (3GW)**, with **TEXAS the largest geographic increase at 14.5GW.** And the judgement: **Chad "continues to view ONLY ~35% OF THE TOTAL PIPELINE, OR 170GW, AS ULTIMATELY CREDIBLE, with HYPERSCALER SELF-BUILDS carrying the HIGHEST confidence level at 79% CREDIBILITY (73GW) and COLOCATION PROVIDERS at 67% (55GW)."** Capacity **under construction rose 4GW to ~70GW, with [[AMZN]] contributing 2GW and becoming the FIRST OPERATOR TO EXCEED 10GW UNDER CONSTRUCTION**, QTS and STACK driving most of the remaining colo growth. **The key watch item: STRANDED CAPACITY ROSE 7GW IN AUGUST AND NOW REPRESENTS A RECORD 12% OF THE TOTAL PIPELINE** as regulatory scrutiny, moratoria risk and voter opposition rise; **behind-the-meter projects added another 14.7GW in the month** and "are becoming a larger contributor to BOTH pipeline growth AND stranded capacity."
- ➤ **THE THREE GW ANCHORS NOW ON THIS PAGE DO NOT AGREE, AND THE DISAGREEMENT IS THE FINDING: Bernstein's **404GW pipeline of which 170GW credible**; Jefferies' supply-constrained model at **~66GW of DEPLOYMENTS over the next five years reaching ~111GW OF INSTALLED NORTH AMERICAN CAPACITY BY 2030 (~50.8GW colocation + ~60.6GW hyperscaler self-builds)**; and Cantor's relayed demand-side claim that **"2028 could require 40 TO 50GW of AI capacity, with leases needing signing now"** plus **"[[AVGO]] points to 10GW of [[ANTHROPIC]] capacity in 2028."** Bernstein's credible 170GW is ~1.5x Jefferies' 2030 installed base and ~3.5x Cantor's 2028 requirement. These are different quantities measured differently — pipeline vs deployment vs requirement — and this page should carry all three without averaging them.**
- 🔴🔴 **JEFFERIES SUPPLIES THE BEST MECHANISM: "EASY POWER" IS GONE. Petersen's framing, relayed verbatim: "all the EXCESS CAPACITY HAS ALREADY BEEN TAKEN, and from here on it will require GREENFIELD, more complex projects. THE LAST FOUR YEARS WERE EASY, GOING FORWARD NOT SO MUCH."** He admits his estimates "may be a bit conservative" but thinks **"assuming it's possible to deliver 2x what he has is also too much."** The leasing shift is the investable part: **leased facilities are ONLY ~21% OF INSTALLED HYPERSCALER CAPACITY TODAY, BUT ~47% OF FUTURE CAPACITY PLANS ARE LEASED** — "a growing share of infrastructure growth is expected to be delivered by third-party operators." Key constraints in his model: **LABOR and ELECTRICAL EQUIPMENT (SWITCHGEAR IN PARTICULAR)** — which [[VRT]]'s management independently corroborated at Communacopia the same week by saying it **"continues to work through supply chain challenges."**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE REGULATORY NUMBERS ARE NOW SPECIFIC ENOUGH TO MODEL: Governor Abbott launched an audit of ALL power in the ERCOT queue, which ERCOT will not complete until DECEMBER (after the midterms), LEAVING BATCH ZERO CLASSIFICATION SUSPENDED in the meantime**; **data centres are ~90% of NEW ERCOT power requests, with OVER 474GW IN QUEUE AGAINST A ~95GW INSTALLED GRID**; **New York's executive order pauses environmental permits for data centres of 50MW OR MORE for UP TO A YEAR**; **over $64BN of projects nationally have been BLOCKED OR DELAYED by local opposition**; and **Pennsylvania is the latest, with an executive order that "effectively GRANTS LOCAL COMMUNITIES BLOCKING RIGHTS."** Petersen's conclusion is the sentence to keep: **"the demand backdrop is UNCHANGED; what changed is THE ABILITY TO CONVERT A PIPELINE MW INTO AN ENERGIZED MW, and APPROVED CAPACITY JUST REPRICED UPWARD."** ➤ **That is the cleanest statement of why scarcity accrues to whoever already holds interconnection rights.**
- 🔴🔴 **$/GW HAZARD — THE COUNT IS NOW SIX, AND TWO OF THEM ARE NEW THIS RUN. None of these may be netted, chained or averaged against another: **(1) BUILD COST ~$30-60bn per GW**; **(2) [[NVDA]] REVENUE per GW $18 / $25 / $40bn**; **(3) 22V CONTRACT REVENUE $16M per MW per YEAR**; **(4) NEW — [[AVGO]]'s $35bn XPV partnership "funding MORE THAN 20GW of compute capacity", i.e. <$1.75bn of FINANCING STRUCTURE per GW** (GS · Hock Tan, 09-08); **(5) NEW — BofA on [[META]]: "can LICENSE this capacity (at UP TO $50BN GW)", a LICENSING-REVENUE per GW basis 2-3x NVDA's revenue/GW** (09-09); **(6) MS semicap's WFE ratio — "$7BN OF WFE PER $100BN OF AI CAPEX", cross-checked as $1.2TN of 2027 hyperscale capex / 29GW of compute (~$41bn of CAPEX per GW) implying 2027 WFE of $204-242BN vs MSe $223BN.**** ⚠️ **Six units, six denominators, one shared symbol. Every one of them is logged as printed and none is adopted as interchangeable.**
- 🔴 **THE MACRO CONSTRAINT GOT ITS SHARPEST QUANTIFICATION YET, AND IT IS A RATES ARGUMENT: JPM's Cembalest, relayed — "hyperscalers are issuing SO MUCH DEBT that they may be causing a SUPPLY-DEMAND ISSUE AT THE LONG END OF THE YIELD CURVE. In 2026 so far, debt issuance by the FIVE HYPERSCALERS PLUS NVIDIA is ~$320BN (including SPVs for which these companies are backstop obligors on data center leases). Looking just at the LONG DURATION component in 2026, we estimate $303BN IN TEN-YEAR EQUIVALENTS. THAT'S 68% OF NEW TREASURY LONG DURATION BORROWING THIS YEAR."** And a second relay: **"to fund next year's $1.3TN in capex, the hyperscaler complex will issue MORE DV01 THAN THE US TREASURY."** ⚠️ **Relays of a chart with no methodology shown — NOT adopted. Logged because it is the first attempt to size the crowding-out effect and it now has a named author. MS's own European semis note independently lists "higher long-end yields and oil prices adding to investor scrutiny around [[NVDA]]'s investments across the AI ecosystem" among four year-end risks, and MS is hosting a dedicated "Alts & AI Debt Issuance" lunch (09-22) and a "Credit + Compute" webcast (09-10).**
