## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-08 — **THE SUPPLY-CONSTRAINT ANATOMY, FROM THE SUPPLIER'S OWN CEO: CHIPS, WAFERS, MEMORY AND SUBSTRATES ARE "PRETTY WELL LOCKED IN" AND "MORE WITHIN OUR CONTROL" — THE PART THAT IS NOT DETERMINISTIC IS A *POWER-READY SITE*, AND THE BINDING ITEM HE NAMES IS CONSTRUCTION AND LOCAL PERMITTING. PLUS THE $30bn-ARR-PER-GW SPLIT DECOMPOSED, CORE WEAVE ON DISEQUILIBRIUM TO END-DECADE, AND ~$450bn OF AI DEBT ISSUED YTD.**

_Sources: **Hock Tan, CEO, [[AVGO]]** — Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference, **2026-09-08** ([BBG **INITIAL DRAFT** transcript](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AVGO_GS_08-09-26.html), **PRIMARY / MANAGEMENT**); **Michael Intrator, CEO, [[CRWV]]** — same conference, same day ([BBG **LIVE** transcript](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CRWV_GS_08-09-26.html), **PRIMARY / MANAGEMENT**); **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak et al** — *"The Morgan Stanley AI Guidebook: Navigating the Age of Inference"*, 99pp, **2026-09-07** ([source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260907_2101.html)). ⚠️ **Both transcripts are BBG DRAFT/LIVE ASR and will be superseded by FINALs. ⚠️ NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS SUPERSEDED — no facility-GW mark, build-cost anchor, $/MW price, rental price, IRR, rating or PT already carried is replaced, and NO NEW per-GW BASIS IS CREATED (see section 2).**_

⚠️ **RELATIONSHIP TO THE 2026-09-09 /wiki-ingest BLOCK DIRECTLY ABOVE — AND TO ITS $/GW COUNT, BECAUSE THE TWO ENUMERATIONS ARE NOT THE SAME LIST.** That block covers the SAME Hock Tan fireside via a **GS relay** (James Schneider) and takes a different item from it — the **XPV financing structure**, logged there as *"<$1.75bn of FINANCING STRUCTURE per GW"* — which is **NOT restated here**. Its running hazard list (build cost · NVDA revenue/GW · 22V $16M/MW/yr · AVGO XPV financing/GW · BofA's META licensing/GW · MS's WFE-per-capex ratio) is a **CIRCULATING-UNITS tally**, a different object from the **5-row LABELLED BASIS TABLE further down this page**. **This block adds NO row to that table and NO unit to that tally** — Hock's $30bn is the table's existing *labs' own annual revenue per GW* row, restated and decomposed. **Read both lists; do not merge them; do not net anything across either.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 (1) THE CONSTRAINT, ANATOMISED BY THE PERSON WHOSE FORECAST DEPENDS ON IT (Hock Tan, management, 2026-09-08)

- 🔴🔴 **THE TWO HALVES, IN HIS ORDER. FIRST THE HALF THAT IS SOLVED: *"we understand what it takes to supply chain for chips. We understand the need for **wafers, leading edge wafers, and memory, as well as even substrates**. And **that's pretty well locked in. We can pretty well lock that in for sure in '27, and the process of locking in '28**"* — *"then it's **more within our control**."* **Then the half that is not:** *"we find out that's **not enough** when you want to put together AI data centers. **You need power, you need a site, you need a building, you need a facility to house your chips for it to run.** And that's the other part of the constraint."***
- 🔴🔴 **AND HE GRADES THEM AGAINST EACH OTHER, WHICH IS THE PART THIS PAGE HAS NEVER HAD FROM A SUPPLIER: *"I'm not saying chips and memory are not constraints, they are constraints. But at least there's constraints we can work with and **MORE DETERMINISTIC**. **What is LESS DETERMINISTIC is a power site that is power ready** — no, that's power available but **not ready**."* And the lead time is stated as an instruction: *"if you want to make it power ready, say in **2028, you've got to START CONSTRUCTION ON THE SITES NOW**. And **that's not us. OUR CUSTOMERS have to start construction on the site now** to make it available and ready by 2028. And that's two years out, almost two years, a year and a half out."***
- 🔴 **THE BINDING ITEM IS NOT THE EQUIPMENT, AND HE SAYS SO EXPLICITLY: *"a lot of it is **not just equipment**, whether they are **transformers, gas turbines**, that obviously are the pieces that go in. **It's also CONSTRUCTION. Construction including LOCAL PERMITTING**, though some of it exists, but also **the RATE OF CONSTRUCTION**. You're talking about those of you trying to renovate or build your own houses, how construction is — that's what, at large scale, this is happening."*** ➤ **This page has tracked transformers, turbines and interconnect queues for months. The largest merchant AI-silicon supplier puts the *civil* constraint — permitting and physical build rate — ABOVE the equipment constraint. Read it against the 09-05 backlash block on this page: local permitting is now named as a supply-chain input by a semiconductor CEO, not just as a political risk.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE MOST-QUOTED GUIDE IN SEMIS — the $115bn FY27 / $230bn FY28 AI numbers already on this page and on [[AVGO]] are, in his own words, **DE-RATED FOR POWER**: *"if I turn around and give you guys a forecast, which we do, we want to be sure that's a forecast that is **very clear line of sight**… **It's a JUDGED NUMBER based on not just supply of memory, wafers, but AVAILABILITY AND READINESS OF POWER SITES**."*** ✅ **This CORROBORATES and extends what this page logged from the 09-02 Q3 call (that AVGO diligences each customer's land, power and site readiness and haircuts its own guide accordingly) — now stated as the construction method of the forecast itself, and with the two-year construction start-date attached. ⚠️ The guide figures themselves are unchanged and are NOT restated here.**

### ⚠️⚠️🔴 (2) THE GW VALUE-CHAIN SPLIT — **READ THE BASIS TABLE ON THIS PAGE BEFORE QUOTING ANY OF THIS**

- **What he said, verbatim: *"Take the case of a very good frontier model. **One gigawatt could generate $30 billion ARR**, as you know. **The cost to run one gigawatt, as you all know, is about $10 billion a year.** So you have **$20 billion that accrue to the model guys and the application that goes with it**, **$10 billion fought over by the power guy, the cloud provider guys, even the chip and memory guys**."* His conclusion: *"the value comes, I think, in creating the best models and through it, the best products to monetize the applications."***

| Slice of one GW-year | Amount | Who gets it, in his words |
|---|---|---|
| **Gross revenue the GW generates** | **~$30bn ARR** | the frontier-model operator running it |
| **Cost to run it** | **~$10bn/yr** | *"fought over by the power guy, the cloud provider guys, even the chip and memory guys"* |
| **Residual** | **~$20bn** | *"the model guys and the application that goes with it"* |

- ⚠️⚠️⚠️ **BASIS DISCIPLINE — THIS IS *NOT* A NEW SERIES AND IT MUST NOT BECOME ONE.** The **$30bn** is **THE OPERATOR'S / MODEL LAYER'S OWN REVENUE PER GW-YEAR**. It is **THE SAME BASIS** as the row already in this page's 5-row per-GW basis table — *"THE LABS' OWN ANNUAL REVENUE per GW · ~$30bn · Hock Tan, Q3 FY26 call, 2026-09-02"* — **restated at a second event and now DECOMPOSED for the first time.** ➤ **DO NOT create a sixth row for it. DO NOT net it against, chain it to, or difference it from: (a) [[AVGO]] CHIP CONTENT per GW ($20-30bn — a different quantity from the same speaker on the same page), (b) the ~$11-12bn implied-revenue-per-GW arithmetic that was an analyst's question and never a company figure, (c) [[NVDA]]'s $18/25/40bn full-rack REVENUE per GW, (d) the ~$30-60bn ALL-IN BUILD COST per GW, or (e) the $16M/MW/YEAR contract-revenue mark. Five bases, one denominator — the standing `$/watt` rule on this wiki applies in full.**
- 🔴 **WHAT *IS* NET-NEW IS THE DECOMPOSITION, AND WHERE THE SPEAKER PUTS HIMSELF: the ~$10bn cost-to-run line is the FIRST time this page has the $30bn figure split into an operating cost and a residual by the person who quoted it — and **Broadcom's own CEO places his company inside the CONTESTED $10bn bucket**, alongside the power provider and the cloud provider, not in the $20bn that accrues to the model and application layers.** ➤ **Read commercially: the supplier is telling a room of investors that two-thirds of the economics of a gigawatt accrue to his CUSTOMERS, and that his own share is fought over with the utilities, the clouds and the memory makers. That is a striking thing for a supplier to volunteer, and it is the same argument he makes on token economics (see [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md)).**
- ⚠️ **AND NOTE THE HEDGE HE ATTACHES TO BOTH FIGURES.** *"as you know"* / *"as you all know"* — these are presented as consensus arithmetic circulating in the room, not as Broadcom disclosures. Neither has a stated model, utilisation assumption, chip generation or price/token behind it. **Logged as a value-chain FRAMING, never as a modelled input.**

### 🔴 (3) CORE WEAVE — DISEQUILIBRIUM TO THE END OF THE DECADE, AND A FLEET DELIBERATELY *NOT* SEGMENTED BY WORKLOAD (Michael Intrator, CEO, 2026-09-08)

- ⚠️ **ASR WARNING BEFORE ANY QUOTE.** This transcript mangles the proper nouns badly — **"coral reef", "Corey" and "Curaleaf" all decode to CoreWeave, and "Mike Andrada" is Mike Intrator**. Names are decoded here on first use and **no garbled name is reproduced verbatim anywhere in the quotes below**.
- 🔴🔴 **THE SUPPLY-DEMAND CALL, REITERATED AND DATED: the interviewer frames it as CoreWeave having *"consistently said that we might not be at supply demand equilibrium until the END OF THE DECADE"*, and Intrator does not walk it back — *"we have never flinched from our assessment that **the world's capacity to deliver compute HAS BEEN AND WILL CONTINUE TO BE WILDLY OVERWHELMED** by the delivery and demand for intelligence, broadly speaking. And we've been righter than wrong on that one."* He later refers to *"**exiting this period of DISEQUILIBRIUM**"* as the strategic objective.** ➤ **A named end-of-decade horizon for supply-demand balance, from the operator with the most exposure to being wrong about it. It is an assertion with no capacity model behind it — but it is the demand-side bookend to Hock's supply-side answer above, given in the same building on the same day.**
- 🔴 **AND THE ARCHITECTURAL POINT THAT MATTERS FOR EVERY GW FORECAST ON THIS PAGE: CoreWeave **does not segment training vs inference infrastructure at all**. *"we don't really think about our infrastructure as being, oh, this is our training infrastructure and this is our inference infrastructure. **We think of it as AI INFRASTRUCTURE.** And we build to a standard that allows our clients to use that infrastructure in whatever way they deem to be most productive for their businesses"* — built that way deliberately, because *"you want to build your infrastructure to have **the most amount of OPTIONALITY embedded in it as possible**."*** ⚠️⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS PAGE'S ARITHMETIC: any "training GW vs inference GW" split applied to a neocloud fleet is an ANALYST'S CONSTRUCT, not an operator's. The 09-07 Jensen/Equinix item at the top of this page (gigawatt sites for training, thousands of smaller facilities for inference) describes a SITING pattern; it is not a statement that a given fleet is dedicated. Do not model the two as separate capacity pools for CRWV.**
- **On why it is vertically integrating downward into physical data centres, and the answer is NOT margin.** *"there are two reasons… first one is… you can **recapture some margin**. And I would say **that's the LESSER of the two**. The second thing is… **this stuff is DIFFICULT**. It's not 'hey, I've got some megawatts and I'm going to turn that into a supercomputer.' There's a lot of steps along the way there"* — control, learning and *"a better sandbox"* for the innovation flywheel. **The first 2-3 self-builds are spinning up now.*** ➤ **Corroborates this page's recurring finding that the scarce input is not megawatts but the ability to convert megawatts into working compute.**
- **On the wave of multi-GW announcements from new entrants (*"ten gigawatts over the next two years, seven gigawatts over the next two years"*): *"the difference between **launching a company and talking about a contract you've signed** and **actually being able to OPERATIONALIZE and DELIVER and MAINTAIN that infrastructure**… the more experience you have with it, the more you realize how difficult it is"*, and capital-intensive industries *"will tend towards periods of proliferation and… periods of consolidation."*** ⚠️ **Self-interested from an incumbent, but it is the same haircut logic this page applies to announced-GW pipelines. ✅ His >1GW of contracted power OUTSIDE the US is a REITERATION of the Q2'26 call figure already on this page (08-11) — not restated as new.**

### 🔴 (4) THE FINANCING CONSTRAINT ALONGSIDE THE POWER CONSTRAINT (Morgan Stanley AI Guidebook, 2026-09-07)

- 🔴 **THE NUMBER.** *"Despite **~$450bn of AI-related debt issuance globally year-to-date**, we think there's still **significant capacity to absorb additional issuance**, especially from high-quality hyperscalers. **Rather than constraining issuance, the market adjustment is likely to come through WIDER SPREADS.**"* The coverage argument underneath it: *"we see the 4 main builders ([[AMZN]]/[[GOOG]]/[[MSFT]]/[[META]]) generating accelerating OCF, set to reach **$980bn/$1.2tn in '27/'28… compared to only $290bn of debt needed to be raised over that period**"*, i.e. hyperscaler OCF *"expected to be **7-8X larger than required debt**."* Separately: **AI-related debt issuance YTD is "already more than DOUBLE FY2025 issuance."*** ⚠️ **This is a MARKET-CAPACITY view, not a cost-of-capital view — it explicitly predicts the adjustment shows up in SPREADS. Pair it with the 6.35% capped Blackstone IRR on [[WMB]]'s 2.7GW JV logged on this page 09-03: that is what the price of behind-the-meter capital looks like when the market is still absorbing.**
- 🔴 **AND MS PUTS POWER AT THE TOP OF ITS OWN BOTTLENECK LIST, WHICH IS THIS PAGE'S THESIS FROM A DIFFERENT DESK: *"We view state and local pushback to data centers as **largely manageable**, although remain focused on the buildup to the **'28 election** as a political risk to exacerbating this bottleneck. **TIME TO MARKET ON POWER will remain a SIGNIFICANT BOTTLENECK, which leaves us CONSTRUCTIVE ON POWER SUPPLIERS.**"*** ✅ **Independently consistent with MS policy strategist Ariana Salvatore's *"conditional buildout"* framing already on this page (09-05) — the research desk and the policy desk land in the same place, which is worth noting because the page carried only the policy-desk version.**
- **THE CAPACITY FRAME THE ABOVE IS FINANCING, for reference only: MS sees *"total compute capacity growing ~4X from **35 GW in '25 to ~145 GW in '28**"*, with **ASICs rising from 34% of incremental capacity in '25 to 66% in '28**, and data-centre capex *"growing 60% ($550bn) in '27"* before decelerating to *"~12% growth in '28 ($170bn)."*** ⚠️ **CAPEX AND MIX ARE NOT THIS PAGE'S — routed to [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md) and [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md), and carried here only as the denominator for the debt figure above. ⚠️ Note the unit: "total compute capacity" in GW, layer unspecified — do NOT assume facility GW; use the Arete conversion ladder already on this page whenever a source says "GW" without saying which layer.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — **THE BARGAINING POWER IN DATA-CENTRE CONTRACTS IS MOVING FROM THE HYPERSCALER TO THE OPERATOR, THE SLA TERMS UNDERNEATH THE BACKLOGS ARE FAR HARSHER THAN ANY MODEL ASSUMES, AND POWER PRICES FOR THE SAME MONTH VARY BY 400% BETWEEN CUSTOMERS.**

**(The Information · Phoebe Liu, "Desperation to Get Data Centers Online Is Reshaping Companies' Bargaining Power", 2026-09-07 — *reporting on unnamed data-centre and credit executives; no company confirmed any of it*)**
- 🔴 **THE SHIFT: "the biggest cloud providers usually have had the upper hand in negotiating to rent capacity from newer clouds [[CRWV]], [[NBIS]] and Nscale… BUT THINGS ARE CHANGING: cloud providers like [[MSFT]] are SO DESPERATE to ensure the server racks they've ordered from [[NVDA]] can get working as quickly as possible that DATA CENTER OPERATORS NOW HAVE MORE POWER TO NEGOTIATE DOWN THE RISKS THEY SHOULDER."**
- 🔴🔴 **WHAT THE OLD TERMS ACTUALLY WERE, AND THIS IS THE DISCLOSURE-QUALITY POINT OF THE WEEK: "the starting point for the big cloud providers in many deal talks is to get the neocloud to commit to ensuring EVERY SERVER RACK IS RUNNING ALMOST 100% OF THE TIME"; temperature and humidity requirements "that can be ALMOST IMPOSSIBLY STRICT"; and one executive "has seen proposals where IF JUST ONE RACK WENT DOWN (due to a power failure, thermal overload, broken switches or other outage), THE BIG CLOUD COULD VOID SIX MONTHS OF RENT." Sources add that "SLA DEFAULTS ARE NOT UNCOMMON; CUSTOMERS USUALLY JUST DON'T ACT ON THEM."**
- ➤➤ **BOTH HALVES MATTER AND THEY CUT OPPOSITE WAYS. The penalty structure is a contingent liability of a severity that appears in no public neocloud model — AND the same sources say it is routinely breached without enforcement, which means the reported backlogs have been economically real. 🔴 THE INVESTABLE CHANGE IS THE DIRECTION OF TRAVEL: operators are now trading PRICE FOR DURABILITY — "the more they can negotiate LESS PUNITIVE SLAs, THE BETTER, EVEN IF IT MEANS A LOWER PRICE." Expect headline lease rates to soften and contract QUALITY to improve at the same time. DO NOT READ A LOWER LEASE RATE AS A DEMAND SIGNAL WITHOUT ASKING WHAT HAPPENED TO THE SLA.**
- 🔴 **THE POWER LINE, WHICH IS THIS PAGE'S CORE: "POWER AGREEMENTS CAN AMOUNT TO MORE (AND SOMETIMES MUCH MORE) THAN A FIFTH OF DATA CENTERS' OPERATING COSTS. Here, the BIGGER AND RICHER CUSTOMERS ARE, THE LESS PRICE-SENSITIVE THEY ARE." A credit executive "has seen FLUCTUATIONS OF AS MUCH AS 400% FOR POWER RATES FOR DIFFERENT CUSTOMERS IN THE SAME MONTH. Everyone thinks they're getting a great deal… but THE BIGGEST CLOUDS ARE USUALLY PAYING THE MOST, BECAUSE THEY CAN."** ➤ **A 400% dispersion in contemporaneous power pricing is the single most important number here: it means there is NO market-clearing price for data-centre power, and any $/MWh assumption in a model is a negotiation outcome, not an observable.**
- 🔴 **TURBINES HAVE GONE PAST TIGHT INTO DYSFUNCTION: "Elon Musk is foreseeing such a POWER SHORTFALL FOR 2027 that he's DRAWING UP PLANS FOR HIS OWN TURBINE BLADE FACTORY. OTHER DATA CENTER DEVELOPERS ARE PURCHASING NATURAL GAS TURBINES FROM WHAT ONE INDUSTRY EXECUTIVE CALLED 'FLY-BY-NIGHT' COMPANIES AT MASSIVE MARKUPS, according to three people… That can PUSH UP INSURANCE AND DEBT EXPENSES too, since it's HARDER TO UNDERWRITE EQUIPMENT WHEN IT'S NOT CLEAR WHERE EXACTLY IT CAME FROM."** ➤ *Near-term price-positive for [[GEV]] and the incumbent OEMs; medium-term it is the classic setup for share loss, because customer self-supply and grey-market tolerance are what displacement looks like at the start. Cross-ref [[SPCX]], where Musk's own blades-and-vanes foundry and the 18-month-vs-45-60-month build dispute are already logged.*
- **AND THE COST BASE: "THE COST OF BUILDING OUT A ONE-GIGAWATT DATA CENTER HAS MORE THAN DOUBLED IN THE LAST FEW YEARS. If costs continue to rise, that could weigh on investor sentiment toward many of the world's biggest public companies."**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-07 — AND THE ARCHITECTURAL RESPONSE: NVIDIA, EQUINIX AND TOGETHER AI PUT INFERENCE IN EXISTING COLO, WITH JENSEN SAYING COMPUTE IS FRAGMENTING.**
- **At Equinix's customer conference, "[[NVDA]] executives said POWER ACCESS IS POSSIBLY THE MOST VEXING PART OF THE SUPPLY CHAIN NOW", and the three companies launched an initiative under which "TOGETHER AI WILL BUY NVIDIA HARDWARE AND INSTALL IT IN EXISTING EQUINIX DATA CENTERS, optimizing and serving OPEN-SOURCE AI MODELS on those servers for businesses OTHER THAN the major clouds or biggest AI model makers." Neither money nor chip count was disclosed.**
- 🔴 **Jensen: "COMPUTE IS FRAGMENTING… The world of AI will be FUNDAMENTALLY UNCENTRALIZED AND HIGHLY DISTRIBUTED", with time-sensitive parts of a task running at the Equinix site nearest the user "while MEMORY AND REASONING CAN HAPPEN AN OCEAN AWAY." Executives from [[GOOG]], [[CSCO]] and Lambda echoed it: gigawatt-scale sites for TRAINING, "thousands of smaller facilities" as the norm for INFERENCE.**
- ➤ **This is the first named commercial vehicle for the distributed-inference argument, and it is a partial answer to this page's central constraint: smaller inference sites can use power that a gigawatt campus cannot reach. It is also, read commercially, NVDA broadening its customer base away from the labs and the majors. Pair it with [[HPE]]'s post-quarter $3.5bn TRADITIONAL-server deal with a hyperscaler FOR ON-PREM INFERENCE (JPM, 09-07) — two independent datapoints in one week that inference is DISPERSING across sites and form factors rather than concentrating.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-05 — **THE DATA-CENTRE BACKLASH STOPS BEING ANECDOTE AND BECOMES A NUMBER: A ~70% RECALL VOTE, A >70% NATIONAL POLL, CALIFORNIA BILLS ON THE GOVERNOR'S DESK — AND MORGAN STANLEY'S POLICY DESK ARGUING IT STILL DOESN'T BREAK THE BUILD.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE HARDEST DATAPOINT IS A COMPLETED VOTE, NOT A POLL: *"Almost 70% of voters in a Missouri city voted to RECALL a council member who said yes to AI data center tax breaks"* — the councillor's own defence being that disagreement over one issue *"shouldn't be enough to unseat him."*** (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini weekly, 2026-09-05, relaying tomshardware.) ➤ **This theme has carried local opposition as sentiment for weeks. This is the first instance on the page of that sentiment REMOVING AN OFFICEHOLDER — which is the mechanism by which a permitting risk becomes a political-career risk, and therefore the mechanism by which it starts pricing into approval timelines.**
- 🔴 **THE NATIONAL NUMBER, WITH ITS SOURCING CHAIN STATED: *"more than 70 percent of the public opposes data centers near them, according to The Wall Street Journal, forcing governors and candidates who had previously embraced AI to backtrack."*** (Xinhua / @XHNews, corpus, 2026-09-05, relaying the WSJ.) ⚠️ **RELAYED THROUGH CHINESE STATE MEDIA — the underlying WSJ poll has NOT been read by this wiki, and the question wording ("near them" is a NIMBY framing, not an approval question) does a great deal of work. NOT ADOPTED as a measured national approval rate; logged because it is directionally consistent with the completed Missouri vote above.**
- 🔴 **CALIFORNIA IS NOW A LIVE LEGISLATIVE RISK: *"Data centers are deeply unpopular, and Gavin Newsom is considering signing SEVERAL BILLS that would regulate them."*** (Bloomberg Opinion / Erika D. Smith, via @business, corpus, 2026-09-05.) ⚠️ **Opinion column; the bills are not enumerated here and this wiki has not read them. Logged as a named, dated, state-level regulatory watch item — the largest state, and the first on this page with legislation actually awaiting signature.**
- ✅ **THE COUNTER-CASE, FROM A POLICY DESK RATHER THAN A HEADLINE, AND IT IS THE MORE CAREFULLY ARGUED SIDE.** Morgan Stanley's Head of US Public Policy Strategy **Ariana Salvatore** (via MS *Global Reflections* and *Friday Five* sales commentary, 2026-09-04 — SALES COMMENTARY, NOT RESEARCH):
  - **Federal policy still looks SUPPORTIVE** — streamlined permits, more federal sites, more energy infrastructure. ➤ **Therefore the risk sits around the 2028 PRESIDENTIAL election, NOT the midterms.**
  - **Races for LOCALITIES (county boards, governors) are likely MORE MATERIAL than Senate races** — which is exactly the level the Missouri recall operated at.
  - **The likely near-term outcome is a *"CONDITIONAL BUILDOUT"*: supportive regions and restrictive regions, rather than a national brake.** Near-term restrictions *"should remain concentrated at state and local levels while hyperscaler capex can exceed $1T next year."*
  - **Accommodations are already forming: hyperscalers signing the RATEPAYER PROTECTION PLEDGE** (covering the cost impact that falls on ordinary electricity customers), more environmental disclosure, and local-community programmes. ➤ **Note what the Pledge concedes: it is an admission that the ratepayer cost transfer is real enough to need pre-emptive indemnifying. Bullish for permitting, bearish for the assumption that power costs stay with the utility.**
  - **Salvatore also flags the September 24th US-China Summit** (*"managed stability as the core incentive"*, while warning technology restrictions could broaden) **and government funding + the FY27 NDAA as December catalysts.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE OTHER DIRECTION, FROM THE TOP: Trump, relayed — *"if you want to get rich as a state or as a town… you're going to want a data center. If you want to go through poverty, crime, and squalor, I would say don't approve data centers."*** (@The_AI_Investor, corpus, 2026-09-04, relaying remarks.) ➤ **Logged because it makes the wedge explicit: federal advocacy is pushing one way while local votes push the other, which is precisely Salvatore's "conditional buildout" — and it is the reason a national poll number cannot be mapped onto a national permitting outcome.**
- 🔭 **TWO 2050 CAPEX FORECASTS ARRIVED THE SAME DAY AND THEY DISAGREE BY 56%. NEITHER IS ADOPTED.** *"AI data center investment projected to hit $32 TRILLION by 2050 — infrastructure spending estimated to exceed capital requirements for railways, electrification, or the internet"* (tomshardware, via Susquehanna weekly, 2026-09-05) versus *"AI Data Center CapEx To Hit Up To $50 TRILLION By 2050: PwC"* (@zerohedge, corpus, 2026-09-04). ⚠️⚠️ **$32T and $50T for the same quantity over the same horizon, published within 24 hours, with no methodology shown in either relay. Logged ONLY as evidence of how wide the long-horizon framing band is — the honest read is that nobody has a 2050 number, and any note citing one should be checked for which of these it picked up. Same treatment as The Information's "42GW unused by 2030" (09-04): framing, kept out of every model.**


## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-03/04 — **THE TURBINE BOTTLENECK GETS ITS DEMAND-SIDE FRAMING ([[GEV]] SOLD OUT THROUGH 2030), [[NVDA]] KEEPS BUYING UP THE POWER STACK (LANCIUM: 15GW OF POWERED LAND AWAITING CONNECTION RIGHTS), AND A NUMBER APPEARS FOR THE STRANDED-SILICON CASE: 42GW OF AI CHIPS UNUSED BY 2030.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE THREE MARKS, FROM ONE CROSS-STORY SYNTHESIS: ① *"**A SMALL GROUP OF FOUNDRIES CONTROLS PRODUCTION OF CRITICAL TURBINE BLADES, while [[GEV]] IS SOLD OUT THROUGH 2030.**"* ② *"[[NVDA]] is looking beyond chips. Its recent infrastructure investments include companies **securing POWERED LAND AND ELECTRICITY CONNECTIONS**"* — specifically, **NVIDIA agreed to invest billions in LANCIUM, which holds 15 GIGAWATTS OF POWERED LAND AWAITING CONNECTION RIGHTS.** ③ 🔴 **the stranded-silicon number: a cited forecast that *"**42 GIGAWATTS WORTH OF AI SERVER CHIPS COULD SIT UNUSED BY 2030 IF INFRASTRUCTURE DOESN'T CATCH UP.**"*** (The Information, 2026-09-03.) ⚠️⚠️ **All three are journalism, not broker research — no rating, no methodology shown for the 42GW, and the piece is a marketing email for the outlet's own research product. **The 42GW is logged as a FRAMING number and is NOT adopted into any capacity model on this wiki.** The GE Vernova and Lancium items are specific and checkable and are consistent with what this page already carries — including [[SPCX]] deciding to build its own blades-and-vanes foundry rather than queue.**
- 🔴 **AND THE FINANCING SIDE OF THE SAME BOTTLENECK GOT A VALUATION: *"[[NVDA]]'s commitments underpin an expected **US$50bn valuation for SoftBank's SB ENERGY IPO**. The builder **CURRENTLY LACKS DATA-CENTER REVENUE and relies on FUTURE LEASE COMMITMENTS linked to NVIDIA-backed [[OPENAI]] infrastructure**, making contracted power demand and project execution the key valuation drivers. SB Energy is planning to develop an **UP-TO-10GW SITE IN PIKE COUNTY, OHIO, with the full capacity slated to be LEASED TO OPENAI**. In August it was reported that **NVIDIA would invest $1.5bn in SB Energy, DOWN FROM $3bn**, and support it in securing land, power and shell (LPS) capacity at the DOE site. SB Energy and SoftBank also plan to invest **at least $4.2bn in new regional grid infrastructure through a partnership with AEP OHIO**, which the companies claim is designed to protect ratepayers. SB Energy has also been signed on to build and operate the previously announced **1.2GW Stargate data center in Milam County, Texas.**"*** (Nikkei via Jefferies Asia TMT · Conor O'Mara, 2026-09-04.) ➤ 🔴 **A $50bn valuation on an entity with NO DATA-CENTRE REVENUE, underwritten by future leases to a private lab, with the strategic investor's cheque HALVED from $3bn to $1.5bn. That combination is the cleanest single expression of how this build is being financed — and the halved cheque is the part to watch.** ✅ **The AEP Ohio $4.2bn grid commitment is a direct, dated counter to the ratepayer-backlash mechanism this page has been tracking (EXC's ~40% pipeline cut, AEP Ohio's >50% decline).**
- 🔴 **THE POWER-CONVERSION AND COOLING LAYER GOT TWO CAPACITY COMMITMENTS IN 48 HOURS.** ① **[[FLEX]] to acquire **EPC Power for $4.4bn** — data-centre and grid power conversion — folded into the **Cloud & Power Infrastructure segment that is *"planned to separate into an independent public company in C1Q27."*** ⚠️ **No revenue, EBITDA, margin or funding disclosed anywhere in this window — the price and the destination are the only hard facts.** ② **LG Electronics: cooling as *"a STRUCTURAL UPCYCLE"* as rack density forces direct-to-chip liquid; **600kW CDU already NVIDIA-validated, 1MW / 2.5MW / 4MW variants planned**; *"commercialization now key debate because hyperscale qualification [is] needed."*** (both via MS desk · Tom Wigg, 2026-09-04.) ➤ **Read alongside [[SPCX]]'s turbine foundry and Furukawa's cold-plate ramp (Jefferies BUY, TP JPY 10,500, DC products to 68% of group OP by FY30 from 30%): four separate entities building capacity into the physical layer in one week. **That is what a real bottleneck looks like from the supply side, and it is the strongest evidence this page has that the constraint is being taken seriously rather than talked about.**
- 🔴 **CHINA'S VERSION OF THE SAME CONSTRAINT, SIZED — AND IT COMES WITH A FIFTH $/GW BASIS THAT MUST NOT BE CHAINED TO ANY OTHER: *"**ByteDance plans to add 5-6GW of data centre capacity in Ulanqab, Inner Mongolia, BY 2028**… We believe this represents **INCREMENTAL demand rather than previously announced projects**, as ByteDance has already tendered roughly 2GW this year… **Using our estimate of RMB220bn OF CAPEX PER GW, a 5.5GW buildout would require roughly RMB1.15tn (US$190bn)**… If ByteDance alone deploys this level of capacity, **our current 2028 demand forecast of 7GW appears increasingly conservative.**"*** (Jefferies · Edison, via Asia TMT morning note, 2026-09-03; echoed in Jefferies Silk Road, 09-04.) ⚠️⚠️ **RMB220bn/GW ≈ US$31bn/GW is a FULL BUILD COST — the same class as the ~$30-60bn/GW figures on this page, and NOT comparable to [[AVGO]]'s ~$13bn/GW of silicon content, Hock's $20-30bn/GW of silicon-plus-systems, or any $/MW REVENUE mark ([[ORCL]] ~$10M/MW · [[NBIS]] $20-50M/MW · 22V ~$16M/MW/yr).** **Beneficiary named: VNET, with >1GW of secured power in the region.** **ByteDance separately borrowed ~$30bn — its largest-ever loan, upsized from $20bn, the second-largest dollar loan in Asia this year after SoftBank's $40bn bridge** (Bloomberg via The Information, 2026-09-04).

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-03 (sources 2026-08-27 / 09-02) — **THE COST OF CAPITAL FOR BEHIND-THE-METER POWER FINALLY GETS A NUMBER: [[WMB]]'s PE JV WITH BLACKSTONE COVERS 50% OF 2.7GW AND **CAPS BLACKSTONE'S IRR AT 6.35%**. PLUS [[ANTHROPIC]] AWARDS 470MW OF GENERATORS, AND [[NVDA]] IR SAYS US PERMITTING FRICTION HAS "LARGELY" NOT SHOWN UP — BECAUSE DELAYED INFRASTRUCTURE GETS *REALLOCATED*.**

**Three independent sources, filed together because they answer the same question from three sides — who funds behind-the-meter power, who is contracting it, and whether the friction is binding:**
- **(A) Unattributed BTM power call, 2026-08-27** — 30-minute post-2Q roundtable on small/mid-cap OFS + midstream power names. ⚠️⚠️ **NO EMAIL METADATA AND NO BROKER NAME ANYWHERE IN THE FILE. It arrived via the P: drop folder. The speakers give first names only (Surab, Nico, Jimmy, Jean-Anne) and this wiki's standing rule forbids inferring a house from analysts' first names — so it is cited throughout as *(unattributed BTM power call, 2026-08-27)* and nothing here should be attributed to any bank.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_27_btm.html)
- **(B) [[NVDA]] Investor Relations ("Stewart") via a BTG Pactual-hosted call, 2026-09-02** — company commentary relayed through a broker-hosted format, i.e. treat as MANAGEMENT, not as a broker view. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_09_02_btg_call_com_ir_nvidia.html)
- **(C) Jefferies "AI Deep Dive" · Brent Thill, 58p, priced intraday 9-2-26.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AI_Deep_Dive_Jeffries.html)

### 🔴🔴🔴 (A1) THE MOST REUSABLE NUMBER IN THE CALL — **BLACKSTONE'S IRR IS CAPPED AT 6.35%**

- **[[WMB]] *"set up a very attractive JV with private equity led by **Blackstone** for **50% of the BTM projects that they've announced so far, so 50% of the 2.7 gigawatts**. Notably, the **maximum IRR that Blackstone can get on this is 6.35%.**"*** *(unattributed BTM power call, 2026-08-27. Blackstone — plain text, no wiki page.)*
- ➤ 🔴🔴 **THIS IS THE FIRST OBSERVABLE COST OF THIRD-PARTY EQUITY CAPITAL FOR BEHIND-THE-METER DATA-CENTER POWER ON THIS WIKI, AND IT IS EXTRAORDINARILY LOW. A capped 6.35% maximum IRR is infrastructure-debt pricing, not private-equity pricing. The speaker's own read is the right one: *"I think it really shows the interest in the space from private equity."* Private capital is competing the return on contracted BTM power down toward a utility cost of capital — which, if it holds, is a structural tailwind for every developer on this page and a warning for anyone underwriting BTM projects at PE-style hurdle rates.**
- ⚠️ **Read the structure precisely: it is a **cap on the maximum** Blackstone can earn, on **half** of the announced 2.7GW, on projects that are already contracted (i.e. de-risked). It is not a blended market clearing rate for merchant or uncontracted BTM.**
- **CROSS-FILE: this belongs directly against the 300-600bp counterparty spread on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md) (08-20, ~8% for Amazon offtake vs 11.5-14.2% for CoreWeave offtake). A 6.35% capped IRR on Williams' contracted BTM sits BELOW even the Amazon-offtake data-center paper — consistent with midstream counterparty quality plus a take-or-pay structure, but it is a different instrument and should not be put on the same axis without saying so.**

### 🔴🔴 (A2) [[ANTHROPIC]] CONTRACTS 470MW OF GENERATION — AND THE SCOPE AWARDED IS ONLY *PART* OF THE JOB

- **EROC (plain text — no wiki page): *"The **470 megawatt Anthropic award** is EROC's **third major data center customer**, which lifted the **backlog to roughly $1.7 billion dollars** and extended **committed production into early 2028**."*** *(unattributed BTM power call, 2026-08-27.)*
- 🔴 **THE SCOPE POINT IS THE ONE TO CARRY: *"the contract currently includes **only [the] generators**, so **installation and O&M remain potential upside as separate scopes are awarded.**"* ➤ **A 470MW "award" here is an equipment order, not a turnkey power contract. This wiki should stop treating megawatt awards as fungible — the same MW number means very different revenue depending on whether install and long-term O&M are inside it.**
- **CAPACITY AND FUNNEL: the **Hyperion facility is scaling to 1.2GW of annual production**. And separately — *"we identified a **Texas Air permit covering 3,428 engines and up to approximately 1.5 gigawatts** at a separate site. EROC confirmed it filed a permit, but emphasized that the opportunity is **not yet contracted and not in their current plan.**"*** ⚠️⚠️ **THE PERMIT IS EVIDENCE OF FUNNEL SIZE, NOT OF DEMAND. The speakers are explicit that it is outside estimates. Given this page's own 09-01 finding that >100 PA proposals produced only 20 permit applications, a filed permit is one of the WEAKEST signals in the chain — log it as optionality and nothing more.**
- **VALUATION MARK FROM THE SAME SOURCE (positioning colour, not a house view this wiki can attribute): EROC at **7.5x 2028 consensus EBITDA**, with the speaker arguing **12x** is appropriate on peer comparison, *"which implies roughly a 60% upside."***

### 🔴 (A3) KODIAK — THE ONLY NAME ON THE CALL WHERE **POWER CAPEX WENT DOWN**

- **Kodiak (plain text — no wiki page): a **limited notice to proceed on a West Texas data center**, *"given management comments, it was likely a little under 100 megawatts"* — and now identified as part of **NScale's Ward County project, 240MW, *"which could scale to 1.2 gigawatts."*** ➤ **Phase optionality on the same site rather than a single 100MW order.**
- 🔴 **THE COUNTERPARTY INFERENCE, AND IT IS A GOOD ONE: *"**NScale recently signed a deal with [[ANTHROPIC]] at its West Virginia data center**… and they previously had a **tentative deal at that data center with [[MSFT]]**. So I think it's reasonable to think that the credit, the hyperscaler that they have signed at the Ward County one is probably just as high credit."*** ⚠️ **That is an inference about an undisclosed counterparty, explicitly framed as one. NScale — plain text, no wiki page.**
- ✅🔴 **THE CAPEX SURPRISE: *"Kodiak's **power capex actually came down**… their **down payments came in actually lower than they had budgeted**, so their **capex actually came down by 25 million at the midpoint.**"* The mechanism named is **OEM relationships inherited from the gas-compression base business**, *"which really runs on these kind of engines."*** ➤ **In a page full of cost inflation, this is the one datapoint where an incumbent's legacy supplier relationships produced deflation. It is also the cleanest argument on this call for why OFS players win BTM scope.**
- **FUNDING AND PIPELINE: *"**no funding gaps at this point**… should be able to execute their **whole two gigawatt pipeline just with cash flow** and with the liquidity"* from an equity raise earlier in the year. Since buying the distributed-power business **in April** they have **added 1.4GW of supply contracts** and signed a **new 100MW deal**, and *"their **pipeline in July alone had grown by two gigawatts.**"* ⚠️ **Pipeline ≠ contracts; the 1.4GW is contracted supply, the 2GW is pipeline growth in one month. Different evidentiary standards, both quoted in GW.**

### 🔴🔴 (A4) CHEVRON / [[GEV]] — THE 2.6GW MICROSOFT PPA, AND THE **2031** FULL-CAPACITY DATE NOBODY HAD

- **Standing structure: Chevron (plain text — no wiki page) has a **JV with [[GEV]] covering 4GW of turbines**, announced *"about a year and a half ago"*, and a **20-year take-or-pay PPA with [[MSFT]] in West Texas for 2.6GW**, expandable.**
- 🔴 **NEW FROM THE 2Q SLIDE: *"they're using these **smaller Cat[erpillar] turbines to start ramping** and then they **don't really reach full capacity with the GEV turbines until 2031.** So that was just a little bit of a **later timeline than I had realized.**"*** ➤ 🔴 **A 2031 full-capacity date on the flagship BTM gas project is materially later than the "speed to power" framing this whole complex is sold on, and the speaker covering it says so in their own words. It corroborates the 09-01 DAMNANG turbine-throughput arithmetic on this page (GEV ~20GW/yr 2026 → 30GW/yr 2030, ~3 years order-to-delivery, 2030 slots largely booked) from the customer end rather than the factory end.**
- 🔴 **AND THE GRID CONNECTION, WHICH IS THE STRATEGICALLY INTERESTING PART: *"they're planning to **connect to the grid in 2030**, so even before they get to the full amount of the behind the meter power… **I was surprised that they were connecting to the grid if they also had this 20-year PPA.** And when we talked to the company, they kind of framed them as **separate decisions**… separately as like a **reliability and optionality decision**… They also think that will help to make **Kilby modular** and allow them to gradually increase the size should they choose to… they kind of have the **path to the full four gigawatts.**"*** ➤ **Read it as an option, not a hedge: grid interconnection converts a fixed 2.6GW BTM project into a scalable one. It also means "behind the meter" and "grid connected" are not mutually exclusive at the project level, which this page has sometimes implicitly assumed.**
- **TEXAS POLITICS: *"**Greg Abbott is conducting an audit of data centers in Texas** and **this project has announced that it complies with Texas standards**, so they're good on that."*** ➤ **Cross-reference the Jefferies Abbott quote in section (C) below and the Reuters "ghost demand" marker in the 09-01 block — the same governor, the same month, and a named project claiming compliance is the first evidence on this page of a developer clearing that bar rather than being caught by it.**

### (A5) [[WMB]] — FIRST PROJECT ON, AND A ~$2bn "MORE DEALS" NUMBER THAT SHOULD BE READ AS TWO SEPARATE CLAIMS

- **First project **"Project Socrates" in Ohio** successfully brought on; **phase two tracking on time for end of this year.** *"We view this phase one being on time as a major milestone… the successful startup should help them get future projects."***
- ⚠️ **TWO CLAIMS, TWO DIFFERENT AUTHORS — DO NOT MERGE: management *"suggested on the call that there could be **around 2 billion dollars more of deals coming by the end of the year**"* (COMPANY framing). *"When we **back calculate** that, that's probably like **one more site, like something like 600 megawatts**"* (the SPEAKER'S arithmetic, not a company figure). The $2bn is sourced; the 600MW is derived.**

### 🔴 (A6) THE STRUCTURAL ARGUMENT — WHY THIRD-PARTY SHARE OF BTM SHOULD **RISE**

**The common client question on the call was *"what are these third parties bringing to the table? Why would a data center partner with Williams or Chevron?"* — and all four speakers land on RISE:**
- **MIDSTREAM brings gas supply and gas marketing: *"for Chevron and Williams, they're clearly getting **gas supply and gas marketing expertise**… that was a key reason why **Williams got this foothold in Ohio**, where they're kind of the only ones that have G&P and gas marketing."* Chevron additionally uses *"about a **third of their own produced Permian gas** to feed this."***
- **OFS brings the operating skill: *"the entire industry basically operates these engines in different shapes and forms, from a land rig to a pressure pumper… **You are used to operating them, maintaining them, running them in remote environments where a lot of these data centers are now coming up away from population centers**… this is a very capital intensive endeavor to own and operate these turbines. We're talking **billions of dollars**… a data center developer, if that's not its core skill, doesn't really need to be in this business."***
- 🔴 **AND THE DEMAND-SIDE PROOF, WHICH IS THE BEST PART: *"some of the calls that they're getting **inbound are actually from data centers that have gotten their hands on these OEM turbines but need help setting them up**… they sign up for these slots, but now they're realizing, hey, maybe we don't really know how to run it quite as effectively as we want to."*** ➤ **Developers who self-procured turbines are calling OFS operators for help running them. That is share migrating to third parties from the "self-build" bucket, observed rather than forecast — the speaker's point being that even projects inside the ~60% self-build slice of the pie may end up third-party-operated.**
- **POSITIONING CONTEXT FROM THE SAME CALL (short-interest colour, credited on the call to a short-analytics colleague): **EROC 43%** short interest (highest in the group), **Atlas ~20% even excluding converts** — *"a crowded short"* — **Kodiak 8-9%**, and **Liberty / ProPetro** *"pretty much no fundamental short interest"* once adjusted for convertible-arb positions. ⚠️ **EROC, Atlas, Kodiak, Liberty and ProPetro all lack wiki pages — plain text. ASR note: "Propetro pump" is ProPetro (ticker PUMP).**

### 🔴🔴 (B) [[NVDA]] IR: THE PERMITTING FRICTION IS REAL AT PROJECT LEVEL AND INVISIBLE IN AGGREGATE — BECAUSE THE INFRASTRUCTURE GETS **REALLOCATED**

*(NVDA Investor Relations via BTG Pactual call, 2026-09-02. Company commentary.)*

- **Asked directly whether permitting, interconnection and power approvals across US states are slowing the pace of capacity getting energised, and whether NVDA is still confident capacity keeps up with shipments — the answer is **"largely no"** to the first and **"largely yes"** to the second.**
- **THE MECHANISM: *"in any given quarter, can there be a project that gets delayed or pushed out? Sure. But you really **haven't seen it show up in our numbers in the aggregate**. And the reason for that is just the **breadth and diversity of our demand** — and that's not only a diversity of **customer** statement, but also a diversity of **geographic region** statement… **if a project is delayed, instead of the infrastructure going to that project, it goes to the other customers that are ready to take the infrastructure. And we just reallocate.**"***
- ➤ 🔴🔴 **THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT QUALIFICATION THIS PAGE HAS RECEIVED ON ITS OWN CENTRAL THESIS. The 09-01 EXC/AEP/PA pipeline-attrition block measures friction at the PROJECT and UTILITY level, and it is real. NVDA IR is saying that friction does not propagate to the VENDOR's revenue line, because the constraint binds allocation rather than demand. **Both can be true at once, and they have different investment consequences: the power names on this page are exposed to WHERE capacity gets built; NVDA claims it is exposed only to WHETHER, in aggregate, someone builds it.** That is a falsifiable claim — it breaks the first quarter NVDA misses on a stated deployment reason.**
- 🔴 **AND IR TURNS IT INTO A COMPETITIVE POINT: *"sovereign clouds, AI clouds outside the US, are **largely 100% Nvidia**… the concern you just laid out I think would be a **much bigger problem for other would-be competitors to Nvidia that are largely beholden to hyperscale builds inside the US.**"*** ⚠️ **That is the vendor arguing its own book, and the "largely 100%" is unquantified. But the structural asymmetry is checkable: US permitting risk is concentrated in exactly the US-hyperscaler channel that the ASIC programmes sell into. Cross-file [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md).**
- ✅ **NEOCLOUD CAPACITY, FROM THE VENDOR'S OWN MOUTH — CORROBORATING THE BARCLAYS RELAY ALREADY ON THIS PAGE (09-01): *"at the end of **calendar 25**, in aggregate, they represented around **three gigawatts of installed capacity.** This year, we expect them to **exit with around eight gigawatts.**"*** ➤ **~3GW → ~8GW is now confirmed directly from [[NVDA]] IR rather than via a relay. **INSTALLED CAPACITY, gigawatts — not revenue, not capex, not contracted MW.** Cross-file [[CRWV]], [[NBIS]].**
- **WHY [[NVDA]] IS ITSELF SECURING LAND/POWER/SHELL — and the answer is customer-mix, not bottleneck-severity: *"it reflects a **certain customer type that has a lot of demand, but they don't have the scale to secure land power shell on their own.** And so we're essentially stepping in to either help them with land power shell or help them generally just secure financing… the ultimate goal is to help them **bridge the gap to the point where they can start self-funding.**"*** ➤ **IR explicitly declines the framing that LPS involvement signals a worsening bottleneck (*"I guess partly, but I would look at it more from the standpoint of…"*). Read alongside the vendor-financing thread on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md): the same intervention is being described here as a market-development function rather than a credit backstop.**

### 🔴 (C) JEFFERIES RANKS POWER AS THE **#1** AI-INFRASTRUCTURE BOTTLENECK — AND PUTS THE LEAD-TIME MISMATCH IN ONE SENTENCE

*(Jefferies "AI Deep Dive" · Brent Thill, priced intraday 9-2-26.)*

- **The bottleneck ranking (p16): infrastructure buildout — **1) Power, 2) People, 3) Permits**; enterprise adoption — 1) Security, 2) Unclear ROI/AI cost, 3) Data/context.** ➤ **People as the **#2** constraint is a leg this page carries almost nothing on. Flag as a research gap.**
- 🔴🔴 **p17, the core mismatch, quoting the **World Economic Forum, May 2026**: *"investment in AI data centers is **accelerating faster than power grids were designed to accommodate.** While compute capacity, capital and talent remain critical, **in many regions, connecting a new facility to the power grid can take 4-10 years, while AI data centers are typically planned and built within two to three.**"*** ➤ **A 4-10 year interconnect against a 2-3 year build is the cleanest single statement of why behind-the-meter exists at all — and it is the same variable the 09-01 @FundaAI item identified from the equipment side (*"the real AI power shortage is not equipment, it's deployment lead time"*). Two independent sources, one institutional and one independent, now converge on lead time as the binding constraint.**
- **p19, permits — **Texas Governor Greg Abbott on data-center developers, 8/24/2026**: *"They have not been working in collaboration with the state or local governments, and so they **basically dug their own grave** for the problem that's been caused for them and **that's why they got the backlash they deserve.**"* The slide carries a map of **states where data-center moratoriums have been proposed.*** ⚠️ **Note the date sequencing on this page: Abbott's quote is 8/24, and the Chevron/[[MSFT]] West Texas project announced Texas-standards compliance at 2Q (section A4). The politics and the compliance response are running in the same month.**

### ⚠️⚠️ (D) **UNIT GUARD FOR THIS PAGE — A NEW $/MW BASIS ARRIVES AND IT IS *REVENUE*, NOT COST**

- **Jefferies p39 quotes **Arkady Volozh, [[NBIS]] CEO, 2Q26 earnings call**: *"We are **negotiating deals for $40 million to $50 million per megawatt** range and sometimes above under this model."***
- 🔴🔴 **THAT IS CONTRACT REVENUE PER MEGAWATT — NOT CAPEX, NOT BUILD COST. It may **never** be netted against, or chained into a series with, any $/GW build-cost anchor (~$30-60bn/GW), any vendor content-per-GW figure ([[AVGO]] $11-15bn, [[NVDA]] $25-40bn — see [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md)), or the customer-side $30bn ARR/GW and ~$5bn/GW/yr cost figures. This page now carries at least FIVE distinct per-unit-of-power bases and the failure mode is always the same: someone subtracts two of them.**
- ⚠️ **AND IT IS NOT DIRECTLY COMPARABLE TO THE $16M/MW/YEAR FLOOR IN THE 09-01 BLOCK EITHER, WHICH IS THE TRAP MOST LIKELY TO CATCH SOMEONE HERE. The 22V figure is explicitly **per MW per YEAR**; Volozh's $40-50m/MW carries **no period** in the quote as printed. Until the term is established, do not annualise it, do not divide it, and do not read it as a ~3x increase over the 22V floor. Logged as an open question.**
- **SAME SLIDE, THE MARGIN SIDE — **Michael Intrator, [[CRWV]] CEO, 2Q26 earnings call**: *"In Q2, the customer contracts we signed came with **contribution margins we expect to be 5 to 10 percentage points above those added in recent quarters.**"*** ➤ **Consistent with the pricing floor resetting upward (09-01, 22V) and with the CRWV CFO's *"we're also passing through component price increases"* on Jefferies p28 — the neoclouds are repricing new cohorts, not absorbing memory inflation. Cross-file [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md).**
## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-01 — THE POLITICAL CONSTRAINT STOPS BEING A NARRATIVE AND BECOMES A MEASURABLE PIPELINE LOSS: **EXC CUT "HIGH-PROBABILITY" DC DEMAND ~40% TO 11GW, AEP OHIO'S PIPELINE FELL >50%, AND IN PENNSYLVANIA 100+ PROJECTS ARE PROPOSED AGAINST 20 PERMIT APPLICATIONS.** PLUS THE FIRST CREDIBLE ARGUMENT THAT THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS *LEAD TIME*, NOT EQUIPMENT.

**(MS Public Policy Strategist · Ariana Salvatore, 2026-09-01, via MS desk · Tom Wigg; corroborating items from NYT, Reuters, Politico, Yahoo; plus @FundaAI, corpus, 2026-08-31)**

- 🔴🔴 **THE HARD NUMBERS, WHICH THIS PAGE HAS NOT HAD BEFORE — pipeline attrition rather than sentiment:**
  - *"**EXC reduced 'high-probability' Datacenter demand ~40% to 11 GW**, while **AEP Ohio pipeline fell >50%** after Ohio introduced rules including **connection-study fees of up to $100K.**"*
  - *"In PA, **>100 data centers have been proposed, but ONLY 20 HAVE APPLIED for necessary permits.** Most of those proposals apparently **LACK SECURED POWER OR END-CUSTOMER.**"*
  - ⚠️ **AND THE CRUCIAL QUALIFIER, FROM THE GRID OPERATOR ITSELF: *"however, even after rooting out the more 'SPECULATIVE' projects, there is still a **VERY REAL STRAIN ON THE GRID, per PJM.**"*
  - ➤ 🔴 **READ THE ATTRITION PRECISELY, BECAUSE IT WILL BE MISQUOTED: what is being culled is SPECULATIVE pipeline that "lacks secured power or end-customer" — it is NOT evidence of funded hyperscaler projects being cancelled. A 100-proposals-to-20-permits ratio is a measure of how much of the announced pipeline was never real. That is simultaneously reassuring (the phantom demand is being removed from utility planning) and disqualifying for anyone who has been sizing the TAM off announcements.**
- **MS'S OVERALL FRAME, AND IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT ALARMIST: *"**Data center political pushback remains the KEY POLICY RISK to the AI infrastructure buildout**, but MS still views it primarily as a **STATE + LOCAL constraint rather than an imminent FEDERAL capex threat.** Federal policy remains supportive of data center construction, permitting, power generation and associated energy infrastructure… **MS analysts expect hyperscaler capex to EXCEED $1T next year.** The more likely consequence is a **'CONDITIONAL BUILDOUT' rather than broad capex destruction — data centers CONTINUE TO GET BUILT, but increasingly MIGRATE TOWARD JURISDICTIONS WILLING TO ACCOMMODATE THEM.**"* **On timing: *"the **2026 MIDTERMS are an important SIGNAL, not necessarily the inflection point** — MS sees the elections revealing which anti-Datacenter policies resonate with voters and potentially laying the groundwork for broader restrictions, but thinks **material federal policy risk is more likely AFTER THE 2028 ELECTIONS.**"*
- **THE DATED POLITICAL MARKERS:** **PA Gov Josh Shapiro *"has REVERSED COURSE on Datacenters, HALTING CONSTRUCTION, even as some struggling citizens have welcomed the construction boom after the depressive steel decline"*** (NYT); **New Mexico** *"court granted two emergency stay requests connected with **PROJECT JUPITER**, which means that the project's **AIR QUALITY PERMIT application proceedings can't progress.** Project Jupiter is the **[[ORCL]]/[[OPENAI]] collaboration** for an AI Datacenter at the **Santa Teresa Industrial Park**"* (Yahoo); *"**Texas' halt on powering data centers** reflects US reckoning over **'GHOST' DEMAND**"* (Reuters); and President Trump: *"**China could not be happier with this anti-Datacenter movement**"* (Politico).
- **THE MITIGATION SPEND IS NOW ITEMISED, AND IT IS A NEW COST LINE: *"Hyperscalers are already responding with **direct community/political mitigation spending: RATEPAYER PROTECTIONS and SELF-SUPPLIED GENERATION, [[GOOG]]'s WATER FRAMEWORK, [[AMZN]]'s new GLOBAL DC WATER-USE DISCLOSURE, and [[META]]'s $1B HOST-COMMUNITY FUND.**"* ➤ **"Self-supplied generation" is the line item [[BE]], [[GEV]], [[CEG]], [[TLN]] and [[SPCX]]'s own turbine programme compete for. Cross-filed to each.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE MOST USEFUL REFRAME OF THE WEEK, WHICH CHALLENGES THE ENTIRE TURBINE THREAD ON THIS WIKI: *"@elonmusk said it so well. **Finding a gas turbine ≠ getting power. You still need PERMITS, INTERCONNECTION and EMISSIONS COMPLIANCE — especially SCR. Each can become a 12+ MONTH BOTTLENECK. THE REAL AI POWER SHORTAGE IS NOT EQUIPMENT. IT'S DEPLOYMENT LEAD TIME.**"* (@FundaAI, corpus, 2026-08-31 — ⚠️ *INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model*).** ➤ 🔴🔴 **IF THIS IS RIGHT, IT RE-RANKS THIS PAGE'S WHOLE WATCH LIST. The [[SPCX]]/Howmet/[[GEV]] blades-and-vanes debate — three houses now bracketing Musk's 18-month acceleration claim at 4-5 years — would be an argument about a NON-BINDING constraint. The binding ones would be the permit queue, the interconnection queue and SCR/NOx compliance, which is exactly what the EXC/AEP/PA numbers above are measuring. **The two sources are independent and they point at the same variable from opposite ends: one from the utility pipeline, one from the equipment buyer.** ⚠️ **It also identifies the winners differently: behind-the-meter, low-NOx, non-interconnection-dependent generation (fuel cells) beats a faster turbine if the turbine still waits 12+ months for a permit.**
- 🔴🔴 **SEPARATELY — THE PRICE OF A CONTRACTED MEGAWATT JUST RESET UP 60%, WHICH IS THE OTHER HALF OF THIS PAGE'S ECONOMICS (22V Research · Dauvin Peterson, 2026-09-01):** three independent long-term compute contracts all priced at **~$16M/MW/year**: [[ANTHROPIC]]/Lambda (**6yr, $35bn, ~350MW** at Hut 8's Texas Beacon Point campus), Anthropic/Nscale (**6yr, $45bn, 460MW**), and Bitdeer (**5yr, $800m, 9MW**). *"**A HIGHER FLOOR IS BEING SET FOR LONG TERM DEALS. $16M/MW/year is 60% HIGHER than the $10M/MW/year long term deals in the past year. Anthropic is a high profile client and likely can command SOME OF THE BEST RATES in the market MAKING THIS A FLOOR.**"* And: *"Anthropic appears to be signaling **LONGER CONFIDENCE IN GPUs with SIX-YEAR deals.** The market has consistently debated the **LIFESPAN OF GPUs** and a term deal for 6 years is a move in the right direction."* ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT GUARD, MANDATORY AND RESTATED PER THIS WIKI'S STANDING RULE: $16M/MW/year is CONTRACT REVENUE to the compute provider, per MW, per YEAR. It is NOT capex, NOT build cost, and must NEVER be netted against a $/GW construction anchor or chained into a build-cost series. Nor may it be netted against [[NVDA]]'s revenue-per-GW ladder ($18bn Hopper → $25bn Blackwell → $40bn Vera Rubin), which is a THIRD distinct basis.**
- **THE DEMAND-SIDE DENOMINATOR, FROM [[NVDA]]'s OWN DISCLOSURE: neocloud partners are *"bringing online capacity FASTER and at a LOWER TOKEN COST, expected to **exit the year with ~8 GWs in total installed capacity, up from ~3 GWs at the end of 2025**"* (Barclays · Raimo Lenschow, 09-01). **And [[TSM]]'s own framing at SEMICON Taiwan: *"TSMC estimates **30 TO 40GW PER YEAR of AI data centers will be built per year, compared to 5 TO 6GW of data centers HISTORICALLY**"*** (@dnystedt, 08-31) — ⚠️ **GW BUILT PER YEAR, not revenue and not capex.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE INSTITUTIONAL-CAPITAL QUESTION NOBODY ON THIS PAGE HAS ANSWERED, put well by the FT: *"the market is still heavily focused on models, [[NVDA]] and token prices but **who is going to POWER, FINANCE, INSURE and ultimately MONETIZE the gigantic physical infrastructure being built behind AI?** In the US, the number of **data centers above 100MW could almost TRIPLE if all announced projects are completed. However… a large part of the pipeline is still only PLANNED and NOT ACTUALLY UNDER CONSTRUCTION. The bottleneck is increasingly ELECTRICITY, GRID[…]"* (via @C_Barraud, corpus, 2026-09-01). ➤ **"Almost triple IF all announced projects are completed" is the same phantom-pipeline problem the EXC and AEP cuts are pricing. INSURANCE is the leg this wiki carries nothing on — flag as a research gap.**

## 🆕🔭 Recent signals (Sep 1, /newsletters-to-vault — **"POWER AND GRID REMAIN THE DOMINANT CONSTRAINT THROUGH 2027" — A DATED CALL WITH A PUBLISHED FALSIFICATION TEST, AND THE POINT THAT THE SEVEREST BOTTLENECK IS *NOT* WHERE THE PROFIT IS**)

**DAMNANG (Substack)** — *"The AI Investment Map: From Tokens to Megawatts"*, **2026-09-01**. ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR (damnang2.substack.com), NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model.** Korean ex-Qualcomm engineer. **His own disclaimer, verbatim: *"This note is for information only and is not a recommendation to buy or sell any security… Third-party estimates vary by source, so they are better read for direction and structure than for absolute levels."*** He frames both calls as a **base case that changes if the conditions change**, and publishes an explicit falsification test for each. [Source](../../Damnang/2026-09-01%20-%20The%20AI%20Investment%20Map-%20From%20Tokens%20to%20Megawatts.md)

🔴🔴 **CALL #1, VERBATIM: *"My base case is that the dominant constraint on the **turn-on timing** of new AI capacity stays with **power and grid at least through 2027**. That does not mean the memory and advanced packaging shortages are over. **They continue to gate the shipment clock.** The difference is response time."* ➤ **THE TWO-CLOCK FRAMING IS THE GENUINELY USEFUL CONTRIBUTION AND THIS PAGE SHOULD ADOPT IT: *"**Power and grid decide WHEN new capacity can be turned on. Memory and packaging decide HOW FAST accelerators already ordered can be shipped.** Both constraints exist at the same time. They simply bind different things."***

⚠️ **HE EXPLICITLY REJECTS THE "BOTTLENECK MOVED FROM X TO Y" NARRATIVE THIS PAGE HAS SOMETIMES USED: *"The shorthand is that supply moved from accelerators to HBM and packaging and then to power. What actually happened is that **the constraints piled up**… **The earlier bottleneck remains in place while a slower constraint is added underneath**."* His onset dating: **accelerators + grid tightened 2023 · HBM + advanced packaging 2024 · power + cooling 2025 · light sources 2026**, and he is careful that *"**The onset of each of the six is documented. The clearing dates are not**: forecasts diverge too widely to fix one."*

### 🔴 THE SUPPLY-SIDE ARITHMETIC, WHICH IS WHY HE THINKS POWER STAYS TIGHT

*"[[GEV]]’s 2Q26 gas equipment **backlog and slot reservations stood at 116GW against annual output targets of roughly 20GW in 2026, 24GW in 2028 and 30GW in 2030**… new heavy-duty turbine orders run **about three years to delivery**, and **2030 production slots are largely booked**."* ➤ **His read is deliberately narrow: *"That number is **not a statement about long-run TAM**. It says **factory throughput is the near-term supply ceiling**. Strong demand does not turn 20GW of annual output into 50GW the following year."*** Transformers: **lead times ~140 weeks in 2023, past 160 weeks in 2026**. Market sizing he cites: **US data-center electrical equipment ~$20bn (2026) → $65bn (2030)** and US data-center capacity **~24GW → ~100GW** over the same period (Wood Mackenzie, via public reporting).

⚠️ **THE WORKAROUND HE FLAGS AGAINST HIMSELF — and it is the BTM argument this page has been building: *"Hyperscalers and developers are expanding **behind-the-meter generation** rather than waiting for a grid connection. But as that path grows, **the bottleneck does not disappear. It relocates.** Interconnection scarcity falls, and scarcity in **gas turbines, generators and fuel infrastructure** rises."* ➤ **So BTM is bearish the grid-queue thesis and bullish the equipment suppliers — the same asset, opposite signs, depending which leg you own.**

### ⚠️⚠️ THE PRINCIPLE THAT CUTS AGAINST THE LAZY VERSION OF THIS THEME: **SEVERITY ≠ PROFIT**

*"**Where the bottleneck sits does not determine who keeps the profit.** Qualified supplier set, switching time, substitution path, contract duration and repricing frequency do."* His worked counter-example is the one this page should carry: **data-center electrical construction** is arguably the most severe shortage in the stack — *"Labor and project capacity are short and lead times are long"* — **and it is the weakest economics**, because *"the contractor base is fragmented and large buyers can use competitive bidding… **The place where scarcity is most severe is not the place where the supplier profit pool is largest**."* **Colocation** fails on contract structure (long terms delay repricing). **Gas turbines and transformers/switchgear** score highest. ➤ **His four-part test — supply response time · qualified supplier set · substitution path · contract structure — is a scoring frame, not pass/fail, and is worth reusing whenever this page adds a new "X is the bottleneck" datapoint.**

✅ **[[VRT]] gets classified as an EXECUTION story rather than a demand one: *"Cooling and power management are past the stage of having to prove demand"* (2Q26 revenue **$3.274B, +24%**, adj. op margin **22.6%**) — *"The question in this layer is not whether the orders exist. It is **how fast backlog converts into revenue, and whether margin holds through that conversion**."*

⚠️ **WHAT BREAKS CALL #1 (published, not inferred): *"**two or more of** [gas equipment backlog / annual production capacity · transformer lead time · major-market interconnection wait time] **improving materially for two consecutive quarters**, or **behind-the-meter generation becoming a standard deployment model** so that grid connection no longer sets turn-on timing."* **And his separate DEMAND reset condition: *"if capex guidance from the major hyperscalers is **cut for two consecutive quarters**, the demand map has to be redrawn before the supply map."*** ⚠️ **Capex backdrop he uses (guides, not his estimates): [[AMZN]] ~$220bn, [[GOOG]] $195-205bn, [[META]] $130-145bn, with the big four **on track to exceed $700bn in 2026** — note [[META]]’s figure is the **company guide**, which is the correct base per the CY-sum hazard already logged.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-02 — THE TWO LARGEST BUYERS OF AI DATACENTRE POWER PUBLICLY DISAGREED ABOUT WHETHER POWER IS A CONSTRAINT, ON THE SAME DAY, WITHIN HOURS OF EACH OTHER. THIS PAGE SHOULD CARRY BOTH AND AVERAGE NEITHER.

- 🔴 **[[AVGO]] — POWER IS A BINDING, TIMING-DETERMINING CONSTRAINT.** Hock Tan, on the Q3 FY26 call (2026-09-02, 17:00 ET), naming his four bottlenecks as *"foundry, substrates, HBM and **land, power and shell**"*: *"**LPS is a big concern and dictates the SPECIFIC TIMING of when it gets deployed**… power shell has a **long lead time for construction**. We do analysis on this and **it's reflected in the forecasts**."* And on why the headline GW numbers are conservative: *"**it's not just about chips, it's about having the DC SHELL IN PLACE ready for production**… we are not saying it's 30GW into production. But **if they can get it all in place** and we can ship the chips and racks, it will be 30GW from the 6 customers."* ➤ **Broadcom has explicitly HAIRCUT its own two-year $350bn guide for power/shell availability. That is a vendor pricing the constraint into a published forecast — the strongest form this page can get.** (Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, verbatim call notes, 2026-09-02.)
- 🔴 **[[NVDA]] — POWER IS *NOT* A SERIOUS FY28 CONCERN.** From the BofA-hosted IR fireside with VP IR **Toshiya Hari** the same day: *"NVDA sees **memory and substrate/boundary wafers** as the clear top-2 constraints while **POWER IS NOT A SERIOUS FY28 CONCERN** given **many ex-US regions remain welcoming** and availability looks **reasonable vs. TSMC's view**"* (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-09-02). JPM's readout of the same executive on a separate NDR names the bottlenecks as *"**memory and ADVANCED WAFERS**"* and does not mention power at all (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-09-02).
- ⚠️⚠️ **HOW TO HOLD THE DISAGREEMENT, BECAUSE IT IS PROBABLY NOT A CONTRADICTION:** the two statements have **different geographic scope and different time horizons**. NVIDIA's answer is explicitly global and leans on *"many EX-US regions remain welcoming"* — i.e. it can route supply to wherever power exists. Broadcom's customers are building **specific named US campuses** for six named customers, so shell and interconnect timing binds at the SITE level. ➤ **Reconciliation: power is not a constraint on global AI compute in FY28; it is a constraint on WHERE and WHEN specific gigawatts land. That is entirely consistent with this page's 09-01 evidence of measurable US pipeline attrition (EXC's "high-probability" DC demand cut ~40% to 11GW, AEP Ohio's pipeline down >50%, Pennsylvania's 100+ proposals against 20 permit applications) — the projects are not disappearing, the US ones are slipping.** ⚠️ **Do NOT resolve this into a single house view. ## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 29, /run-inbox — **THE RETURNS ON A GIGAWATT, RANKED BY WHO YOU SELL IT TO — AND A PRE-IPO NEOCLOUD'S BACKER LIST AS A MAP OF THE FINANCING STACK**)

### 🔴 (1) A GIGAWATT IS NOT A GIGAWATT: the same 1GW earns a 10% IRR or a 45% IRR depending on the customer

**Rothschild & Co Redburn models the returns on **one gigawatt of compute** allocated four different ways at [[META]] — the most useful framing this theme has for why "selling compute" and "selling intelligence" are different businesses on identical hardware (15-year IRR, 5-year hardware depreciation cycle):**

| Use of 1GW | Revenue per 1GW | EBIT margin | IRR (0-15yr) | CROIC |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| **AI cloud — CONCENTRATED** (selling to model providers: [[ANTHROPIC]], [[OPENAI]], [[AMZN]]) | **$13bn** | 35% | **10%** | 8% |
| **AI cloud — DIVERSIFIED** (mid-market software & AI vendors on open-source models) | **$23bn** | 46% | **30%** | 19% |
| Core advertising | $22bn | **64%** | 43% | 26% |
| **SMB LLM** | **$25bn** | 58% | **45%** | **27%** |

➤ **THE INVESTABLE POINT: selling raw compute to a handful of frontier labs earns a 10% IRR — roughly a quarter of what the same gigawatt earns pointed at a diversified customer base or an owned software product. Redburn notes precedent deals set the concentrated price at *"c$13bn per GW"*, and prefers *"this diversified route to a concentrated neo-cloud business selling to a handful of buyers."* Zuckerberg's own framing, quoted in the note: *"the margin on selling intelligence is higher than raw compute."*** (Rothschild & Co Redburn · Dominic Ball, "Selling Intelligence over Selling Compute", 2026-07-30) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Selling_Intelligence_over_Selling_Compute.html)

⚠️⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE — THE STANDING `$/watt` RULE ON THIS WIKI APPLIES DIRECTLY HERE: the $13-25bn per GW above is REVENUE PER GW earned by the OWNER of the capacity. It is NOT a build cost. It must NEVER be netted against, or chained with, the ~$40bn/GW TOTAL BUILD COST used elsewhere on this wiki (Wells Fargo/Gawrelski 07-09 on [[META]]; BofA/Post 08-27). Two different quantities that share a denominator — mixing them silently inverts the economics.**

**Also from the same note, and relevant to every "should hyperscaler X build an AI cloud" debate on this page: Redburn argues building the next "AI AWS" would be *"a material uphill battle, including a massive incremental capex build-up (AWS HAS SPENT $450BN SINCE 2011), with an IRR BELOW Meta's core ads business."*** **Their [[META]] GW model has **c6GW of over-capacity by 2030** (excess/AI-cloud rising 0.6GW → 6.2GW of a 14.2GW total), with training FALLING from 50% to 10% of the mix as inference scales.**

### (2) NSCALE — a pre-IPO neocloud whose investor list is itself the datapoint

**Bloomberg Intelligence published a pre-IPO assessment of NSCALE, and for this theme the useful content is the FINANCING AND PARTNER STACK rather than the governance write-up.** ⚠️ **No covered company is the subject here — logged as a theme read-through only, no company page patched.**

- **Strategic backers span the whole stack: [[NVDA]] (compute), Dell and Lenovo (systems), Nokia (connectivity), Aker (Nordic industrial and POWER expertise) — plus financial capital from Citadel, Jane Street, Point72, Fidelity and Blue Owl.** ➤ **The presence of Blue Owl and a sovereign-adjacent industrial partner alongside the silicon vendor is the same off-balance-sheet financing pattern this wiki is tracking at [[META]] (BlackRock JV) and [[ANTHROPIC]] (Theseus / MAM / GIC) — a third instance of the structure, at a company small enough that it must disclose it in an S-1.**
- **[[MSFT]] is the anchor customer, and Nscale hired Nidhi Chappell — the former Microsoft AI-infrastructure chief who led the supercomputer infrastructure supporting ChatGPT.** Global headcount topped **800 in August** with **200+ openings** concentrated in AI infrastructure, data centres and finance.
- **The strategy is explicitly full-stack, not GPU rental: the agreed ANYSCALE acquisition adds Ray-based orchestration and ~200 software employees, which BI argues *"should raise switching costs and provide more scope to capture economics beyond infrastructure rental."*** ➤ **Consistent with the Redburn table above: the neocloud business model is migrating up-stack precisely because raw compute rental is the lowest-IRR use of a gigawatt.**
- ⚠️ **BI's own caveats, recorded: the operating track record is short, execution *"is unproven at scale"*, and strategic investors *"may also benefit commercially from its growth"* — so the backer list does not establish stand-alone economics. Board (Sheryl Sandberg, Nick Clegg, Susan Decker) is framed as IPO-grade governance, with the S-1 to reveal founder control, related-party terms and the rationale for 2025 director departures.** (Bloomberg Intelligence · Vasu Kasibhotla, "Nscale's People, Partners Bolster Pre-IPO Execution Credibility", 2026-08-28) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260828_Nscale-s_People-_Partners_Bolster_Pre_IPO_Execution_Credibility.html)

- ⚠️ **THE LEAKAGE CLAIM, worth tracking as a political datapoint: "if we have new companies going for GPUs, it's often in Mexico or Australia or another country just because it is so difficult in the United States… we're creating huge both job and long-term economic opportunity in other countries by banning data centers here."** ➤ Same direction as the permitting/moratorium thread; **note the speaker's incentive — this is also the argument a fund wants US policymakers to hear.** Water usage is separately conceded at *"probably 10%"* of DCs *"wasting a lot of water"*.
- **Two smaller items.** **Robots in the halls:** the large DC owners are *"furiously experimenting with the robots… to do the work of assembling or putting servers into the data center."* **GW intuition, for sanity-checking headlines:** 1 GW ≈ **~50,000 homes**, i.e. roughly a town of 40-60k people — and *"there's very few gigawatt data centers that are actually up. We've got a long way to go."*

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 27, /wiki-ingest — **A 1.35GW CAMPUS THAT RUNS *OFF-GRID* ON 2GW OF ON-SITE GAS PLUS BATTERIES, AND FOUR MORE $/GW FIGURES ON FOUR DIFFERENT BASES**) — 🔴🔴

### 🔴🔴 THE NSCALE MONARCH CAMPUS IS FULLY BEHIND-THE-METER, AND [[ANTHROPIC]] IS PAYING $45BN OVER SIX YEARS FOR 460MW OF IT
***"[[ANTHROPIC]] has agreed to pay **Nscale $45bn over six years to rent 460MW** of AI compute (on **[[NVDA]] Vera Rubin** chips, **online late next year**) at Nscale's **Monarch campus in WEST VIRGINIA**, the first of **three buildings totalling about 1.35GW**. The site was **originally reserved by [[MSFT]] under a March letter of intent, but Microsoft walked away this summer** and Anthropic stepped in… The full campus carries roughly **$71bn of capex ($47bn of it chips)**, equating to about **$52.6bn per GW including GPUs (around $17.9bn for the physical infrastructure alone)**, consistent with the emerging **$50bn-per-GW benchmark**, and 🔴 **it will run OFF-GRID on 2GW of ON-SITE NATURAL GAS PLUS BATTERIES.**"*** (Rothschild & Co Redburn, TMT Daily, 2026-08-27.)
➤ 🔴 **2GW of on-site generation for a 1.35GW campus — a ~1.5x overbuild ratio. Going fully off-grid removes interconnection-queue risk entirely and moves the binding constraint to **gas turbine and battery supply**, plus gas offtake. This is the clearest single example on this page of AI capital routing around the utility interconnection process rather than waiting in it. Read with the [[SPCX]] primary already on this wiki (15-20GW of power/cooling deliberately overbuilt against ~10GW of nameplate compute).**
- **And the counterparty context: for Nscale, *"landing Anthropic as anchor tenant is essential ahead of its own **IPO as soon as next month**"*; the deal continues Anthropic's *"pre-IPO compute land-grab, following **$50bn with Fluidstack, $45bn with SpaceX and $10bn with Volta**"* — **~$150bn of committed compute across four counterparties.**

- **THE POWER-EFFICIENCY LEVER IS NOW THE HEADLINE PRODUCT METRIC: [[NVDA]] frames AI-factory performance as **tokens per megawatt**, with **Vera Rubin at ~30x higher throughput per MW and 35x lower token cost vs Grace Blackwell Ultra** (MS · Moore, 2026-08-27), and **NVHBM claiming −15% HBM power and +25% free XPU die area**. ## Recent signals (Aug 25 — Invest Like the Best EP488 / Sail Research) — 🔴 **A CONTRARIAN ESCAPE HATCH FROM THE POWERED-SHELL BOTTLENECK: BACKGROUND INFERENCE CAN AGGREGATE SMALL, LOW-UPTIME AND INTERMITTENT SITES THAT TRAINING CLUSTERS CANNOT USE.**

- **Practitioner frame (Neil Movva, Sail Research founder/CEO, 2026-08-25):** in the US, Movva describes a new-site ladder of **~1 GW as effectively impossible, ~100 MW increasingly hard, ~10 MW near the edge of feasibility and ~1 MW as plentiful**. With liquid cooling, he says **~1 MW of compute can fit in roughly eight refrigerator-sized racks.** ⚠️ **These are a developer's availability observations and physical illustrations, not energised-capacity data, facility-GW forecasts or additions to the canonical GW ledger.**
- 🔴 **THE SERVICE-LEVEL TRADE:** Sail is willing to consider sites with only **~95% uptime—and perhaps ~80% at the right price—**with no backup diesel, limited power/network redundancy and occasional multi-minute retries, because long-running agents can checkpoint and move elsewhere. Training and real-time inference cannot generally tolerate the same architecture.
- 🔴 **THE RENEWABLES ANGLE:** weather-dependent solar/wind can become usable without sizing storage for full baseload if the control plane forecasts multi-day outages and migrates work to another region. The physical arbitrage works only if chips are cheap enough that idle capacity is acceptable and failures are uncorrelated across sites.
- **Investment read:** the proposal expands the addressable supply from scarce hyperscale shells to stranded/distributed megawatts and interruptible generation. It is therefore a potential release valve for inference—not evidence that the large powered-shell, grid, turbine or construction constraints tracked on this page have disappeared.
- **What would falsify it:** state migration costs overwhelm the power discount; inference demand remains latency-sensitive; outages correlate geographically; or small-site operating overhead erases the benefit. ⚠️ Sail discloses no fleet size, realized uptime, PUE or customer economics in the interview.

Stocks across the board all exposed and **no upside surprises… all is negative as far as project delays, lower power prices, negative revision environment**"* … *"**If TX data centers are delayed, EVERY STATE WILL HAVE DELAYS**"* … *"**Other states to worry about: VA and OH**"* … *"Typical August scare with low vols and trading on headlines."* Final ranking: *"**E&Cs and OEM most at risk; IPPs lack positive catalysts with lower forwards/lack of contracting; regulated utes — a lot of this priced into names like SRE, CNP; BTM — may have some impact, but the CLEAR WINNERS.**"*

A buyer and a seller jointly declining to underwrite 2027-28 data-center power volumes is a small, market-priced vote against the "power suppliers are guaranteed" framing.**
