The largest silicon vendor has now said it from a conference stage in its own words. That is management corroboration, not a new estimate.**

## Recent signals (Aug 24, /wiki-ingest — MS POWERED SHELL PROVIDERS + JPM OCP APAC + THE OCTOBER DEADLINE) — 🔴🔴 **THE BINDING CONSTRAINT ON AI REVENUE IS NOW NAMED, BY A SILICON ANALYST, AS POWERED SHELL: UBS SEES BROADCOM'S RAMP "TIED TO READINESS OF POWERED-SHELL INFRASTRUCTURE RATHER THAN COMPUTE SILICON AVAILABILITY". MORGAN STANLEY PUTS A HARD DATE ON IT — CONTRACTS MUST BE SIGNED BY *OCTOBER 2026* FOR CAPACITY TO ENERGISE BEFORE YEAR-END 2027. AND JPM's OCP WORK SAYS AI-DC BUILD SCHEDULES HAVE ALREADY STRETCHED FROM ~8 MONTHS TO 12-18+ MONTHS.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE CROSS-VALIDATION THAT MAKES THIS WEEK MATTER. From the silicon side (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, "SemiBytes Weekly", 2026-08-24):** UBS sees **[[AVGO]]'s AI revenue ramp timing *"tied to readiness of POWERED-SHELL infrastructure rather than compute silicon availability."*** **From the real-estate side, the same morning (MS · Stephen C. Byrd w/ Vishwas Patkar, Fernanda Lima, James E. Faucette, Michael N. Infante, "Powered Shell Providers: Valuation Debates, ERCOT, Power Shortfalls, and Recommended Positioning", 2026-08-24):** *"we expect to see many PSP-data center contracts by October, driven by the growing power and data center shortfall facing AI players"* — because **October 2026 "would be the latest possible date to begin construction on data centers that would come online before year-end 2027."* **Two teams, two asset classes, one constraint. October is now a dated, falsifiable checkpoint.**
- 🔴 **JPM's OCP APAC read quantifies the slippage that creates the constraint (JPM · Albert Hung w/ Anthony Leng, Gokul Hariharan, "Servers: 10 key takeaways from 2026 APAC's OCP Summit", Taipei Aug 11-12, note 2026-08-23):** *"Token demand remains strong, but datacenter infrastructure delivery is emerging as a potential bottleneck… as AI datacenters grow more complex and larger in scale (multi-hundred-MW to GW-scale), time-to-market for deployment is becoming increasingly challenging. As a result, build schedules for AI datacenters have extended from ~8 MONTHS TO 12-18+ MONTHS."* ⚠️ **And the consequence it names: "Any slippage in construction timelines could trigger a temporary INVENTORY-ADJUSTMENT cycle across the supply chain."** That is the cleanest statement on this page of how a power/shell delay transmits into a semis correction.
- **MS's PSP economics, with every assumption on the record (so the model can be re-run):** a **20-year lease, $2.38/watt annual lease payment, 85% margins, $11.50/watt construction cost, 80% LTV, 7.25% project debt cost, 6.7% terminal cap rate (≈15x EV/EBITDA), 9% equity discount rate** ⇒ **PV of all equity cash flows ≈ ~$11/watt** (including equity to fund construction). MS's published models show **future value creation of $13-17/watt, discounting to $10-12/watt today.** Forward-deal expectations: **longer duration (20+ years), stronger credit support, and "somewhat higher average yields on cost (but we see an upper limit at perhaps an average yield of 18-19%)"** — earlier in the note phrased as *"we expect average unlevered yields to remain below 20%."* ⚠️ **UNITS DISCIPLINE: these are BUILD-COST and LEASE-PAYMENT dollars per watt, not revenue-per-watt. Do not net them against "$/watt" figures quoted from AI-infra revenue notes.**
- 🔴 **ERCOT — MS says Friday's PSP selloff was a misreading, and gives two dated gates:** the concern was that the **765 kV transmission project**, if it does not proceed, would invalidate all ERCOT data-centre interconnection studies. MS: *"This is an incorrect interpretation… We actually view this development as a positive."* Why — if 765 kV dies, **several Base Load projects (2030-32 interconnection dates) drop out, so the 65 GW in that group "could fall to a much smaller number", making the ERCOT grid more likely to accommodate the smaller set.** Dates: **by 31-August 2026 all proposed interconnection projects are notified of their provisional designation (base load / studied load / Batch 1)**; **final designation likely at the 17-December 2026 PUCT Open Meeting**; and **any highly-advanced project with an early-2027 energisation date is notified by 1-November 2026 at the latest** (MS believes **CIFR and HUT** have projects in that cohort). ⚠️ **Commercial discussions can advance after 31-Aug but transactions cannot be announced until the final designation — so expect a signing-to-announcement lag.** MS's picks: **OW CIFR, HUT, WULF, RIOT, GLXY**; sees upside in **EW APLD** and **UW MARA**; notes **RIOT's sites are not subject to the ERCOT Batch process "and are therefore increasingly valuable."**
- **Execution evidence MS cites, name by name:** **APLD** — *"all of our construction projects are on time and on budget"*; **RIOT** — AMD data-centre build-out *"on schedule and on budget"*; **CIFR** — Black Pearl campus delivered **two months ahead of schedule**; **CORZ (not covered)** — substantially completed four of five [[CRWV]] campuses ahead of schedule, reaching **437 billable MW**; **WULF** — on schedule but **total project cost now ~$9.1mn/MW versus the $8.6mn/MW financed in October 2025**, still inside original **$8-10mn** guidance, with the causes named as *"electrical labor constraints and evolving customer equipment and operating requirements."*
- 🔴 **MS's labour-risk stress test, because it bounds the bear case:** if **labour is ~$4/watt of a ~$12/watt total cost** and overruns by **20%**, that sits inside contract contingency (**believed >12% of total project cost**). For shareholders to lose **$1/watt** (7-8% of net equity value creation) at a **14% contingency**, **labour costs would need to run 67% above expectations — "a low probability outcome."** ⚠️ **But MS still calls labour cost overrun risk "material, given chronic shortages o[f] skilled labor (especially for electricians, welders and pipefitters)."**
- **Credit strategists' positioning, and it is a structural preference:** *"On the back of increasing debt issuance across multiple credit channels to finance the AI infrastructure build, our credit strategists favor COLLATERAL-BACKED risk, such as the project finance deals by the PSPs, over UNSECURED CORPORATE risk."* Context: MS Credit's own tracker this week is titled **"AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider"** (MS · Vishwanath Tirupattur et al., 2026-08-21), and ⚠️ **[[AVGO]] CDS reportedly hit a record 122 while [[NVDA]] went to a new all-time wide** after AVGO's ~$100bn off-balance-sheet SPV deal (@zerohedge relaying Bloomberg screens, 2026-08-22 — **level unverified, direction corroborated**).
- 🔴 **THE POWER-ARCHITECTURE ROADMAP FROM OCP, WITH A CHANGED PREFERENCE (JPM · Hung, 2026-08-23):** **800VDC ramp in late 2026/27 unchanged, with migration to solid-state transformers (SST) in 2027/28**; HVDC power racks *"remain on track to ramp in 4Q26/1Q27, with optional adoption by VR200 servers and select ASIC models."* 🔴 **"We sense a growing preference for a direct 800VDC power rack over ±400V, as the mid-point ground adds complexity and could limit the future flexibility of AI datacenters."** See themes/800v-dc-power.
- **Two content read-throughs from the same note:** **vertical power delivery (VPD) — two ASIC vendors have migrated from lateral to vertical** to shorten power paths and cut losses, which *"require[s] smaller, higher-density, lower-impedance power components"*: **positive for high-end MLCCs (high-capacitance 0201/0402) and high-Isat molded inductors**, naming **Murata, SEMCO** and VPM vendors **Delta and [[FLEX]]**. And **HVDC migration raises the testing barrier**: moving from **54V power shelves to HVDC racks and 100kW-to-MW-class systems** needs materially higher-voltage/higher-power source equipment, while **AI workload transients make dynamic-response testing harder** (capturing voltage undershoot/overshoot and validating PSU/CBU/BBU/DC-bus interactions) — *"drives both volume growth and higher ASP/content-per[-unit]"*, favouring **vendors with total-solution capability.** Read-through to [[TER]] / [[ADVANTEST]] / [[AEIS]].
- **The permitting/social constraint, from the trade press:** *"America Hates Data Centers"* (The Information, 2026-08-22) and *"Blocking Datacenter Development is Bipartisan"* (JPM TECH SKETCH · Mark Schilsky, 2026-08-21) — plus **Bernstein's own DC survey flagging "2x ordering"** and **PA data-centre pushback** (Bernstein AM Note, 2026-08-21). ⚠️ **"2x ordering" is a double-counting warning on the order book, not a demand signal.**
- **NVDA's move up the stack, restated with terms (UBS · Arcuri, 2026-08-24):** Jensen's blog describes deeper involvement in procuring and financing **land, power and shell ("LPS")**. **PORTS-Pike: [[OPENAI]] remains tenant/operator; NVDA supplies land/power/shell via a $1.5bn investment alongside SB Energy plus up to $105bn of residual value/credit support; 4.25GW initial with an option to ~8GW.** 🔴 **NVDA's own estimate: each generation of AI-factory systems at the site ≈ 1.5mn GPUs ≈ $150-200bn of revenue, with multiple upgrade cycles over the campus life.** UBS reads NVDA's investments in **SB Energy, Lancium and Cloverleaf Infrastructure** as securing **"corridors of capacity"**, and flags that it *"may even set the stage for NVDA to ultimately become more vertically integrated as both a developer and potentially even an operator of AI factory assets."*

_(Sources this block: **Morgan Stanley Global Credit Strategy & Securitized Products** — Fernanda Lima / Carolyn L Campbell / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Vishwas Patkar / James Egan / Aron Becker / Jonathan Loke / Mayank Verma / Lindsay Tyler / Kate Konetzke, **"AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20**, 36pp · **Morgan Stanley US Thematics** — Michelle M. **① 🔴🔴 THE OFFTAKER-PRICED PROJECT-BOND TABLE — the hardest evidence this page has ever carried on what a hyperscaler offtake is actually worth, because it is priced by lenders with money at risk on comparable steel and comparable megawatts** (MS "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20). **None of these issuers has a wiki page — plain text with the bond identifier.**

| Offtaker | Bond | Site / critical capacity | Yield |
|---|---|---|--:|
| [[CRWV]] | **APLD 9.250 '30** | Ellendale, ND — **250 MW** | **8.2%** |
| [[CRWV]] | **CORZ 7.750 '31** | TX / GA / OK / NC — **589 MW** | **11.5%** |
| [[CRWV]] + Alibaba | **EDGCOM 7.500 '31** | Chicago / Atlanta — **114 MW** | **14.2%** |
| [[CRWV]] | **ELKGVP 7.500 '31** | Chicago, IL — **72 MW** | **12.0%** |
| [[AMZN]] | **STNGRY 6.000 '31** | Wink, TX — **216 MW** | **7.9%** |
| [[AMZN]] | **STNGRY 6.000 '31** | Andrews, TX — **70 MW** | **8.8%** |

- ➤ **THE POINT, AND IT IS THE ONE THIS PAGE HAS BEEN CIRCLING SINCE THE AUG-17 EPOCH TABLE: the credit market charges roughly 300-600BP MORE FOR CoreWeave OFFTAKE THAN FOR AMAZON OFFTAKE. That is a market-priced, observable measure of counterparty quality inside this build — far harder than any equity-side opinion on whether neocloud offtake is bankable. It is also the missing price axis on the Aug-17 project-finance table (five project companies, 1,428 MW of critical IT, ~$15.183bn of debt): that table gave the QUANTITY of project debt per MW; this one gives the PRICE, and the price is set by who signs the lease.**
- ⚠️ **UNIT AND BASIS DISCIPLINE, because this page has been burned repeatedly: the MW figures above are the SITE capacities MS attaches to each bond, and the yields are secondary-market/new-issue yields on PROJECT DEBT. They are NOT build costs, NOT revenue-per-watt, and NOT comparable to the facility-GW marks elsewhere on this page. Do not chain them into any $/GW series, and do not net them against the ~$10.6M-of-project-debt-per-MW-of-critical-IT anchor derived from Epoch on Aug 17 — that is a leverage figure, this is a price of leverage.**
- **The rest of the same table, for the cost-of-capital ladder: [[CRWV]] term loan (S+550), drawn 7/30/26, 9/30/31 maturity, BB, 9.15% all-in, spread 584 → 527bp (−57bp); QTS (S+225) BBB−, 5.90%, 236 → 231; "LAMBCM" (Lambda) 8/12/26, 6.65%, 303 → 287. Investment-grade new issues: [[GOOG]] 8/6/26, the '31 at 4.88% (spread 60 → 56) and the '36 at 5.45% (85 → 81); EQIX '31 5.25% (95 → 96), '36 5.80% (120 → 127)** (MS, 2026-08-20). ⚠️ **CoreWeave's LOAN spread tightened 57bp in the same window in which Equinix's IG BONDS widened — so the direction of AI-credit repricing differs by instrument and by issuer, not just by theme. Anyone using "AI credit spreads" as a single indicator is aggregating two opposite moves.**
- 🔴 **THE FUNDING CHANNEL DEDICATED TO THE PHYSICAL BUILD DID NOT EXIST A YEAR AGO: "Data Center Construction" issuance went from $0 in the prior-year YTD period to $43BN YTD-2026, alongside REITs at $5bn, inside a $271bn total IG print** (MS, 2026-08-20). **MS's stated preference on the back of it is FAVOUR COLLATERAL-BACKED RISK, INCLUDING DATA CENTERS — i.e. take the asset rather than the promise — while flagging that "the pace of new assets FROM TEXAS TO OHIO added to the noise on bonds with HIGHER CONSTRUCTION RISK."** ➤ **Read that geography against this page's own political map: Texas is where ERCOT is auditing >474 GW of queued load and where projects were halted pending audits (Aug 14/15), and Ohio is where the PORTS-Pike campus sits. The bond market is charging for construction risk in exactly the two states where this page has logged the binding non-financial constraints.**

**② 🔴🔴 THE FALSIFIER SERIES: ~$156BN OF PROJECTS CANCELLED OR DELAYED IN 2025, AND $130BN ALREADY IN 1Q26 — AND THE 1Q26 FIGURE IS RUNNING AT ROUGHLY 3.3x THE 2025 ANNUAL PACE.** *"Third parties report an estimated value of ~$156 billion of projects were cancelled or delayed in 2025, and $130 billion already in 1Q26"* (MS "Power Struggle: AI, Data Centers & Local Backlash", 2026-07-14). **MS's own conclusion: *"If realized — or if local opposition continues to grow, even if contained at the state level — then we see A GREATER RISK OF ACTUAL AI CAPEX FALLING SHORT OF OUR $877 BILLION ESTIMATE FOR 2026, carrying a multitude of implications across equity sectors & the macro backdrop."***
- ➤ **WHY THIS IS THE SINGLE MOST VALUABLE ITEM IN THE NOTE FOR THIS WIKI: on 08-07 this page and [hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md) logged Bernstein's monthly "Capacity, Construction & Cancellations" tracker as a DATED POINTER, with the explicit note that the CANCELLATIONS series is "the single most direct falsifier available for the capex thesis." This is the first time that series arrives with numbers attached. $130bn in one quarter against $156bn in the prior full year is ~3.3x the annualised pace — say the multiple, because the level alone does not convey it.**
- ⚠️ **THREE THINGS TO HOLD BEFORE THIS BECOMES A BEAR HEADLINE. (1) "CANCELLED OR DELAYED" is one bucket — a delayed project is deferred capex, not destroyed capex, and MS does not split the two. (2) The figures are attributed by MS to "third parties," unnamed in the captured text, so the methodology is not inspectable. (3) It is a PROJECT-VALUE series, not a capex series, so it is not subtractable from the $877bn. Carry it as a RATE OF CHANGE and as a falsifier tripwire, not as a haircut.**
- **THE MECHANISM, and note where MS says the action is: *"Community pushback against data center construction has ACCELERATED IN 2026 as new moratoriums have been introduced, with the MAJORITY OF THE CHANGE HAPPENING AT THE LOCAL LEVEL. This puts PRESSURE ON COSTS AND TIMELINES and could ALTER THE GEOGRAPHIC DISTRIBUTION of data centers."* Opposition falls into three main categories; moratoriums *"have precedent, and are expanding at the state and local level,"* with a growing number of localities passing or proposing guardrails and restrictions — and FEDERAL LAWMAKERS NOW INTRODUCING LEGISLATION toward the same end.** ➤ ✅ **CORROBORATION OF THE STRUCTURE THIS PAGE ALREADY BUILT, not a new claim: the Aug 14/15 block already concluded that pushback is "systemic rather than local" and the Aug 12 companion already logged the first local-politics constraint. What MS adds is (a) that the majority of the CHANGE is happening at the local level — which cuts against reading state-level halts as the main channel — and (b) the federal leg, which is new to this page.**
- **THE POLITICAL FRAME, which dates the call: MS had named "the politics of energy" among its 10 thematic predictions for 2026, specifically *"the risk of rising backlash against data center growth becoming a KEY WEDGE ISSUE AHEAD OF THE MIDTERM ELECTIONS"* — and states *"this has played out so far: as the issue rises in salience with voters."*** ➤ **Consistent with the corpus read this page carries from 08-19 that the Pennsylvania pushback is "probably some degree of pre-Midterms political posturing" — same mechanism, opposite trading conclusion. Both retained.**
- ⚠️🔴 **SCOPE FLAG THAT MUST TRAVEL WITH THE $877BN, OTHERWISE THIS PAGE WILL READ AS IF MS CUT ITS NUMBER: MS's "Power Struggle" figure of $877BN of AI capex in 2026 is a GLOBAL measure. MS's later "AI Sovereignty" note (2026-08-19) puts "$860bn in the US THIS YEAR ALONE" — a US-SCOPED measure. THESE ARE DIFFERENT MEASURES, NOT A REVISION. Neither supersedes the other, and they must never be presented as a $17bn mark-down.** ⚠️ **Also do not net either against the ~$860bn "broad cloud aggregate" BofA figure already on [hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md) (08-02) — the numerical coincidence with the MS US figure is exactly that, a coincidence of two unrelated perimeters.**

**③ ✅ PORTS-PIKE / SB ENERGY — LOGGED AS CORROBORATION, NOT AS NEWS, WITH ONE INTERPRETIVE ADDITION.** An independent account of the same campus this page already carries as signed (Aug 17 block) restates the structure: **[[NVDA]] credit support for the land, power and shell of an initial 4.25 GW of IT capacity with an option over a further 3.75 GW (8 GW total); a $1.5bn NVDA investment into SB Energy; NVDA as the site's EXCLUSIVE AI compute provider; [[OPENAI]] expected to occupy eight gigawatts under a 20-year lease; SB Energy builds, owns and operates; first phases expected in 2028** (Ash & Seed Press · Shanaka Anslem Perera, "THE CONTINUITY STACK", 2026-08-18). ⚠️⚠️ **ATTRIBUTION WARNING: the ingest router tagged this file's broker as "Goldman." FALSE — Goldman Sachs is merely NAMED IN THE BODY as one of the six financing partners on the [[NVDA]] platform. This is an INDEPENDENT PAID SUBSTACK with no rating and no price target. Do not lend it bank authority.** ✅ **Every structural term above is already on this page from the 08-17 signing and the 08-20 Jefferies item, and the "up to 25%" residual-value mechanic is already on this page from the Aug-11 block — all logged as corroboration from a second non-company source, which raises confidence that the 25% figure is CIRCULATING, not that it is DISCLOSED.**
- 🔴 **THE ONE GENUINELY NEW IDEA, AND IT IS AN INTERPRETIVE ONE WORTH CARRYING BECAUSE IT REFRAMES WHAT THE BACKSTOP IS FOR: *"NVIDIA is not merely supporting the resale price of aging chips. It is supporting the FIXED INFRASTRUCTURE THAT ALLOWS SUCCESSIVE GENERATIONS OF CHIPS TO REMAIN INSTALLED, POWERED, AND BILLABLE."*** ➤ **That is the residual-value argument relocated from the silicon to the powered shell — and on this page's own numbers it is the more defensible version of it. The 30-plus-year shell amortised across five to six server generations ([[AMZN]], 07-30) and the A100-contracted-to-2029 datapoint ([[CRWV]], 08-19/20) both say the durable asset is the POWERED SITE, not the accelerator. If that is what is being guaranteed, the guarantee is written against the asset with the longest life in the stack rather than the shortest. ⚠️ It is an independent author's characterisation, not a disclosed NVIDIA rationale.**

**④ 🔴 THE SOVEREIGN LEG NOW HAS AN EXPLICIT POWER REQUIREMENT ATTACHED TO IT, and it is additive to every GW forecast on this page.** MS's AI-sovereignty work states that sovereign capacity mechanically requires *"additional domestic and regional data centers, greater redundancy, MORE POWER GENERATION AND TRANSMISSION, localized cloud and networking infrastructure, and in some cases, PARALLEL TECHNOLOGY STACKS"* — with the conclusion that governments seeking control drive *"both a more fragmented as well as more CAPITAL-INTENSIVE global AI landscape"* which *"should extend and reinforce the AI investment cycle"* (MS "AI Sovereignty: Policy, Market, and Economic Implications", 2026-08-19). ➤ **REDUNDANCY AND PARALLEL STACKS ARE THE OPERATIVE WORDS FOR THIS PAGE: they mean the same unit of AI demand converts into MORE megawatts under a sovereignty regime than under a globally-fungible one, because capacity is duplicated across jurisdictions rather than pooled. That is a structural upward pressure on the GW series this page tracks, and it runs in the OPPOSITE direction to the cancellation/delay series in ② above. Both are live; neither is netted.** Full treatment on [secure-sovereign-infrastructure](secure-sovereign-infrastructure.md); the Korea power leg (SK Telecom + [[NVDA]], up to 2 GW of AI data-centre capacity via a new entity "SK Hyper", first stage a 100 MW facility for AWS online late 2027 at a site with 1 GW maximum capacity) is filed on [outros-asia](outros-asia.md).

## Recent signals (Aug 20, /wiki-ingest) — 🔴🔴 **THE CHIP-DEMAND-vs-GRID-SUPPLY GAP GETS ITS CLEANEST NUMBER YET: ~30 GW SHORT IN C27 AND ~53 GW SHORT IN C28 — AND THE ANALOG CONTENT PER GW IS NOW DISCLOSED AT $1.0-1.5bn**

- 🔴🔴 **THE GAP, QUANTIFIED BY THE SELL-SIDE ANALYST CLOSEST TO THE CHIP RAMP: "the one macro question we keep coming back to is POWER, where the gap between chip demand and grid supply looks like ~30 GW in C27 and ~53 GW in C28, and state-level pushback on new builds only adds to it."** (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, [[NVDA]] preview, 2026-08-20.) ➜ **This page has carried the power bottleneck qualitatively (powered shells, blades & vanes, ERCOT's >474 GW queue audit, the Texas halt) and financially (~$510bn of hyperscaler debt capacity vs $5.8tn of planned capex). This is the first clean GW-denominated statement of the SHORTFALL itself, from the supply side of the compute, and it is the number to carry forward. Note it is a gap between what the silicon roadmap implies and what the grid can energise — not a forecast of what gets built.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE ANALOG/POWER-SEMI CONTENT PER GW IS NOW A DISCLOSED FIGURE RATHER THAN AN ESTIMATE: [[ADI]] puts its own data-centre opportunity at $1.0-1.5bn of ANALOG SAM per GW of AI infrastructure build** (UBS · Arcuri and BofA · Arya, both 2026-08-19; reconfirmed on the 08-20 IR call). ➜ **A per-GW content number is exactly what this page has needed to convert GW forecasts into revenue for the electrical layer. Cross-check it against the $/watt hazard already flagged on this wiki: this is ANALOG CONTENT per GW of build, NOT revenue-per-watt and NOT $/GW build cost — the three must never be chained.**
- 🔴 **AND THE PART OF THAT SAM THAT IS STILL UNWON, WHICH IS WHERE THE SHARE SHIFT WILL SHOW UP: per ADI's post-print IR call, "POWER is 50% of the SAM but ADI's share remains LOW — creating greater upside than optical, where ADI already leads the control plane." Stage-one 800V conversion and protection are already winning content (hot-swap controllers), and "core power is the largest opportunity as architectures migrate from LATERAL to VERTICAL and eventually IN-PACKAGE."** 🔴 **Empower's IVR (Integrated Voltage Regulator) performance "has secured engagement with THREE LARGE HYPERSCALERS," with design decisions occurring in 2H26** (BofA · Arya, 2026-08-20). ➜ **First named count of IVR hyperscaler engagements on this wiki. The lateral → vertical → in-package power progression is the architecture roadmap behind the 800V transition this page tracks — and 2H26 design decisions are the dated falsifier. See [[800v-dc-power]].**
- 🔴 **ADI also states the DC segment's own inflection year: "2028 represents a potentially strong INFLECTION for the segment," with strong growth through decade-end, and ADI's own CY30 data-centre + energy-infrastructure TAM has DOUBLED versus what management assumed one year ago** (BofA, 2026-08-19/20). ➜ **A doubling of an internal TAM assumption in twelve months is a datapoint about forecast instability as much as about demand — carry it as evidence that per-GW electrical content estimates on this page are still being revised UP, not converging.**
- 🔴 **THE BALANCE-SHEET LAYER TAKES ANOTHER STEP TOWARD THE CHIP VENDOR, AND THE GW FIGURE IS LARGE: the 8/17 [[OPENAI]]/SB Energy agreement at the former Portsmouth site has [[NVDA]] providing credit support on an initial 4.25 GW (option to 8 GW) plus a $1.5bn investment — taking OpenAI's total commitment to ~16 GW / ~$600bn of compute through 2030.** Jefferies: **"a material change to what sits on NVDA's balance sheet that may reignite circular-financing concerns"** (2026-08-20). ➜ **Consistent with the "vendor-supported financing" mechanism this page named on Aug 17, and with the Ohio backstop structure logged the same week. The pattern is now established rather than exceptional: the compute vendor is underwriting the POWER commitment, not just the silicon.**
- **Demand-side corroboration that the constraint is not the chip:** *"Investors also note bullish datapoint from [[CRWV]] earnings that they are signing contracts for A100 chips (which are from 2020!) out to 2029"* (MS TMT Sales relaying Joe Moore, 2026-08-19), and GS confirms **"pricing remains FIRM across new AND legacy GPU generations"** at CoreWeave (2026-08-20). ➜ **Six-year-old silicon contracted to 2029 at firm pricing is a statement about POWERED CAPACITY scarcity, not about compute performance.**

## Recent signals (Aug 18, /run-inbox) — 🔴 **THE CREDIT DESK PUTS A CEILING ON HOW MUCH OF THE AI BUILD THE BOND MARKET CAN ABSORB: ~$510BN OF INCREMENTAL HYPERSCALER DEBT CAPACITY BEFORE INDEX-CONCENTRATION LIMITS BIND — AGAINST $5.8TN OF PLANNED CAPEX**

_(Source: **Goldman Sachs Credit Strategy · Amanda Lynam** w/ Spencer Rogers, Sara Grut, Shamshad Ali — "Global Credit Trader: AI-related financing — A mix of markets will be required", 2026-07-09 — [link](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/458094ae-fd80-435d-b209-406bcfab4123.html). ⚠️ **CREDIT-STRATEGY note, not equity research: no ratings, no PTs, no company estimates. Dated 07-09 and swept late — the sizing is the value, not the market colour.**)_

- 🔴 **THE CONSTRAINT, AND IT IS A NEW *KIND* OF CONSTRAINT FOR THIS THEME: the objection is not that hyperscalers lack borrowing capacity on credit metrics — "the extremely low leverage levels of the largest hyperscalers provide ample headroom to add much more debt while staying well within the typical credit metric guardrails of IG." The objection is INDEX CONCENTRATION. "Adding such large amounts of debt would push hyperscalers' USD IG index weights well above current market conventions," and credit investors — "more focused on downside risk vs. their equity market peers" — may not accept the issuer concentration.** ➤ **Using the US Banks (currently the largest IG index issuers) as the ceiling case, raising the five hyperscalers' index-eligible debt to bank-sized levels implies only ~$510BN of incremental capacity. Against $5.8tn of planned FY2025-FY2030 AI capex, the entire USD IG market's tolerance is under 9% of the bill.**
- **WHY THE CEILING MAY BE GENEROUS, in GS's own words: (1) given US equity sector weights, many investors ALREADY carry large Technology exposure, which limits appetite for hyperscaler debt when risk is evaluated holistically across the firm; (2) hyperscaler issuance skews LONGER in duration than banks', making dollar-for-dollar comparison less relevant.** ➤ **Both cut the same way — the $510bn is an upper bound on an upper bound.**
- **WHERE THE MARKET ALREADY IS: hyperscalers have issued $194BN across currencies YTD, ~9% of ALL IG supply globally, with 32% of it priced OUTSIDE the USD market. Total YTD 2026 USD IG gross supply reached $1.37tn against a full-year forecast of $2.1tn, with risks "firmly skewed to the upside." The Bloomberg USD IG Corporate Index has $7.9tn notional outstanding.** **[[AMZN]] priced a $25bn deal on 2026-07-07 that entered the index in August.**
- 🔴 **THE CONCLUSION, WHICH IS THE ONE TO CARRY: "a RANGE of financing markets will ultimately be required" — syndicated credit (IG, HY, loans), private markets (especially infrastructure), "new PROJECT FINANCE-STYLE JV STRUCTURES," multiple currencies, and bank financing.** ➤ **This is the macro-level statement of exactly what the Epoch/Anthropic and AI XPV structures already on this page do at the deal level: when the IG market cannot absorb the volume, the build moves to project-finance SPVs with vendor support. The Aug 17 site-by-site table (five project companies, 1,428 MW, ~$15.183bn raised before construction) is not an exotic structure — on GS's arithmetic it is the necessary one. Vendor-supported project finance is a CONSEQUENCE of a bond-market ceiling, not a preference.**
- ⚠️ **NO COMPANY PAGES PATCHED FROM THIS NOTE beyond a single [[AMZN]] capital-structure line. Its per-name content is bond-index mechanics (ratings, index weights, spread moves) and does not clear the bar for an equity page. [[INTC]], [[NFLX]], [[ORCL]], [[MSFT]], [[GOOG]] and [[META]] appear only in index constituent tables.**

## Recent signals (Aug 18, /wiki-ingest) — **THE DURABLE ASSET LEFT BY AN AI BUBBLE IS POWER, AND THAT IS BOTH SOCIALLY BULLISH AND NVIDIA-MARGIN BEARISH**

**Ben Thompson's railroad/dot-com analogy ends at the physical layer: GPUs age and data centers can be repurposed, but excess generation is the infrastructure residue that can raise economy-wide productive capacity.** The stock-level implication cuts the other way: a binding power constraint favors [[NVDA]] because buyers must pay for the best tokens/watt; power arriving faster than expected gives [[GOOG]] TPU and [[AMZN]] Trainium more time to close the efficiency gap, making merchant-GPU margins harder to sustain. This source introduces no new GW basis and does not alter the canonical facility-GW / IT-load-GW / 800V-subset guardrails (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).
## Recent signals (Aug 17, /run-inbox) — 🔴 **THE PROJECT-FINANCE LAYER GETS ITS FIRST FULL SITE-BY-SITE TABLE: FIVE PROJECT COMPANIES, 1,428 MW OF *CRITICAL IT*, ~$15.183BN OF DEBT RAISED **BEFORE** CONSTRUCTION — AND THE MECHANISM HAS A NAME, "VENDOR-SUPPORTED FINANCING"**

**Source: Epoch AI · Campbell Hutcheson, "Will financing bottleneck AI compute? An Anthropic case study" (Gradient Updates, 2026-08-12). Subject is [[ANTHROPIC]]'s buildout; the content that belongs to THIS page is the datacenter leg and the financing mechanism.**

- 🔴 **THE TABLE. Five dedicated project companies issued ~$15.183BN of debt to build 1,428 MW OF CRITICAL IT CAPACITY, leased by Fluidstack for Anthropic's deployment, with [[GOOG]] protecting investors against PART of the loss if Fluidstack stops paying rent** (at Lake Mariner specifically, Google can pay missed rent and assume the lease, or fund a termination payment applied to the project debt). **The debt was raised BEFORE construction began.**

| Project | Sponsor | Critical IT load | Project debt |
|---|---|--:|--:|
| Lake Mariner | TeraWulf | 378 MW | $3.200bn |
| Barber Lake | Cipher | 207 MW | $1.733bn |
| Abernathy | Fluidstack JV | 168 MW | $1.300bn |
| River Bend | Hut 8 | 245 MW | $3.250bn |
| Meridian Arc | Next Frontier / Fluidstack JV | 430 MW | $5.700bn |
| **Total** | | **1,428 MW** | **$15.183bn** |

- 🔴 **THE DERIVED ANCHOR, AND READ THE BASIS BEFORE USING IT: $15.183bn / 1,428 MW = ~$10.6M OF PROJECT *DEBT* PER MW OF *CRITICAL IT*, i.e. ~$10.6BN PER GW OF IT LOAD.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **CRITICAL UNIT DISCIPLINE** — this page's recurring hazard, and this number is a trap for three separate reasons. (1) It is a **DEBT** figure, not a build cost: it is what lenders advanced against the shells, not total project capex, and there is equity and sponsor capital on top of it. (2) It is measured against **CRITICAL IT LOAD**, not facility/nameplate GW — so it must NOT be compared with the facility-GW figures elsewhere on this page. (3) It is emphatically NOT a **REVENUE**-per-watt figure — do not net it against, chain it to, or difference it from the $/W revenue marks on this page (Musk's "$30–50" per watt of Rubin monetization; the ~$29/W blended Colossus rental; IREN's revenue-yield-per-IT-MW series), and do not compare it with the all-in build anchors ($39bn/GW MS, ~$36bn/GW Bernstein, ~$50bn/GW Mizuho, $50–60bn/GW BlackRock). State the basis every time it is quoted: **debt per MW of critical IT.**
- 🔴 **THE MECHANISM, NAMED, AND IT IS NOW A LIVE DETERMINANT OF BUILD SPEED: "VENDOR-SUPPORTED FINANCING" — suppliers use their own CREDIT (not their cash) to make deployments they profit from cheaper to finance.** Both legs of this buildout run on it: on the datacenter leg **Google** backstops part of the rent risk; on the compute leg **[[AVGO]]** backstops **$30bn of the $34.5bn** of debt raised against **>1 GW of Google TPU systems** (five-year Anthropic lease into the **"AI XPV Platform"** equipment SPV; A1 $6bn at Treasuries+1pp, A2 $24bn at 5.75%, B $4.5bn at 8.5% and unbackstopped; drawn in ~16 stages with ~$24bn out by summer 2027; Broadcom's reported maximum exposure $29bn). ➜ **This is the same structural item as the [[NVDA]] $500bn/third-party-capital and Ohio-guarantee threads on this page, arrived at from the lender's side: the constraint that gets relieved is COST OF CAPITAL, and the thing being consumed is SUPPLIER CREDIT.** Epoch's conclusion, with its condition intact: **financing is unlikely to be the immediate blocker to frontier AI compute growth — PROVIDED the suppliers who benefit from the deployment back part of the risk.** ⚠️ **So the tracked question on this page shifts from "is there capital?" to "how much supplier credit is left, and what happens to build velocity when a supplier declines to guarantee?"**
- 🔴 **WHY MULTIPLE DEVELOPERS RATHER THAN ONE — the cleanest statement this page carries of what a "powered site" actually is: a powered datacenter site is NOT interchangeable with an empty parcel of land. The valuable inputs are a LARGE POWER CONNECTION, COMPLETED INTERCONNECTION WORK, PERMITS, EQUIPMENT and an ESTABLISHED CONSTRUCTION PIPELINE** — so working across several developers and power markets in parallel is a deliberate way to assemble capacity faster than any single developer's queue position allows. ➜ Directly supports the interconnection-queue/powered-shell scarcity thesis already on this page (and the 08-13 "powered-up data center shell" quote from the buyer itself), and explains the diversification across TeraWulf, Cipher, Hut 8, Fluidstack and Next Frontier rather than a single anchor developer.
- ⚠️ **CROSS-PAGE CONSISTENCY NOTE: Lake Mariner appears on this page already via the WULF/TeraWulf thread (Bernstein's 07-07 "Anthropic contract at $19Bn", and WULF's 3,630 MW of power access). Epoch's $3.200bn is PROJECT DEBT at that site against 378 MW of critical IT — a different quantity from the contract value and from WULF's total power access. Do not net or compare the three.**

## Recent signals (Aug 17, /wiki-ingest) — 🔴🔴 **THE OHIO DEAL SIGNS AND THE GUARANTEE IS *HALVED*: [[NVDA]] BACKSTOPS <$120BN (REUTERS: UP TO $105BN) OF AN 8 GW-IT [[OPENAI]] CAMPUS, NOT THE $250BN THAT LEAKED — AND THE POLITICAL CONSTRAINT GETS ITS FIRST NUMBERS (ERCOT AUDITING >474 GW OF QUEUED LOAD, ~90% DATA-CENTER)**

**1. PORTS-Pike: the structure, as signed.** **[[NVDA]] "guarantees SB Energy's PORTS-Pike Technology Campus in Ohio to exclusively host NVIDIA AI compute"** (@nvidianewsroom, 2026-08-17), built on the old Portsmouth Gaseous Diffusion Plant site. Terms as reported: **~8 GW-IT total for [[OPENAI]] — 4.25 GW-IT initially with an NVDA option on a further 3.75 GW-IT; SB Energy builds, owns and operates under a 20-YEAR LEASE TO OPENAI; first 800 MW available in 2028 largely on existing AEP infrastructure; six-year buildout through 2032; 35,000 construction jobs and 2,500 long-term operating jobs. NVDA invests $1.5bn directly into SB Energy alongside SoftBank and OpenAI. SB Energy and SoftBank additionally plan at least 10 GW of new power generation and $4.2bn of regional grid investment"** (@firstadopter / @wallstengine / @StockMKTNewz, 2026-08-17).
- 🔴 **The term that moved is the one this page has been tracking: the financial guarantee was cut from $250bn to "less than $120bn" for the first ~5 GW phase BEFORE signature (WSJ, 2026-08-16), with Reuters putting the final number at UP TO $105BN. NVDA retains the option to finance the remainder later and separately "could finance up to $350bn of chip purchases across the build."** NVDA sizes the whole relationship at **"roughly $600 billion of NVIDIA compute through 2030"** (@FirstSquawk, 2026-08-17).
- Jensen's stated rationale, which is the clearest articulation yet of why the chip vendor is in the capital stack: **"frontier AI labs have extraordinary demand for training and inference compute, but many are growing faster than their balance sheets and long-term credit profiles can support. They may have strong customer demand and rapidly growing revenue yet still lack the decades-long infrastructure contracts and investment-grade financing capacity needed to secure the AI factory infrastructure independently"** (@firstadopter, 2026-08-17).
- ⚠️ **Where the risk actually sits, per the bear side: "Nvidia gets paid upfront, the borrowers and their lenders carry the default risk, and because private credit is ultimately funded by ordinary savers through instruments like pension funds, the tail risk lands on households"** (@carlquintanilla, 2026-08-16). Scale context: **"AI / Hyperscaler / Datacenter issuance (IG + Loans) is now tracking at roughly 12× the annual average of the entire 2015-2024 period — and already 2× all of 2025, at $269bn YTD"** (@zerohedge, 2026-08-16).

**2. The political constraint, now with numbers and dates.** MS (Michelle Weaver, Ariana Salvatore, Stephen Byrd, David Arcaro et al, "Power Struggle: Data Center Pushback Update & Expert Call Takeaways", 2026-08-17): **"Texas is auditing every data center advancing through ERCOT's interconnection process against MORE THAN 474 GW of pending requests, ~90% DATA CENTER-RELATED, with major developers indicating they will comply. Virginia's July 31 Rider T1 order moves Dominion toward 'cost causer pays' for direct-connect transmission."**
- **The three drivers of community opposition, per MS's expert sessions: (1) affordability — that data-center consumption raises consumer bills; (2) environmental — especially water use and air pollution; (3) quality of life — noise, dust, traffic.** 🔴 **The framing worth stealing: "opposition has become a DEVELOPMENT CONSTRAINT rather than a communications problem… political risk now should be underwritten like land, power or labor," with the key distinction between concerns that can be ENGINEERED AWAY (water, farmland, emissions) and societal objections that cannot. "Opposition scales NON-LINEARLY with project size and the earliest signals appear in municipal minutes and local social media well before litigation."** MS is constructive on brownfield siting and on relocating location-flexible training/batch workloads to colder, less populated jurisdictions.
- **Timing read: "the Texas audit reads as a TIMING issue rather than a DEMAND issue. ERCOT has filed a Request for Good Cause Exception on Batch Zero deadlines while preserving the April 9, 2027 study date; we view the pause as likely 1-3 MONTHS for higher-quality projects."** ⚠️ **Note the drift on this page: the Aug 11 entry logged ERCOT slipping from "1-3 months" to "several months"; MS is now back at 1-3 months for higher-quality projects. Both are estimates, neither is ERCOT's.** BofA's Ross Fowler reads it the same way — "the review is intended to weed out speculative projects rather than restrict demand growth" — and names **the 20 August ERCOT meeting** as the checkpoint for an updated Batch Zero restart timeline (BofA, 2026-08-17).
- **Who it helps and hurts, per MS:** softer 2027-28 ERCOT forwards are a marginal negative for **[[VST]], [[CEG]]** and NRG (though the IPPs are heavily hedged); prolonged uncertainty is the bigger risk to capex upside embedded in AEP and SRE plans; **additional grid uncertainty is bullish for on-site power (SEI, [[BE]], INIO)** and for off-grid generation generally (CIFR, WULF, GLXY, SEI, BE, [[GEV]], INIO, NEE, WMB, LBRT, EROC, CMI). **Batch Zero "should be a positive catalyst for powered shell providers"** — Base Load designation is effectively a green light to connect, Studied Load carries required upgrades and no guaranteed MW; **determinations land within weeks**. **PJM separately proposed allowing large loads to connect without matching generation capacity provided they accept curtailment during reliability events, excluding unsupported load from future capacity-market requirements; FERC approval requested by 12 October** (BofA · Fowler, 2026-08-17).

**3. The power path is being redesigned end to end — grid to chip.** From the **OCP APAC Summit** (FundaAI, 2026-08-17): **the industry is converging on 800VDC as the next standard, with SOLID-STATE TRANSFORMERS emerging as the endgame** — Delta framing SST as "replacing a transformer's copper-and-iron materials with semiconductors," a more fundamental shift than DC conversion alone. ⚠️ **No convergence yet on WHICH DC standard: Vertiv notes bipolar ±400V (lower ground-voltage stress, higher safety redundancy) and unipolar 800V (better cabling and conversion efficiency) "remain two coexisting, unresolved paths."** **Vertiv's four-stage efficiency ladder: in-rack 50VDC (~94%) → adding BBU/CBU (~96.5%) → 800VDC sidecar with DC/DC conversion (~96%) → 800VDC sidecar paired with native 800VDC servers (>96.5%).** At the package level, **power delivery is moving underneath the chip via vertical power delivery, driving higher-capacitance MLCC, smaller-form-factor SPS and a materials transition in inductors** — read-through to [[APH]], [[VRT]], [[NVT]], [[ETN]], [[POWI]], [[NVTS]] and [themes/800v-dc-power.md](800v-dc-power.md).
- **Facility-level standardisation is accelerating:** Google's 5th-gen Cooling Distribution Unit (**Project Deschutes**) went to spec 0.8 in 25Q3 and reaches 1.0 in 26Q2, co-developed with 10 suppliers, with the **Brazos** approach letting existing air-cooled facilities retrofit to liquid cooling **without new water infrastructure**; Google's separate **Open Data Center spec** shipped 0.5 in 26Q1 (incorporating its own 2027-28 TPU density expectations) with 1.0 targeted later in 2026 together with **AMD, Meta and Nvidia**. **[[FLEX]] demonstrated a self-built chain spanning modular data centers, racks, cooling and monitoring and can scale from hundred-MW-class modular builds to GW-class**; Foxconn argues for a four-plane architecture co-designing power and cooling together. **Amazon, Meta, Microsoft and Google are jointly driving a Data Center Innovation Initiative writing thermal, water, carbon-lifecycle and low-carbon-materials pillars into OCP specs.** ➜ **Supply-chain implication FundaAI draws: a retrofit purchasing wave for CDU/cold-plate suppliers once the 26Q2 spec ships; pressure on standalone UPS/PDU suppliers as rack power integrates into 800VDC sidecar/Power Center products; and vertically integrated builders like Flex compressing the role of traditional mechanical-electrical system integrators.** Adjacent: **Trane and [[ETN]] launched a joint power-and-cooling architecture aligned to NVDA's DSX AI Factory platform, claiming up to 15% better energy efficiency, 80% less copper and 30% lower installation cost** (@wallstengine, 2026-08-17); TrendForce sees **liquid-cooling penetration of AI chips rising from ~33% in 2025 to 53% in 2026 and ~60% in 2027** (@QQ_Timmy, 2026-08-17).

**4. Insurance is the quiet constraint nobody had priced.** **FT reports [[META]] and BlackRock's $14bn, 1 GW Sopaipilla data center in Texas carries only PARTIAL insurance — about $450mn of property coverage once operational and $645mn of terrorism coverage, plus construction-delay and liability protection. Rather than insuring against a complete loss, lenders are relying on modelled "probable maximum loss" scenarios, an approach becoming more common as projects grow into the tens of billions. The debt still received strong investment-grade ratings (A+ S&P, AA− Fitch)"** (@wallstengine citing FT, 2026-08-17). ⚠️ **Worth watching as a systemic item: PML-based underwriting on GW-class single sites is untested against a correlated loss, and it is now embedded in investment-grade paper.**

## Recent signals (Aug 16) — 🔴🔴 THE BOTTLENECK MOVES ONE LAYER DEEPER THAN THE TURBINE: **HOT-SECTION BLADES AND VANES**, THREE CASTERS WORLDWIDE, ALL BACKLOGGED — AND A ~10GW BUYER IS REPORTEDLY BUILDING ITS OWN FOUNDRY TO GET AROUND THEM

**Source: Morgan Stanley · Adam Jonas / William Tackett / Kristine Liwag / David Arcaro / Justin Lang / Jeremy Herring — "SpaceX: Blades & Vanes: SpaceX Tackles Time to Power" (2026-08-16). OW, PT $300, bull $600.** ⚠️ **The foundry thesis is reconstructed from FOUR SPACEX JOB POSTINGS plus an FTC early-termination notice — SpaceX "has not commented directly on any of the aforementioned reports." Treat the GW figures as MS's reconstruction.**

- 🔴 **THE CONSTRAINT, ONE LAYER BELOW WHERE THIS PAGE HAS BEEN LOOKING. This page has tracked gas turbines and gas ENGINES as the bottleneck (Aug 2) and shells/permits above them (Aug 13). MS puts the true chokepoint inside the machine: the HOT-SECTION BLADES AND VANES. Musk, quoted from the February Dwarkesh Patel / John Collison interview: "The turbines are SOLD OUT THROUGH 2030… The limiting factor is the vanes and blades. **THERE ARE ONLY THREE CASTING COMPANIES IN THE WORLD that make these, and they're MASSIVELY BACKLOGGED**."**
- **THE MARKET STRUCTURE, named: a handful of merchant casters — **Precision Castparts (private), Consolidated Precision Products (private), Howmet (HWM), Doncasters (DPC)** — plus in-house casting at some engine OEMs (though "a large % still sourced from casters"). **Blades-and-vanes EBITDA margins ~15-20%+** (per DPC, the cleanest public pure-play). **Pricing "has TRIPLED and OEMs are now recognizing MID-30% EBITDA MARGINS on new orders."**
- 🔴 **WHY THIS IS A STRUCTURAL CEILING AND NOT A PRICE PROBLEM — MS's own timeline for a new entrant: "from a greenfield commitment to first qualified production, the **MINIMUM TIMELINE IS TWO YEARS**, and full run-rate output typically takes **THREE OR MORE**." Single-crystal casting is "widely described as the most technologically demanding casting process in the world": a 40-50 step sequence with dozens of simultaneously controlled variables, alloys surviving **>3,000°F while spinning at 12,000-15,000 RPM under 40:1 pressure ratios**, **capital equipment lead times of UP TO THREE YEARS**, and NADCAP/OEM qualification locking suppliers in at the PART-NUMBER level.** ➔ **Any 2027-28 power plan that assumes new casting capacity arrives to relieve turbine lead times is assuming something that cannot physically be ordered in time. That is a harder constraint than the labor ceiling (Aug 12) and the permitting/audit constraint (Aug 14/15), because it cannot be relieved by capital OR by policy.**
- **THE BUYER'S RESPONSE, and it is the same move the industry made on behind-the-meter generation: VERTICAL INTEGRATION. MS reads four SpaceX job postings (Automation, Sr. Automation, Sr. Operations, Sr. Materials Engineer) for a greenfield "**BLADES AND VANES FOUNDRY" IN BASTROP, TEXAS**, producing equiaxed, directionally-solidified and single-crystal nickel-superalloy castings — dual-use across **datacenter gas turbines AND Raptor turbopumps** ("one foundry, two customers"). MS: "it may be **2-3+ YEARS BEFORE THE FIRST VOLUME PRODUCTION**."**
- **THE SIZE OF THE POSITION BEING DEFENDED: MS can identify **3-4 GW of likely accessible gas turbine power by YE27** for [[SPCX]] "through a mix of purchase agreements and Musk's personal acquisition of mobile-power provider APR Energy," with **total reported commitments closer to 8 GW under any timeline**; ~**$2.8BN OF S-1 TURBINE COMMITMENTS** ($805mn purchase agreement through 2029 + a ~$2.0bn mobile gas turbine "Turbine Acquisition" ≈ APR Energy, >1.1 GW mobile fleet, FTC notice dated 2026-05-14 naming Musk personally as buyer). Reported-but-unconfirmed suppliers: **Doosan Enerbility, Caterpillar Solar Turbines via Solaris (SEI), FTAI, APR Energy.**
- 🔴 **THE NUMBER THAT MATTERS FOR THIS PAGE'S GW LEDGER: MS models **5 GW BY YE27 FOR SPACEXAI vs MUSK'S 10 GW**, and says the gap is POWER, not chips — "after the exclusivity agreement with NVIDIA, we would think chip supply for SpaceX is mostly secured." Terrestrial compute is modelled to peak at **~15 GW IN 2031**.** ⚠️ **Do not conflate with the unsourced "SPCX 15-20 GW by YE27" mark logged on the [[SPCX]] page on 08-14 — different years, different basis.**
- ⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE (recurring hazard on this page): "each GW of SpaceXAI compute represents an opportunity of **upwards of $50bn in ANNUAL REVENUE**" is REVENUE per GW. It is NOT a build cost and must not be netted against the $50-60bn/GW BUILD-COST anchor from BlackRock logged on Aug 11.**
- **WOULD A NEW ENTRANT RELIEVE THE MARKET? MS says no: "we would NOT expect SpaceX to be a material supplier of blades & vanes for supply OUTSIDE OF SPACEX USE." So the casting bottleneck stays intact for everyone else even if Bastrop works.**

**Adjacent, same window — the China side of the same demand curve (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan et al, "GDS Holdings: Revised up 2026 booking target to 1GW", 2026-08-16; GDS NOT RATED, off-coverage — carried here as a read-through only):**
- **GDS **RAISED ITS FY26 BOOKING TARGET TO 1 GW, FROM PRIOR GUIDANCE OF 500 MW** — a doubling — driven by its three largest hyperscale customers (**77% of 1H26 bookings from the top 3**) plus emerging AI customers. **600 MW of reservations secured YTD**, expecting **1 GW+ of new reservations**, on a **100% exercise rate over the past 1-1.5 years**. **1H backlog 757 MW**, to **1 GW+ by end-2026**. Capex guidance raised to **RMB10bn from RMB9bn**.
- **THE UNIT ECONOMIC, from management: "each MW can generate ~**RMB2.2MN of EBITDA ANNUALLY**."** ⚠️ **EBITDA per MW, not revenue and not capex — a third basis to keep separate from the two above.**
- **Workload mix: move-ins currently **~50/50 CPU-based vs GPU-based**, with GPU share "likely to be slightly higher in 2027 as domestic GPU supply catches up"; **2027 move-ins to MORE THAN DOUBLE vs 2026**, weighted to 2H27, with another step-up in 2028.** ➔ **The read-through worth holding: a Chinese operator doubling its booking target on a DOMESTIC GPU ramp is an independent signal that China AI-infra demand is power-constrained on the same axis as the US — and that the 50% CPU share of move-ins is larger than the AI-only narrative implies.**


## Recent signals (Aug 14/15) — 🔴🔴 THE POLITICAL CONSTRAINT GRADUATES FROM ANECDOTE TO STRUCTURE: TEXAS HALTED DATA-CENTER PROJECTS PENDING AUDITS, AND THE READ IS THAT PUSHBACK IS NOW SYSTEMIC RATHER THAN LOCAL — WITH BEHIND-THE-METER GENERATION AS THE INDUSTRY'S ANSWER

**Source ①: Morgan Stanley Tech Talk — Stephen Byrd (Head of Thematic Research) with Tom Wigg, Mark Carlucci, Duke Chiolis. ⚠️ THE PODCAST IS DATED 2026-08-07; the Capstone desk summary that carries it is dated 2026-08-14. Per the standing relay rule, the DATE OF THE VIEW IS 08-07 — it predates the 08-13 items above and should not be read as newer than them.** ⚠️ **Summary is Capstone's own; no rating, PT or estimate captured.**
- 🔴 **THE CORE CLAIM AND WHY IT IS DIFFERENT FROM PRIOR NIMBY ITEMS: "The halting of data center projects in TEXAS, a traditionally PRO-BUSINESS REPUBLICAN STATE, signals a rising and widespread risk of political/regulatory pushback across the US."** Governor Abbott's letter requests assurance projects *"don't have detrimental impacts and are thoughtfully developed."* The generalisation: *"This backlash is NOT LIMITED TO TEXAS, with **VIRGINIA now regulating EVERY project** and the **Republican candidate for Governor of OHIO calling for a full review**."* ➜ **The market's prior was "red states will be fine." Byrd's point is that the prior is broken — and the mechanism by which it bites is not cost but PERMISSION.** Why it matters upstream: *"potentially hindering the ability to 'FIND A HOME FOR ALL THE CHIPS, ALL THE EQUIPMENT'… shifting focus from DEMAND to INFRASTRUCTURE READINESS."*
- 🔴 **THE ERCOT QUEUE, WITH NUMBERS — this is the most useful part because it separates the real pipeline from the noise:** ERCOT received *"hundreds and hundreds of gigawatts"* of proposals against **Texas peak demand of only ~80 GW**. **Batch Zero: over 200 GW proposed. Inside it, "BASELOAD" ≈ >60 GW — projects "already moving forward," financed, spending, awaiting final connection approval, and therefore higher-probability. Batch One: a "massive amount," "quite low probability of getting connected to the grid anytime soon."** ⚠️ **CRITICAL FRAMING DISCIPLINE, and it is the same error this wiki keeps flagging on GW: >200 GW "proposed" and >60 GW "baseload" are INTERCONNECTION-QUEUE figures, not build plans and not IT load. They must NOT be compared with, added to, or netted against the facility-GW / IT-load-GW / 800V-subset-GW bases in `_meta/assumptions.md`. A queue number is a request, not a project.** Timing: *"near-term catalyst dates are **August 14th and 20th** for regulatory hearings; however, **full resolution for baseload projects may take until NOVEMBER/DECEMBER**."*
- 🔴 **THE INDUSTRY'S ANSWER IS BEHIND-THE-METER, AND BYRD FRAMES IT AS A DELIBERATE RISK TRANSFER RATHER THAN A COST DECISION:** the tech community *"increasingly likes the idea of going off grid"* because it *"REDUCES THEIR EXECUTION RISK"* and lets them *"show definitively that they're NOT IMPACTING OTHER POWER CUSTOMERS."* **Abbott has applauded data centers "bringing their own power."** Constraint named: demand for these services *"likely EXCEEDS THE AVAILABLE SUPPLY [of] SKILLED LABOR"* — ➜ **which is the same binding constraint as the 35 GW/yr labour ceiling logged on Aug 12 below. Two independent routes to the same wall.**
- **Geography is re-sorting:** *"a general shift away from data center development in **PJM**"* (Virginia, Ohio, New Jersey resistance plus PJM's own structural issues) with *"the center of gravity moving towards the **DEEP SOUTH and the UPPER MIDWEST**,"* where execution risk is *"at least lower."*
- **The valuation observation, which is Byrd's actual pitch and should be read as such:** public equities are depressed while *"the ASSET MARKET shows 'remarkably good deals,'"* citing an **unlevered cash-flow yield "above 50%"** on one transaction and **17 PSP transactions with "quite excellent" economics.** *"This is the classic time to buy when you see technical weakness and yet the fundamentals are just absolutely excellent."* ⚠️ **A >50% unlevered CF yield is an extraordinary claim on a single deal with no terms given; NOT ADOPTED. The named beneficiaries (Solaris, Blue Energy, Williams, Cipher/CIFR, Hut 8/HOD, Riot/RIOT, Vistra) are all OFF-COVERAGE — no pages created, per the whitelist rule.**

**Source ②: Jefferies Energy/Power Sales · James Snyder — a two-sided BTM debate, written up as bull vs bear rather than as a house view (2026-08-14). ⚠️ Sales & trading commentary, explicitly not Jefferies Research.**
- **BULL side, the parts that are checkable:** *"Governor Abbott FURTHERED CONFIDENCE in the BTM theme. The audit process is focused on TRANSMISSION CONNECTIVITY / FTM"* — ➜ **i.e. the Texas action is read as targeting GRID-CONNECTED projects specifically, which mechanically advantages behind-the-meter. "TX could be 30-50% of data center adds."** *"Hyperscalers want to be electrified in '27/'28 and BTM is really the best way to achieve this. **They are LONG GPUs and SHORT POWER.**"* And the sizing: *"**Are we going to get to 25 GW in 2027… don't think so… likely FAR BELOW that**, but BTM will allow hyperscalers to get closer"*; *"**DOUBLE-DIGIT GWs of chips that are looking for a home.**"* ⚠️ **"Double-digit GW of chips looking for a home" is an unsourced desk assertion and is exactly the kind of GW figure that gets mis-based. Logged verbatim, NOT adopted, and NOT reconciled against the ERCOT queue numbers above — they measure different things.**
- **BEAR side, and it is unusually specific for a sales note:** *"Not seeing many contracts coming out of the BTM players"* · *"BTM is not [a] total panacea — a lot of EMISSION issues, HIGH CARBON, WATER USAGE questions, community engagement"* · *"Still have NIMBY issues with BTM"* · *"WORKING CAPITAL issues"* · *"NO EXPERIENCE in running very complex / islanded sites"* · *"**Bridge solution and will eventually be [an] EQUIPMENT GRAVEYARD**"* · *"Huge capex spends WITHOUT ANY CONTRACT ANNOUNCEMENTS"* · *"These BTM companies are OIL SERVICES COMPANIES that have huge volatility… hard to lever up given their base business"* · 🔴 *"**2027 power capacity ISN'T AS IMPORTANT as some think as it will take 2-3 YEARS TO GET DATA CENTERS BUILT, so [the] need for power is more 2028**"* · *"Worried about PEAK ORDERS — **OEMS GET HIT FIRST**."* ➜ **That second-to-last point is the sharpest thing in either source: if the shell/build cycle is 2-3 years, then 2027 power availability is not the binding constraint the bulls say it is, and the whole speed-to-power premium compresses. It is directly testable against the contract-announcement cadence the bulls promise for 2H26.**
- **Company-specific from the same debate, both flagged as unverified:** *"**ETR looking to increase [[META]] mega site in LA to up to 13 GW**"* and *"**[[SPCX]] — 15-20 GW by YE27**."* ⚠️ **Both unsourced; the SPCX figure sits ABOVE the company's own 10GW claim, which Susquehanna has already haircut to ~6GW. NOT ADOPTED — see [../SPCX.md](../SPCX.md) and [../META.md](../META.md).** Also: *"Even the UTILITIES are looking at some BTM strategies right now"* and *"[[BE]]: if BTM works, BE works… it is [the] tip of [the] spear. Speed to power and better emissions profile… **Recips win over aeros that require water**."*

**Source ③: the affordability framing, which is the political mechanism underneath all of the above** — BofA's Ross Fowler hosted Kate Jeffers and Tom Brookes of Milltown Partners on data-center opposition: *"The real pushback? **HOUSEHOLD ENERGY BILLS. AFFORDABILITY is the top objection and DISTRUST OF AI FUELS IT.** The winning move: **data centers pay their own infrastructure costs, with MEASURABLE BILL PROTECTIONS for existing customers**"* (BofA Sales · Nicholas Villani relaying Ross Fowler, 2026-08-14 — *not* Research). ➜ **This is the causal link the Byrd note asserts but does not explain: the objection is not carbon and not land, it is retail electricity bills. That makes behind-the-meter not merely faster but POLITICALLY LOAD-BEARING — a BTM project by construction cannot raise anyone else's bill. It also predicts the shape of the eventual regulation: cost-allocation rules, not build bans.** Independent corroboration from the tape: *"**AI data center bans surge past 500 nationwide** as local US politicians begin blocking new developments"* (Tom's Hardware, in Susquehanna's weekly link digest, 2026-08-15 — ⚠️ **aggregator headline, not read, not verified**).

## Recent signals (Aug 13) — 🔴🔴 THE CONSTRAINT, FROM THE BUYER ITSELF AND ON THE RECORD: A **POWERED-UP DATA CENTER SHELL** — AND MANAGEMENT EXPLICITLY RULES OUT BOTH GPUs *AND* ENERGY AVAILABILITY AS THE BINDING ITEM

> **Source: (Microsoft IR call — Mary, 2026-08-13), a Capstone-internal recap of a post-Q2 Investor Relations call. FIRST-PARTY MANAGEMENT COMMENTARY — NOT a broker note, and it must never be attributed to a bank.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_13_ir_msft_post_2q.html)

- 🔴🔴 **THE MOST IMPORTANT FIRST-PARTY DATAPOINT THIS PAGE HAS ON WHAT ACTUALLY GATES THE BUILD, and it is more precise than the framing this page has been carrying: *"from a capacity constraint perspective, **it's no one individual component. And so it's not that we can't get servers**… But broadly, the way that you hear us talk about it, is it's the idea of a **powered up data center shell**. And so **it's not that we can't get the energy**. It's the fact that it just takes time to **acquire the land, get the permit, build a shell, put in all of the equipment so that then you can plug in a server**… And so **we don't have enough data center shells that are just built up, ready to go for us to plug the servers into for all the demand that we have**."*** ⚠️⚠️ **READ THE SECOND NEGATION CAREFULLY — it is the part that refines this page. Management is NOT saying electricity is unavailable. It is saying the binding item is the SEQUENCED TIME to convert land + permit + shell + equipment into a rack position. That is a CONSTRUCTION-AND-PERMITTING constraint expressed in months, not a generation or interconnect constraint expressed in MW — which is consistent with, and arguably the demand-side confirmation of, the labor-ceiling and permitting-timeline items in the 08-12 and 08-10 PM blocks below, and a caution against reading every "power constraint" headline as a generation shortage.** Note also the granularity: *"Any individual region, capacity is very **hyper local**, right? Can maybe have its own unique constraints."* (Microsoft IR call — Mary, 2026-08-13)
- 🔴 **THE DURATION, AND MANAGEMENT PRE-EMPTIVELY DISCOUNTS ITS OWN GUIDE: *"We expect to be constrained **at least through the end of the calendar year**. I say the word **at least**, because **every time we've given you an end date, we've been wrong**. Because demand continues to en flux, **it's not a statement on supply**."*** ➜ **The last clause is the one to carry: MSFT is attributing the repeated slippage of the constraint end-date to DEMAND running ahead, not to supply disappointing. For this page that is the cleanest available first-party statement that the shortage is being extended from the top of the funnel — and it makes "constrained through year-end" a floor rather than a forecast.** (Microsoft IR call — Mary, 2026-08-13)
- **THE CAPACITY LADDER, IN GW, WITH ITS DISCLOSURE LIMIT STATED: *"yes, we added **two gigawatts in FY25**. We've told you **the last three quarters, we've added about a gigawatt** [each]. We didn't say in Q1, but **fair to think upwards of three added in this year**. You can make a Q1 assumption."* On the two-year plan: *"What we told you at the beginning of FY26, is over the next two years, **we would double the size of our footprint. So we're halfway through that, remain on track** to do that."*** ⚠️⚠️ **THE LIMIT, VOLUNTEERED BY IR IN THE SAME BREATH AND THE REASON THESE MUST BE TREATED AS ADDS ONLY: *"**We haven't told you exactly like what the size of the footprint was before**."* Without a base, the doubling is NOT independently checkable and NO total MSFT footprint may be derived from these adds — do not back into one. ⚠️ Second limit: *"**Capacity doesn't necessarily come online linearly**… it can depend on when large sites come online, if you have lease sites get delivered, when in the quarter."* So the ~1GW/quarter run-rate is not a schedule.** On execution: *"**I wouldn't say that we're delayed. We're on track to double the size of our fleet.** And so if anything, we've even talked about like **reducing dock to live times, bringing capacity online earlier**."* (Microsoft IR call — Mary, 2026-08-13)
- 🔴 **WHY [[MSFT]] LEASES AND WHAT IT THINKS OF THE NEOCLOUDS AND POWERED-SHELL DEVELOPERS — the most commercially useful passage on the call, because it prices their moat at zero and their speed at a premium: *"**In an ideal world, we would build own and operate our own data centers**. We would start from scratch… **you get the best TCO**… We don't live in an ideal world… So that's where you see us do things like **augment with leases**. A lot of times, it looks like a data center lease. **You already did the work of acquiring the land, building the building, we'll go do the rest.** But sometimes it even looks like more like a **neo cloud lease**, where you have capacity ready to go in a region where we have a lot of demand and just can't get in there fast enough. **If we can strike a good economic deal**, you've seen us do that as well."* And the selection criterion, verbatim: *"a lot of it is **hyperlocal**… where do we see demand, **latency really matters**… if there's somebody that has the capacity there and we can strike a good economic deal, like that's what we'll do… **It's not because we think any of these providers can do anything that we can't.** It's really about just **how quickly can we bring more capacity on and in the right places**, while also just ensuring that we're striking good deals."*** ➜ **The buy-side read for [[CRWV]], [[NBIS]] and the powered-shell developers: their customer describes them as a SPEED-AND-LOCATION arbitrage on a time constraint that MSFT expects to relieve — explicitly NOT a capability gap and explicitly second-best on TCO. That is a direct, first-party cap on the structural-moat version of the neocloud thesis, and it dates the arbitrage to the life of the shell shortage. It also tells you what kills the trade: not a demand air pocket, but MSFT's own shells arriving.** ⚠️ **The questioner named counterparties, but the machine transcript garbles them ("Corwave", "Iron", "Cru", "Compass") — NOT attributed here; only Mary's framing is carried.** (Microsoft IR call — Mary, 2026-08-13)
- **REVENUE PER GW — LOGGED AS AN UNCONFIRMED QUESTIONER'S PREMISE, NOT AS A DATAPOINT. A participant put it to IR that *"recent deals that we have with Google and SpaceX and [An]tropic [are] around **30 billion to 50 billion revenue per gigawatt**"* and asked whether that is the economics Azure would exchange at. ⚠️⚠️ **MARY DID NOT CONFIRM, ENDORSE OR CORRECT THE NUMBER** — she redirected to pricing mechanics and efficiency. **The $30-50bn/GW figure is therefore a SELL-SIDE/BUY-SIDE PREMISE from the floor of the call and must not be carried as a Microsoft disclosure.** ⚠️ **Basis flag: it is also not on the same basis as the two rev/GW marks this wiki already holds — MS's ~$22.9bn/GW for hyperscaler IaaS on 1GW of GB300 (07-27, [tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md)) and SemiAnalysis's ~$35bn/GW for [[GOOG]] GROSS-BASIS TPU SYSTEM SALES to external SPVs (08-07, [hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md)). Three different objects; do not average them.** (Microsoft IR call — Mary, 2026-08-13)
- **WHAT MANAGEMENT SAYS RAISES REVENUE PER GW INSTEAD — the efficiency ladder, and it is the mechanism behind the claim that cloud revenue outpaces cloud capex: *"If you think about the efficiency of a gigawatt or of the fleet, you can almost think of it as **the capacity today is as inefficient as it will ever be**, because the work we're going to do to drive efficiencies… **you'll see silicon get more efficient, you'll see models get more efficient, you'll see the routing mechanism for models get more efficient, software, things like token throughput**… Even operationally, like **how fast you can plug in a GPU**, all of that will drive improvement in just **how much capacity or productivity you can drive out of the fleet** that we have… it's all those things together that give us the confidence to say that, hey, **in the long term, cloud revenue will outpace cloud capex**."*** Alongside it, the pricing levers: *"every new deal that we sign, we're thinking about **discounting**… do you have to have the same discount rate?"* and *"**every new SKU that comes on is priced with the current pricing environment in mind** — so think about new SKUs as having **some of an effective price increase**."* ⚠️ **Contract structure limits how fast either lever bites: large contracts use **"tranche delivery"** — *"it's not like you say at the beginning of a multi-year period, here's everything and this is the agreed upon price for the entirety of the contract… you go through that same exercise again. And when you do that, **you price for the current environment**"* — so re-pricing happens per tranche, not per contract.** ➜ **For this page: a GW is not a fixed unit of output. MSFT is explicitly underwriting improving output-per-GW, which means GW-based capacity forecasts systematically understate delivered compute — the same direction of error as the facility-GW vs IT-load-GW trap this wiki already tracks, but from a different cause.** (Microsoft IR call — Mary, 2026-08-13)

## Recent signals (Aug 12) — 🔴 THE BUILD GETS A **LABOR CEILING (35 GW/YR BY 2030)**, AND IT IS THE FIRST CONSTRAINT ON THIS PAGE THAT CHIPS, TURBINES AND TRANSFORMERS CANNOT RELIEVE

### Companion items from the same run — the operators' own power ladders, a US-vs-Asia build-time gap, and the first LOCAL-POLITICS constraint

- 🔴 **[[NBIS]] RAISES YEAR-END-2026 CONTRACTED POWER TO 5 GW (from ">4 GW"), and the full disclosed escalation is now a clean series: >1 GW (Aug'25) → >2.5 GW (Nov'25) → >3 GW (Feb'26) → >4 GW (May'26) → 5 GW (Aug'26)** — with *"almost all of that power coming online over the next three, maybe 2 or 3.5 years"* on a mix of grid and behind-the-meter supply, and a **first per-year deployment guide of ">1 GW PER YEAR, starting in 2027."* ⚠️ **CONTRACTED-power basis — not active, not IT-load. Do not mix with the active figures below (see the standing GW-basis rule in `_meta/assumptions.md`).** (Nebius Group Q2 2026 Letter to Shareholders + call, 2026-08-12) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/SHLQ226_1.html)
- **[[CRWV]]: 1.5 GW ACTIVE (+~500 MW in Q2, of which >300 MW in June alone — *"June itself larger than any full quarter in our history"*); contracted power 4.2 GW, EXCLUDING *">1.5 GW of further potential power from powered land, expansion options and executed LOIs"*** — management totals *"close to about 6 gigawatts already… and it's middle of 2026"* against ≥8 GW by 2030. 🔴 **NET-NEW: >1 GW contracted OUTSIDE the US, including APAC entry with 360 MW IN INDONESIA coming online in ~18 months.** (CoreWeave Q2'26 earnings call · Michael Intrator / Nitin Agrawal, 2026-08-11)
- **A useful BOUNDARY for the theme, from the other end of the scale ladder: [[AKAM]]'s CEO says the company is deliberately NOT in the gigawatt game** — *"we're not out there buying or building gigawatt data centers… some of the larger data centers we have will be **tens of megawatts**"* — used for enterprise-grade training and inference, all **third-party leased**, with no self-build planned *"for the foreseeable future."* ➜ **Edge/enterprise inference does not consume the same power envelope as frontier training, and this page should not aggregate them.** (Akamai CEO Dr. Tom Leighton, JPM-hosted call, 2026-08-10)
- 🔴 **US BUILD TIMES HAVE ROUGHLY DOUBLED VERSUS ASIA — an integrator's view, and it corroborates the labor ceiling from a different direction:** *"in Asia, you could get a building up and running in about **12 months**… because of the expansions that are happening in the United States, which can take longer than a year. It could take **a year and a half. It can even take two years** sometimes to fully build something out and **having the grid operator in line with you**."* Combined with power and component lead times, customer forecasting windows have pushed out to **2028 and in some cases 2029** — *"the visibility window today is longer than it has been in recent memory."* (Celestica management, UBS-hosted call, 2026-08-12) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_12_cls_follow_up_call_with_ubs.html)
- ⚠️ **THE FIRST LOCAL-POLITICS CONSTRAINT ON THIS PAGE, and an expert names it as the next big story ahead of overspending:** *"**data center construction, not overspending**… people are **revolting** against companies who want to build data centers in towns in the United States… if people are pushing back, **politicians have to give into that**. I know for a fact that **New Jersey** was planning to build two data [centers] and the local county said no — **that entire project got cancelled**."* And the consequence for the demand chain: *"they can buy all the hardware they want, but **if they can't implement it, there's no point buying anything**."* ⚠️ **EXPERT VIEW (StoneX-hosted call, former NVIDIA employee), single anecdote, not verified.** (2026-07-30)
- **The operators' own read on that risk, and they disagree with the expert.** [[CRWV]]'s Intrator claims no impact — *"none of those numbers will be impacted by the regulatory pushback as of today"* — framing moratoriums as changing *"where this infrastructure gets built,"* not demand. Against that, **[[NBIS]]'s Vineland NJ expansion hearing resumes 2026-08-17**; management says the amendment is only a site-layout change caused by switching the power source to **Bloom Energy fuel cells**, that the building shell *"finished earlier this summer,"* and that *"no significant impact [is] expected on the project timeline."* ➜ **An assertion, not an outcome — and 08-17 is a dated, checkable test of exactly the claim the expert makes. Note the two neoclouds' position is "relocation, not destruction," which is testable against build-time data rather than against sentiment.** (CoreWeave 2026-08-11; Nebius 2026-08-12)

**🔴 THE CONSTRAINT NOBODY HAD QUANTIFIED: MEP CRAFT LABOR.** This page's constraint debate has run through power, turbines, transformers, gas and chips. Bernstein adds the one that has been missing: **mechanical/electrical/plumbing craft labor *"sets the speed limit of US data center construction."*** (Bernstein · Chad Dillard / Miguel Marques / Madison Rezaei / Varun Govindaraj / Alasdair Leslie, "The Data Center Labor Ceiling: How much capacity can MEP labor support by 2030?", 2026-08-12) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_259326.html)
- **The ceiling, three ways.** Base case (2022-25 peak recruitment rates persist) = **+6 GW/yr of incremental adds, 12 GW/yr in 2026 → ~35 GW/yr by 2030, ~31% CAGR** — *"enough to support the consensus view of 25-35 GW/year."* On the **20-year-average** recruitment rate: **~25 GW (~20% CAGR)**. Theoretical maximum if **all** US MEP labor were redeployed to data centres: **85 GW/yr**. **Mechanical is the binding trade** — 35 GW, against 49 GW electrical and 149 GW plumbing.
- **The unit economics, which is what makes it checkable:** **0.9 MEP workers/MW** (0.3 M / 0.5 E / 0.1 P) · **1,772 man-hours/MW** (665 M / 843 E / 264 P) · **$15.0mn/MW total MEP cost** (labor + materials), **~50% labor ≈ $7.5mn/MW**. A 100 MW build = **30 mechanical + 48 electrical + 10 plumbing ≈ 88-90 workers**.
- **The supply funnel, and the geography is the sharper half:** **1.75m headline → 1.37m construction → 789k non-residential** (194k M / 397k E / 198k P). **Only ~30% of that labor sits where ~70% of the 359.5 GW tracked project pipeline is** — **TX alone is 93.8 GW of pipeline on ~9% of the labor**; FL and CA are the inverse. MEP as a share of the US labor pool is at a **20-year high**, recruitment has **plateaued since the 2023 peak**, and *"only craft labor can make craft labor — training is the true bottleneck."*
- **🔴 THE FOUR CEILINGS DO NOT AGREE, AND THAT IS THE TRADE.** Bernstein stacks them for 2030: **consensus US DC capacity adds 25-35 GW · MEP craft-labor ceiling 35 GW · mid-scale power gen (<120 MW/generator) implied need ~40 GW · global GPU manufacturing (new build + retrofits) ~70 GW.** ➜ **Read as: chips are NOT the binding constraint, labor is — and the power-gen complex is provisioning for a build the trades cannot staff.** Bernstein's named casualty is the mid-scale gen industry, *"at risk of overbuild, which would impact **CAT and CMI** the most."*
- **The stock conclusion is a share-shift argument, not a volume one:** *"**the only way to break through the labor ceiling is modularization**. This technology shift not only unlocks more data center construction capacity, but it also **shifts the profit pool from field to factory floor**, which benefits the most vertically integrated OEMs and contractors like **[[ETN]], SU (Schneider), [[VRT]], [[PWR]]**, LGN, FIX and EME."* ➜ **This is the first argument on this page that still works if DC capex growth decelerates.**
- ⚠️ **CROSS-SOURCE CONFLICT, UNRESOLVED, AND IT GOES TO THE HEADLINE NUMBER: Bernstein's 1,772 man-hours/MW and ~0.9 workers/MW are ~7x below SemiAnalysis's ~12,000 field man-hours/MW and ~300 craft at peak on a 50 MW hall (~6/MW) ("The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters", 2026-07-29). Different bases — full-project average across shell+TFO vs peak-concurrency MEP fit-out field hours — explain part of the gap, not a factor of seven. Bernstein's entire 35 GW ceiling is derived from its workers/MW, so if SemiAnalysis's intensity is closer to right, the ceiling is materially too generous and the note's central conclusion inverts.** Both retained; not netted.
- **Corroboration already on this page:** BofA (2026-07-24) found only **30-50% of 158 GW** of announced BTM projects get built; Jefferies (2026-08-02) that *"craft labor remains constrained."* ⚠️ **Source-quality caveat: the note's own prose and exhibits disagree on the recruitment inputs (base case variously "2020-25"/"2022-25"/"2023-25"; headline "~15K mechanical" vs the model's 12,440). Per the house rule, the EXHIBIT values are the ones carried here.**

## Recent signals (Aug 11) — 🔴 THE $500BN GETS ITS MECHANICS (**RESIDUAL-VALUE SUPPORT ON UP TO 25%**, NOT CREDIT), BLACKROCK PUTS THE COST AT **$50–60BN PER GW** AND THE US NEED AT **>70GW**, AND ERCOT's AUDIT SLIPS FROM "1–3 MONTHS" TO "**SEVERAL MONTHS**"

> **Duplication note: the $500bn announcement itself and the six partners were ingested on 08-10 PM below. What follows is net-new — the STRUCTURE, the per-GW cost math, the credit-market reaction, and the Texas interconnection timeline.**

**🔴 (1) THE STRUCTURE, DISCLOSED — AND IT IS RESIDUAL-VALUE SUPPORT, NOT LOAN GUARANTEES (Morgan Stanley / Joseph Moore, 2026-08-11).** The partners *"will evaluate individual opportunities on their merits, so **no current decisions have been made thus far**"*; [[NVDA]] provides ecosystem support and *"may provide a **'residual support mechanism' for up to 25% of the investments**, which in their words is **'limited, residual value based, and designed to complement — not replace — independent underwriting'**."*
