[[OPENAI]]'s initial $100bn and ~$250bn guarantee) at **~15% of ~$470bn of NVDA CY26-27 FCF** and frames the platform as *"**NVDA underwrites the value, not the loan**… the burden sits with the consortium, not NVDA's balance sheet"* (BofA / Vivek Arya, Buy, PO $320).
- **🔴 THE PER-DEAL TEST TO APPLY GOING FORWARD, in Moore's words: "we would prefer a backstop which involves GPU USAGE rather than pure credit backing… judging 25%, and passivity, WILL BE ASSESSED ONE DEAL AT A TIME."** ➜ **For this page that is the single most useful forward test: each announced platform deal should be classified as a usage backstop (cheap for NVDA, since it wants neocloud GPUs for its own open-source model work) or a credit guarantee (a real liability). Watch the 10-Q lease-guarantee line — $3.5bn today — as the running scoreboard.**
- **The upside NVDA gets in exchange, unmodelled anywhere: "a 35% revenue share could mean >$10bn per GW/yr of 100% margin revenue to Nvidia," worth 10%+ to FY29 EPS.**

**🔴 (2) BLACKROCK PUTS HARD NUMBERS ON THE BUILD, AND THEY ARE THE MOST USEFUL SIZING INPUTS THIS PAGE HAS HAD FROM A CAPITAL PROVIDER (Larry Fink on CNBC, via Jefferies / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-10 PM).**
- *"**Each GW costs $50-60bn.** He said **the US alone will need over 70GW** to include all the needs around the world and the budget required is going to be huge."*
- *"Over time this will require **trillions of dollars of investment, making compute an asset class**."*
- 🔴 *"**The financing of datacentres reminds Larry Fink of MORTGAGE BACKED SECURITIES IN THE 70s** — this is the next future for financial engineering, he says."*

- ⚠️ **THE CAVEAT THAT BOUNDS ALL OF THE ABOVE (Jefferies financials specialist Adam Panzer): "everyone agrees this $500B DOES NOT REPRESENT THE EQUITY CAPITAL INVESTMENT — unclear how much leverage though," with syndication and transaction fees a recurring theme in client conversations.** ➜ **Cross-check against this page's own 08-10 GW sizing (≈10-13 GW all-in on the MS $39bn/GW anchor). Fink's $50-60bn/GW is 28–54% HIGHER than that anchor, which would put $500bn at roughly 8-10 GW all-in — i.e. LESS than a single year of the 14-16 facility-GW/yr buildout. The two anchors should be reconciled; the direction of the discrepancy makes the $500bn look smaller relative to need, not larger.**

**⚠️🔴 (3) THE CREDIT MARKET DISAGREES WITH THE EQUITY MARKET, AND THAT IS NEW.** **NVDA's CDS widened +5bps to 77bps against CDX IG at 52bps** on the announcement, with the stock **-2.9%** (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-11); WSJ reports debt investors now demand **~0.40 points over Treasuries** to lend to NVIDIA itself. **Corroborating in the ASIC complex: BofA Credit downgraded [[AVGO]] to Marketweight from Overweight the same day, saying "XPV financing creates more complicated spread and RESIDUAL-VALUE risk."** ➜ **Two asset classes, opposite conclusions on the same structure: equity desks call residual-value support de-risking, credit desks price it as a liability. The same structure produced an equity re-rating and a credit downgrade.**

This is a regulatory-queue problem, not a construction problem — and it sits directly on the Texas assets in the news this week, including [[ANTHROPIC]]'s RIOT leases at Rockdale (191MW, 20yr, $9.1bn) and the 1GW Corsicana LOI.**
- **Corroborating scarcity pricing from the same complex: Bernstein's Gautam Chhugani (RIOT PT $35 from $30) says "pricing was significantly better than expectations" and that "APPROVED, FRONT-OF-THE-METER SITES ARE BECOMING INCREASINGLY SCARCE AMID GROWING POLITICAL SCRUTINY, strengthening Riot's pricing power, with the latest deal struck at the HIGH END of recent market pricing."** **Also: FRMI +22% on its first binding lease at Project Matador (Carson County, TX) — 222MW to TensorWave, ~$6.5bn of contracted revenue over an initial 15-year term; and EROC's Power Systems backlog up 10x y/y to ~$1.7bn, including 470MW of equipment for [[ANTHROPIC]].**

**(5) TWO SMALLER ITEMS WORTH LOGGING.** **[[OPENAI]] is reportedly hiring a POWER-TRADING LEAD to manage commodity hedging across its data-center power portfolio** (Bloomberg, via MS TMT desk, 2026-08-11) — ➜ **a buyer building a trading desk implies expected exposure to merchant power prices rather than all-fixed PPAs, which is a change in how this page should think about hyperscaler power cost.** And **BofA hosted an expert call on DATA CENTER OPPOSITION with Milltown Partners (Kate Jeffers, Tom Brookes) on their published report** (BofA, 2026-08-11) — the local-opposition risk this page named as the #1 risk on Aug 4 now has dedicated sell-side expert coverage.

- **⚠️ NOT DISCLOSED, AND THE PRECEDENT SAYS TO WAIT:** the split by partner, the tenor, what *"attractive rates"* means, and whether NVDA carries any first-loss. The Information's audit of the July SK announcement applies verbatim — *"NVDA will not say which company is putting up how much"* — so **treat $500bn as MOBILIZATION CAPACITY, not committed capital.** Cross-ref [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md), [../SPCX.md](../SPCX.md).
- **⚠️ WHAT $500BN BUYS IN GW — because this is the GW page, and the headline is smaller than it sounds.** On MS's **$39bn all-in capex/GW** (Nowak/Byrd/Wood, 2026-07-27: $23bn IT on a 5-yr life + $16bn non-IT on a 15-yr life), **$500bn ≈ 12.8 GW**; at the ledger's ~**$50bn/GW** NVDA-GPU-infra mark **≈10.0 GW**; on the compute leg alone (~$23bn/GW) **≈21.7 GW**. Set against **14-16 facility-GW built in 2026 / ~20 GW 2027** (Jefferies satellite tracking, 50% haircut) the *entire* pool is **on the order of ONE YEAR of the global buildout, spread over an unstated number of years**, and **~9-10% of the cumulative 2026-30 program** (106 GW × ~$50bn ≈ $5.3T; JPM's 138 GW-through-2030 lands the same). ⚠️ **Directional, not precise — three forks:** the $/GW basis swings it **2.2x**; if the $500bn is *fund equity* later levered at infra/ABS LTVs, purchasing power is 2-3x higher; and **MS's GW is IT-LOAD while Jefferies's is FACILITY (~1.25x PUE gap) — do not merge the two bases** (`ai-dc-power-gw`, [../_meta/assumptions.md](../_meta/assumptions.md)). 🔴 **The conclusion that matters for this page: financing was never the binding constraint — power was, and still is (MS · Byrd: 38 GW US shortfall through 2028, up to 122 GW through 2030). More capital does not produce more GW on a 2027 horizon; it changes WHO OWNS the GW and at what cost of capital.** Which is exactly why item (2) below — a permitting hearing on a named site — is the more informative datapoint of the two.

**🔴 (2) THE PERMITTING RISK THIS PAGE NAMED AS "#1" ON AUG 4 NOW HAS A NAMED SITE, A NAMED OPPONENT-PROCESS AND A DATE (BofA / Tal Liani on [[NBIS]], relayed 2026-08-10).** *"An expansion to the **Vineland, NJ** data center faces some **local opposition**. This revised design proposes it will be **primarily powered by [[BE]] fuel cells** (announced 2026-05-20); **hearing scheduled to resume on AUGUST 17th**."* ⚠️ **First instance on this wiki of data-center opposition attaching to a specific listed operator's specific site with a calendar date** — and note the mitigation *is* the behind-the-meter generation trade this page has tracked (fuel cells to bypass interconnect queues), which is now itself the subject of the hearing.

## Recent signals (Aug 5) — 🔴 A NAMED BUYER SAYS IT IS DELIBERATELY OVERBUILDING POWER AHEAD OF GPUs — 15-20 GW of power-and-cooling against ~10 GW of compute, and the reason is the cost ratio

**This is the highest-conviction demand statement this page has for the electrical/cooling layer specifically, and it came from a customer on an earnings call rather than from a vendor or a broker** (SpaceX Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Space_Exploration_Technologies_Corp_Earnings_Call_2026804_DN000000003115071217pdf.html).

- ⚠️⚠️ **READ THE GW BASES BEFORE READING THE NUMBERS — this single answer contains two different ones, and the whole point is that they diverge.** MS's Adam Jonas asked [[SPCX]] to confirm ~10 GW of **nameplate compute** by end-2027 and whether Musk had line of sight on *"permitting chips and turbines."* The answer moved to a different basis entirely: ***"we're actually aiming to FAR EXCEED that gigawatt number in terms of POWER ONLINE, POWER COOLING AND ELECTRICAL EQUIPMENT. So our tentative target is to actually have 20 GIGAWATTS AT THE POWER AND COOLING LEVEL ONLINE BY THE END OF NEXT YEAR. Now, I DON'T THINK WE'RE GOING TO ACHIEVE 20 GIGAWATTS, but we want to have a series of projects that cumulatively come to 20 gigawatts by the end of next year. Some of them won't pan out exactly on time, but I would expect that we still probably have AT THE POWER PLANT LEVEL, SOMETHING CLOSE TO 15 GIGAWATTS. So assuming that maybe a quarter of the projects take longer than expected."*** **Per `../_meta/assumptions.md`: ~10 GW is IT LOAD / nameplate compute; 15-20 GW is POWER-PLANT / power-and-cooling level. They are not comparable and must never be netted or averaged.**
- 🔴 **THE MECHANISM, and it is the genuinely new idea on this page: the overbuild is INTENTIONAL and the justification is relative cost.** ***"our goal is to have FAR MORE POWER COOLING AND ELECTRICAL EQUIPMENT THAN WE HAVE GPUs. That's like logical thing to do given the RELATIVE EXPENSE OF GPUs VERSUS BALANCE OF SYSTEM."*** ⚠️ **Unpack that: when the GPU is the expensive, supply-constrained, rapidly-depreciating asset, the rational move is to make sure a GPU never waits on a transformer. It implies a target ratio of roughly 1.5-2x power-and-cooling capacity to IT load at this account — i.e. every GW of compute demand this page tracks may be understating the electrical/cooling order book by 50-100%. If that behaviour generalises to other buyers facing the same allocation scarcity, every GW-based sizing on this page built off IT load is too low for the turbine/transformer/switchgear/genset/cooling complex.**
- ⚠️ **IT ALSO CUTS AGAINST THIS PAGE'S OWN BOTTLENECK FRAMING, and the tension should be held rather than resolved.** The Aug-2 and Aug-4 rows above have the bottleneck as gas engines and the risk as regulatory/time-to-power — Baker: *"if there's weakness, it's just because we can't energize the gigawatts fast enough."* **Musk is describing a buyer who has pre-solved exactly that by over-ordering: he did not answer the permitting question, he answered it by saying he has bought his way around it.** Both can be true — a well-capitalised buyer front-running the queue is entirely consistent with the queue being the binding constraint for everyone else, and it makes the scarcity WORSE for marginal developers. **Note the direct interaction with the Bernstein ERCOT-audit item on [[NVDA]] (08-04): Texas has paused the "Batch Zero" data-center interconnection review against a 474 GW request queue, which raises the value of already-approved MW — precisely the asset Musk says he is stockpiling.**
- **Scale check on credibility, because a 15-20 GW claim needs one: [[SPCX]] deployed 400 MW (Q2-25) → 1.0 GW (Q1-26) → 1.4 GW (Q2-26) of nameplate compute, guides >2 GW exiting 2026, and spent $18.4bn of capex in Q2 alone (of which $15.8bn on AI infrastructure), with Q3/Q4 guided "very similar." It ended Q2 with $100bn of cash.** ⚠️ **Counterweight, and it is material: FUNDA's channel checks found six independent supply-side sources converging on 3.5-5 GW against management's ~8-10 GW ambition (P(8 GW) ≈ 12-17%). Musk himself pre-discounted the 20 GW figure to ~15 GW in the same breath. Treat 15-20 GW as a PROCUREMENT INTENT — which is what matters for order books — not as a delivery forecast.**
- **Adjacent number from the same call, relevant to the returns debate on this page: Musk's guess at monetization is *"somewhere between $30 AND $50"* per watt for Rubin (= $30-50bn/GW), and CFO Bret Johnsen said new compute capital is achieving *"a LESS THAN ONE-YEAR PAYBACK"* with *"increasingly favorable economics with each agreement we sign."*** ⚠️ **Hedged as a guess, on the newest and most expensive silicon generation, and 2-3x MS's own modelled $16.5/W for this company — but if revenue per watt is anywhere near that, the incentive to overbuild the balance-of-system is arithmetically obvious rather than merely rhetorical.**

Even in some of these deep-red, super pro-growth states they're just like: you guys are not doing a good job telling your story."** ⚠️ File alongside the state-level sales-tax-exemption repeals (four states + nine considering, TX directive for 2027 — The Information, 08-02, on [hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md)): two independent political vectors in one week, same direction.

The reality is, given the deals being cut now" (the data-center pledge), "when a data center goes in, electricity prices actually generally go DOWN for everyone around there because of BEHIND-THE-METER DEALS"; developers now fund "a hospital, a school, a new police station and a fire station"; and the jobs persist (RMA + upgrades + refresh cycles) — "data centers are in a lot of ways THE BEST THING TO HAPPEN FOR BLUE-COLLAR WAGES IN MY LIFETIME."** The water myth traced to a single book's admitted **10,000x overestimate** of DC water usage, still propagating ("faster than truth gets out of bed" — the spinach/Popeye decimal error as the template). His proposed fix: an industry foundation/PAC running the story during the NFL/Final Four/World Series.
- **Supply-side color consistent with this page's gas-engine bottleneck rows: "if there's weakness, it's just because we can't energize the gigawatts fast enough. The gigawatts ARE going to get energized — regulatory policy is moving in a good way [federally], the turbine manufacturers, the diesel-gen manufacturers are ramping up, you're RIPPING TURBINES OFF OLD AIRPLANES and reconditioning and repurposing them. There's crazy things happening. Capitalism is very, very good at this."** — i.e., the physical constraint clears on capitalist timelines; the binding risk vector is state/local politics.

Got to have RIGHT OFFTAKE, RIGHT EQUIPMENT AT THE RIGHT TIME, E&C CONTRACT, GAS CONTRACTS, GAS PIPES, PERMITTING… 2027 TIMEFRAME FOR ANYTHING DOESN'T SEEM REAL… 2028 SEEMS LIKE A MORE LEGITIMATE TIMEFRAME FOR A LOT OF THESE SITES," and "LARGE COMPANIES ARE NOT FAST OR NIMBLE. Negotiations take a long time and constantly burning weeks at a time." On counterparties: "HYPERSCALERS HAVE BEEN FLAKY, WISHY WASHY, AND HAVE GHOSTED THEM A COUPLE OF TIMES. 60 days go by and then they come back… has happened to them but have also happened to much larger groups as well."** **This is the honest counterweight to every 2027-dated behind-the-meter forecast on this wiki — including Bloom's own "30 to 40 gigawatts of new AI data center capacity is going to be turned on in 2027" and Jefferies' expectation of "a MEANINGFUL NUMBER OF CONTRACT ANNOUNCEMENTS IN 2H26" because "hyperscalers highly focused on 2027/2028 electrification SO NEED TO SEE CONTRACTS SOON to hit the timelines." ⚠️ Note the two claims are compatible only if the contracts sign in 2H26 AND slip in execution — which is precisely the pattern the developer describes.**
- **On overcapacity risk in behind-the-meter, the developer is structurally constructive: "Potential overcapacity on BTM side? Think it is FIRST IN, LAST OUT. Once the generation is on site, DON'T SEE THAT EQUIPMENT MOVING… don't think grid connection for 4-5 years so see MODULAR WORKING WELL FOR A WHILE before large frames come on." And the framing of the whole bottleneck: "LAND IS NOT THE ISSUE… IT IS HOW DO YOU POWER THAT LAND."** **On fuel cells specifically: likes the technology and the "clean install," but "THEY DON'T RAMP FAST OR REACT FAST. NEED COMPLIMENTARY ASSETS AROUND THEM like solar, batteries, etc. THEY ARE NOT AT SCALE and HIGHER CAPEX," with the design pattern being fuel cells as baseload and "LOAD FOLLOW WITH SMALLER RECIPS" — the concrete instance being a "40 GW PROJECT (20 GW OF BE FUEL CELLS)."**
- **Equipment-side marks from the same rundown: [[GEV]] estimates raised again to "STREET HIGHS, NOW +36% VS STREET BY FY30" with PT unchanged at $1,210 and ⚠️ "GAS OE CAPACITY INCREASE TO 30GW INTENSIFIES MID-2030s OVERSUPPLY CONCERNS but also DE-RISKS ability to convert on services"; GNRC "confirms signing of a SECOND HYPERSCALER committing to large data center backup volumes in FY27 & FY28"; SPP has "ANOTHER MAJOR REGIONAL TRANSMISSION EXPANSION COMING IN NOV"; and "CRAFT LABOR REMAINS CONSTRAINED." BTM equities are still "down 20-40% over the past month for some of the BTM plays like SEI, LBRT, KGS and equipment names like INIO, FPS."**

**★ ③ A NEW COST LINE: US STATES ARE REPEALING DATA-CENTRE SALES-TAX EXEMPTIONS, ~7%+ ON EQUIPMENT (The Information · Ann Davis Vaughan, exclusive, 2026-08-02).** **"FOUR STATES ROLLED BACK OR PAUSED data center tax incentives [this summer], while officials in NINE OTHER STATES ARE CONSIDERING REPEAL measures… potentially adding 7% OR MORE TO EQUIPMENT COSTS."** **The arithmetic against this theme's own per-GW stack: "a gigawatt-scale campus requires roughly $40 BILLION IN IT EQUIPMENT, compared with $19 BILLION for the land, facilities, non-IT equipment and ON-SITE POWER that might supplement or replace a grid connection… In a state imposing, say, a 7% state sales tax, that $40 billion expense per gigawatt could grow by NEARLY $3 BILLION A GIGAWATT."** ⚠️ **TEXAS IS THE BIGGEST EXPOSURE AND IT IS THE MARGINAL GROWTH MARKET — "supposed to DETHRONE VIRGINIA AS THE WORLD'S LARGEST DATA CENTER MARKET in power capacity by 2030, according to JLL," with "TENS OF GIGAWATTS of AI campuses already underway or announced" and the grid operator holding requests "to connect HUNDREDS OF GIGAWATTS more." Governor Abbott "in June suddenly directed the Texas Legislature in its next session to 'REPEAL SALES TAX EXEMPTIONS and other outdated or unnecessary incentives for data centers,'" effective when it reconvenes IN 2027. Washington ended the exemption on REPLACEMENT AND REFURBISHMENT of DC equipment (~$207mn by 2029) — that taxes the ~5-year refresh cycle specifically. Arizona paused DC sales-tax exemptions for three years. VIRGINIA'S COMPROMISE IS THE TEMPLATE TO WATCH: instead of repeal, "a NEW TAX ON ELECTRICITY CONSUMPTION… data centers will pay an estimated $600 MILLION A YEAR, collectively" vs "$1 billion or more in sales taxes if the exemption had been repealed" — i.e. the political settlement is migrating from equipment taxes to POWER-CONSUMPTION taxes, which lands squarely on this theme rather than on the silicon.**
- **Framing worth keeping: "TAX-BREAK REPEALS ARE ONE OF A GROWING LIST OF FACTORS RAISING THE COSTS of AI data centers. Tech firms are paying a lot more than they bargained for to generate the power they need and FORTIFY INFRASTRUCTURE RANGING FROM WATER TO TRANSMISSION LINES. In many states, they are also scrambling to INSTALL NEW EQUIPMENT TO SATISFY POWER REGULATORS' DEMANDS that the facilities won't harm or overload grid equipment," and "LATELY THESE CONCERNS HAVE IMPACTED THE FINANCIAL TERMS OF SOME DEBT DEALS FOR DATA CENTERS."** ⚠️ **The last clause is the one to track — regulatory cost inflation showing up in DC debt pricing is the transmission channel from state politics to the cost of capital of the whole build. See [themes/hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md).** **Industry counter-position (Data Center Coalition): would "welcome the opportunity to work with the legislatures to put in more GUARDRAILS" pegging incentives to job creation and local investment, noting 40 states already exempt manufacturers' capital equipment.**

## What it is / why it matters

The AI build-out has turned electricity into the binding constraint on compute. Hyperscaler capex is running toward a **~$1.2T buy-side bogey for 2027** ("could be 1.3, could be 1.4," Jefferies power/hyperscalers, 2026-05-08) — and a data center is useless without firm, 24/7 power to plug into. Cantor frames the supply imbalance bluntly: NVIDIA can ship accelerators faster than anyone can build the buildings to house them — **~40 GW of chips into the US between 2025–2030 vs. ~21 GW of data center capacity built over the prior 25 years** (Cantor/CoreWeave, 2026-06-18). For a buy-sider, this is the rare theme where the bottleneck is physical — turbines, transformers, interconnects, baseload electrons, and the labor to assemble them — so the money concentrates in whoever owns scarce, contractable supply: nuclear/merchant generation (CEG, VST, TLN), gas-turbine OEMs (GEV), EPC labor (PWR, AGX), and gas midstream (WMB). The single biggest risk to the bull case is not demand — it is the grid, the labor pool, and timing.

  - **(1) Who funds it.** "**Funds managed by Macquarie Asset Management will, together with GIC, own the platform and fund the majority of the equity for each project.**" This is the **anchor-tenant / third-party-majority-equity template** applied to a frontier lab: the buildout sits on infrastructure-fund and sovereign-wealth balance sheets, the lab takes long-term lease obligations instead of capex. It is a different animal from the hardware SPVs this theme already tracks (Broadcom/TPU systems sold to external SPVs, booked gross ~$35B/GW, with **Google backstopping the lease payments**) — here **no credit backstop is disclosed**, so on the face of it MAM/GIC underwrite the tenancy risk. Watch whether this becomes the standard shape for lab-anchored capacity, because it moves the marginal AI data center's funding source from hyperscaler cash flow to **infra-fund + SWF equity** — directly relevant to the Bernstein funding-gap thread (2026 hyperscaler capex running level with CFO, so the marginal dollar comes from capital markets).
  - **(2) The ratepayer clause — a direct pre-emption of the political risk this page has been tracking all week.** "**Anthropic will cover electricity price increases that consumers otherwise may face from these sites**, in line with commitments it announced earlier this year." Read that against the three political vectors logged immediately below: Abbott's **June directive that data centers bear their own infrastructure costs and protect residential/small-business ratepayers** (the origin of both the ERCOT interconnection audit and the tax-exemption repeal), the **New York moratorium**, and the **state sales-tax-exemption repeals**. The tenant is now volunteering to absorb the consumer-rate delta as the price of siting velocity — i.e. the industry's answer to ratepayer backlash is becoming a **contractual cost line, not a lobbying position**. If that becomes table stakes it is a real, quantifiable margin item for every lab/hyperscaler siting new load, and a competitive advantage for the ones who can afford it.

  - **THE EVENT.** **Texas Governor Greg Abbott has directed the PUCT (Texas utility regulator) and ERCOT (grid operator) to conduct a comprehensive audit of ALL data center projects in ERCOT's interconnection process**, following the **Governor's June directive** to ensure data centers **bear their own infrastructure costs and protect residential and small-business ratepayers**. **ERCOT has PAUSED the "Batch Zero" review process, which was previously due to announce classification results on 2026-08-07, and plans to request timeline and process exceptions for the Batch Zero study.** ⚠️ **The June directive is the same one already on this page from the tax angle** (Abbott's June instruction to the Legislature to repeal DC sales-tax exemptions, effective when it reconvenes in 2027 — The Information, 08-02, on [hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md)): one governor, two instruments, both landing on this theme rather than on the silicon.
  - **★ THE SCALE MARKER, AND THE BASIS DISCIPLINE THAT GOES WITH IT: "ERCOT's interconnect queue stands at 474 GW and data centers represent 90% of the new requests."** ⚠️ **This is an INTERCONNECTION-QUEUE figure — requested capacity, i.e. applications — and it is NOT comparable to facility GW, IT-load GW or energized GW; it must never be netted against any deployment number on this page or in [`_meta/assumptions.md`](../_meta/assumptions.md) (`ai-dc-power-gw`: facility ≠ IT-load ≠ 800V-subset).** The nearest existing mark is a different queue: **>2,000 GW in the *FERC* interconnection queue with a 4-5 year wait** (Bernstein power names, 2026-06-18) — 474 GW is the ERCOT subset, on the same requested-capacity basis, so the two are consistent rather than conflicting. The genuinely new content is the **90% data-center share of new requests**, which is what makes a queue audit a data-center policy instrument rather than a grid-housekeeping exercise.
  - **BERNSTEIN'S INVESTMENT LOGIC — a scarcity-value argument, stated in three steps.** **(1) All contracts currently under execution are for ALREADY-APPROVED capacity and are NOT impacted by the audit.** **(2) Future pipelines for select ERCOT-exposed players will likely face delays from the additional audit — CIFR and CORZ named.** **(3) But "this audit throttles speculative data center pipeline and makes genuine sites with development history more valuable"**, with **bitcoin mining sites favourably placed — "the longest gestation, self-funding infra and local community management."** The conclusion: **"with increasing political opposition to new data center projects and fresh capacity being throttled by moratoriums/state directives, the approved MWs become more valuable."** ⚠️ **Read this as the pricing counterpart to the political risk this page already carries: the same backlash that raises cost and delays new builds transfers value to whoever is already energized. It is not a demand argument and Bernstein does not make one.**

  - **PER-NAME DETAIL WORTH KEEPING.** **CIFR — the approved 1.2 GW is unaffected**, comprising **700 MW under construction (for AWS and Fluidstack)**, **grid-approved 270 MW (70 MW Texas + 200 MW Ohio)** and a **207 MW operational BTC mining site in Odessa, Texas**; the pipeline is **8 Texas sites (~3 GW) plus one 200 MW Ohio site**, with **2 GW of the total 3.2 GW pipeline dependent on Batch Zero approval for energization** — i.e. the audit gates roughly **two-thirds of the pipeline** while leaving the approved base intact. **CLSK (300 MW Brazoria County) and CORZ (300 MW Pecos) are likewise dependent on the Texas grid for expansion of existing sites.** **WULF benefits from geographic diversification — 3.6 GW of power access across Kentucky, Maryland and New York, with no Texas exposure at all**, which on this note's own logic makes it the cleanest expression of the thesis (approved MW, no queue risk). **IREN — a 5.8 GW portfolio across the US, Canada, Australia and Europe, with ~2 GW ENERGIZED in Texas: 650 MW at Childress (ongoing cloud expansion) and 1.4 GW recently energized at Sweetwater 1, where a 200 IT MW data center is under construction.** ⚠️ **Note the basis stack inside that one sentence — 5.8 GW portfolio (planned pipeline) vs ~2 GW energized vs 200 IT MW of data center under construction: three different bases on a single asset, which is exactly the conflation this page's GW rule exists to prevent.**
  - **⚠️ AN INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY IN THE NOTE ON RIOT, LOGGED RATHER THAN RESOLVED.** On page 1 Bernstein writes that **RIOT "has ~2 GW of grid connected power assets, including ~1 GW energized operating capacity in Texas available for AI deployments (700 MW in Rockdale, and 400 MW in Corsicana)"** — 700 + 400 = 1.1 GW, consistent with "~1 GW energized." On page 2, inside the co-location-lease cohort, the same note says **"RIOT with ready operational 1.7 GW capacity in Texas looks well-placed."** **The 1,700 MW in Exhibit 1 is explicitly the *Texas concentration of the planned pipeline*, not energized capacity — so the page-2 sentence appears to promote a pipeline figure to "ready operational."** Both marks are carried with attribution; **neither is adopted as this page's number for RIOT's energized Texas capacity, and any model using 1.7 GW as available-now capacity should be treated as unsupported by page 1 of the same note.**
  - **★★ THE MONETIZATION DATAPOINT — the most transferable content in the note, and it belongs to this page rather than to the miners (Exhibit 2).** **IREN's revenue yield per IT MW has risen 50%, from ~$10mn under the [[MSFT]] contract to ~$15mn under the [[NVDA]] contract.** **On a GROSS-MW basis the same series runs ~$11mn (MSFT) → ~$14mn (NVDA) → ~$11mn (Enterprises).** ⚠️ **The two bases are different and must not be mixed — $/IT MW and $/gross MW are separate series in the same exhibit, and the Enterprises column exists only on the gross-MW basis.** Alongside them: **recent enterprise cloud deals indicate GPU-hour rates 20-25% HIGHER than previous guidance**, and **IREN is targeting $4bn of ARR on 480 MW of cloud deployment** (~$8.3mn of ARR per MW of cloud deployment on the deployment basis Bernstein states — our arithmetic, not Bernstein's, and on an unlabelled MW basis, so treat it as indicative). Bernstein's logic in one line, worth quoting because it is the whole thesis: **"If MWs are scarce, it makes sense to earn more per MW."**
    - ⚠️ **THE READ-THROUGH THIS PAGE NEEDS, AND IT IS THE UNCOMFORTABLE SIDE OF THE SAME TRADE: a rising $/MW for the neocloud/host is a rising COST LINE for the compute BUYER.** Every mark above is revenue to the landlord and expense to whoever rents the capacity. Cross-file to **[[CRWV]]** — whose own capacity is leased, not owned (every datacenter leased, $10.1B operating-lease ROU), and where SemiAnalysis has already measured **neocloud colocation leases at a 3-5% higher yield-on-cost** than hyperscale/IG deals — and to **[[NVDA]]** and **[[MSFT]]** as the contract counterparties named in the exhibit. **Sanity-check the direction against the marks already on this page and they agree: MS's realised powered-shell value creation of ~$7 to $19 per watt "continues to trend higher" (07-27), JPM's third-party neocloud $/W ladder (Anthropic ~$29, Google ~$50 vs ~$10-20 industry average, 07-07), and Baker's contracted-base-repricing-higher argument (08-04). Four independent sources, same direction: the price of scarce approved capacity is going up, and the buyer pays it.** ⚠️ **Basis caution when stacking them: Bernstein's figures are $mn of revenue per MW per year; JPM/MS quote $/W of deal value or value creation. Do not put them in the same column.**
  - **SECTOR-LEVEL SIZING — the miner/AI-infra cohort as a supply source, which is the leg this page has only ever had second-hand (via MS's "converting Bitcoin sites 10/14/19 GW" workaround line).** **"Emerging AI Infra stocks remain well positioned to solve 'time to compute' given their planned 30 GW power portfolio and operating ability to deliver 'warm powered shells' in time."** And the contracting track record, which is net-new to this page: **"over the past two years, miners have contracted 8 GW of power capacity to hyperscalers, neoclouds and AI-chip manufacturers across 20+ deals worth over $160bn."** ⚠️ **That is ~$20bn of TCV per contracted GW at the cohort level — a blunt but useful cross-check on the $40-60bn/GW-annually deal pricing Citi quotes and on MS's ~$15,500mn/GW of powered-shell value creation, and it sits on a CONTRACTED-GW basis (not energized, not facility). Note also that the 30 GW planned portfolio is a planned-pipeline figure and is roughly consistent with Exhibit 1's six names summing to ~18.7 GW of planned pipeline before the rest of the cohort.**

IREN 97.0x/−277.3x/−38.1x; CLSK 175.2x in 2026E) and quoting them would mislead rather than inform.** ⚠️ **Also worth stating plainly: six of seven names are Outperform, so this is a cohort-level bullish slate rather than a set of differentiated calls — the relative PTs, not the ratings, carry the information.** Disclosure context available in the note: **Bernstein is part of a joint venture between Société Générale and AllianceBernstein**, and because the publication "covers six or more companies" the valuation-methodology and risk disclosures are delegated to a website rather than printed — i.e. **no per-name valuation basis is disclosed in the note itself**. ⚠️ **And the position disclosures, now read from the full PDF appendix, are material to how much weight a 6-of-7-Outperform slate deserves: "Gautam Chhugani maintains long positions in various crypto currencies";
