- **NEW (2026-07-20) — Bernstein (Rasgon): US DC active capacity ~51 GW end-June (+29% YoY); pipeline ~338 GW (+179% YoY); ~$8B of WFE per incremental GW (Bernstein · Rasgon "WFE / AI GW note", 2026-07-20).** Bernstein tracks **US DC active capacity ~51 GW at end-June (+11.4 GW / +29% YoY)** against a **pipeline ~338 GW (+217 GW / +179% YoY)** — both facility/active-capacity GW — and converts the buildout to equipment at **~$8B of WFE per incremental GW** (the WFE-TAM/GW-conversion detail sits on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md)). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_WFE.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-17) — Bernstein: AI-accelerator power ~37 GW by 2028 (from 12.8 GW in 2025, 43% CAGR); total AI-DC power >50 GW (Bernstein "The CPU Renaissance? $223bn TAM", 2026-06-17).** Bernstein models **AI-accelerator power ~37 GW by 2028** (up from **12.8 GW in 2025**, ~43% CAGR) and **total AI-DC power >50 GW** — an **accelerator/IT-load GW basis** (distinct from the facility-GW active/pipeline figures above; do not merge). CPU is **40-60% of general-server power but only 5-10% of AI-server power**. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_CPU.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-16/17) — Data-center opposition is becoming a real gating variable → "build/bring/buy your own power" + a wave of local moratoria (MS · Michelle Weaver/Ariana Salvatore, 2026-07-17; Jefferies · Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-07-16).** MS's Macro-to-Micro argues expanding US opposition to DC development will **raise project costs, extend timelines and disperse the build** — the core fight is electricity affordability, with policymakers increasingly wanting **developers (not existing ratepayers) to fund the generation + grid infra**. The **Ratepayer Protection Act** (House) would require a "large-load standard" (DCs pay for grid upgrades) and could codify the Ratepayer Protection Pledge signed by Amazon/Google/Meta/Microsoft/OpenAI/Oracle/xAI, moving from piecemeal utility jurisdiction toward a **national DC tariff**. MS estimates a **~38 GW US DC power shortfall through 2028** with interconnection delays already >5 years in several regions → **structural tailwind to onsite generation** ("build/bring/buy your own power"); MS's trade is **Long Onsite Power (MSXXONPW)** (OW SEI/INIO/BE), down 19.1% from 6/23 highs = entry point. Jefferies catalogs the local backlash concretely: **Palm Beach County rejected "Project Tango"; NY signed a 1-yr EO barring new large-scale DCs; Prince William County (VA) QTS abandoned the Digital Gateway after a rezoning legal challenge; Fayetteville GA banned DCs in all city zones; Denver CO passed a 1-yr moratorium** — Jefferies expects DC siting to be a **midterm-election issue** at local and federal levels. (See also the Oracle/OpenAI **Project Jupiter** NM permit fight on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md).) Read: opposition isn't stopping the buildout but is repricing who pays for power and accelerating behind-the-meter/onsite generation. (MS · Weaver/Salvatore, 2026-07-17; Jefferies · Favuzza, 2026-07-16; see [../CEG.md](../CEG.md), [../GEV.md](../GEV.md), [../TLN.md](../TLN.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-12) — MS: 120 GW hyperscaler compute by '28 (from 30 in '25); cost-per-GW +~20% on memory + powered-shell inflation; ground-break-to-opening timelines stretching to ~3 years (Morgan Stanley "Internet: $1.4trln of Capex, 4X in Compute Capacity", 2026-07-12).** Morgan Stanley's bottom-up hyperscaler deep-dive frames the power/build side of the $1.4trln '28 capex wall: total available hyperscaler compute reaches **~120 GW by '28, up from ~30 GW in '25** (~4x). Cost-per-GW is being revised **~20% higher (for GPUs)** on **memory + powered-shell cost inflation** — MS now models **~$11-13mn/MW of outside-the-rack cost for GB200/TPU/Trainium, rising to ~$16-19mn/MW for GB300/Vera Rubin/Rubin Ultra** — driven by extended lead times for electrical/mechanical equipment, building materials, skilled-labor scarcity, and the shift to behind-the-meter power. Two power-relevant structural drivers: **limitations on chips/racks, powered shells and other bottlenecks are stretching ground-break-to-opening timelines to as much as ~3 years**, and **growing social backlash to DC development + political uncertainty into the '28 election are pushing hyperscalers to start building sooner (to emphasize job creation)** — both add to inflationary pressure. GW adds: GOOGL adds the most compute (9/11 GW '27/'28), AWS has the most available compute in '28 (~35 GW vs GOOGL ~31 GW); META 14/21 GW '27/'28 (from ~3.5 GW YE25). (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak "Internet: $1.4trln of Capex, 4X in Compute Capacity", 2026-07-12; see [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md), [../META.md](../META.md), [../AMZN.md](../AMZN.md), [../GOOG.md](../GOOG.md)) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MS_full_note.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-11) — Mizuho sizes the GW-deployment wall: ~106 GW cumulative DC deployed 2026-30E (~$5.3T at ~$50B/GW, ~4x 2025-26 combined); DC to drive >50-60% of US power capacity adds (Mizuho "DC Capex Ramping to >$1.4T in 2028E", Vijay Rakesh, 2026-07-11).** Mizuho estimates a **cumulative ~106 GW of global DC capacity deployed 2026-30E at an avg ~21 GW/yr** — **17.5 GW / 20 GW in 2026E/2027E, accelerating to ~23-24 GW/yr in 2028E+** (vs ~12 GW in 2025) — and at **~$50B/GW of total AI-infra cost implies ~$5.3T of spend 2026-30E (~$1T/yr), ~4x the combined 2025-26E spend of ~$1.4T**. Mizuho reads **DC as >50-60% of US power-capacity deployments 2026-30E**, with the US share of global DC capacity reaching **~70% in 2027E** before declining to **~66% by 2030** as other geographies ramp — reinforcing the "power is the binding constraint on the compute wall" thesis this page tracks. (Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh "DC Capex Ramping to >$1.4T in 2028E; FCF Outlook Improving", 2026-07-11; the capex/FCF legs sit on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md)) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/DC_Capex_Ramping_to__14T_in_2028E_FCF_Outlook_Improving.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-11) — Goldman: ~$7.6T AI capex by 2031 (~$5.1T compute / >$2.1T data centers / only ~$358B power, <5%) — flagged as too light on power given power is the emerging bottleneck (Goldman Sachs via @C_Barraud, 2026-07-11).** Goldman estimates **~$7.6T of AI capex by 2031**, split **~$5.1T compute / >$2.1T data centers / only ~$358B power (<5% of total)** — a mix flagged as **too light on power** given power is the emerging bottleneck this page tracks. Sits alongside JPM's $5.5T-through-2030 frame below as an even larger, longer-dated total, and its <5% power allocation is the quantified version of the grid-is-the-constraint thesis. (Goldman Sachs via @C_Barraud, 2026-07-11)
- **NEW (2026-07-09) — Meta's "5 titans": five simultaneous 1GW+ clusters, tent DC design, behind-the-meter power, and 2,000km scale-across as the demand read-through (SemiAnalysis "The Future of Meta Superintelligence", 2026-07-09).** SemiAnalysis's Datacenter + AI-Networking Model details what it calls "an unprecedentedly large compute ramp led by 5 titans" — Meta building **five 1GW+ clusters simultaneously**: **Prometheus** (Ohio; already partially operational; expanding from an initial ~1GW to **>3GW** within two years), **Hyperion** (Louisiana; **~1.5GW under construction today = 3× the world's-largest single buildings at 400MW each + 3× standard 100MW buildings**), plus **El Paso, Iowa (1GW lease signed) and Indiana**. Historically no single full-GW campus was ever under construction at once (the closest was AWS's 800MW Rainier in Indiana) — Meta now has two (Hyperion + Iowa). It uses the scrappy **"tent" DC design** to bring capacity online fast, and **some sites use behind-the-meter power** (SemiAnalysis tracks the exact system type + flags permitting risk). **Networking / scale-across:** Prometheus is not one datacenter but a **"constellation" of 27 datacenters across 6 campuses** (5 within 6km of each other, the 6th ~75-80km away); spreading a >100MW training workload across sites is the path past current thermal/power limits. Meta's **AI-Backbone (AIBB)** — L3 Superspines (Backend Aggregation / BAG) aggregating up to 5 DSF / 7 NSF scale-out regions onto a single L4 Inter-BAG hub — delivers **~22 petabits/s of bi-directional bandwidth** across Prometheus, using a mix of **LR optics + DWDM/ZR optics** between campuses (light-propagation latency to a 100km site can't drop below ~500µs → asynchronous training: pretraining synchronous in one region, RL spread globally). The other titans go further — **connecting campuses up to 2,000km apart.** Demand read-through: bottoms-up confirmation that hyperscaler GW build is accelerating (power, land, networking optics and BTM gas the binding inputs) and that scale-across networking is the new architectural frontier. See [../META.md](../META.md); the compute/monetization legs sit on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md) / [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_META.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-08) — A 100+GW US generation shortfall 2026-30: 230+GW demand vs ~93GW accredited utility supply (BofA "Power gap: US electricity supply & demand", Obin + Arya, 2026-07-08).** BofA's industrials + semis teams jointly size a **US electricity generation shortfall of 100+GW between 2026-30**. Feeding the BofA semi team's raised chip forecast (208GW of *global* IT load 2026-30; annual capacity additions ~17GW 2025 → ~60GW 2030) into US demand — grossed up for cooling at a **1.20 PUE** and a conservative **50% US mix** — yields **~125GW of US DC load** (would be ~187GW at a 75% US mix), which on top of base load growth implies **230+GW of total US capacity demand**. US electric load is modeled to grow at a **4.1% CAGR 2026-30** (production 4,407 TWh 2025 → 5,278 TWh 2030, +20%), the fastest since the 1990s. Against that, the BofA utilities team forecasts only **~93GW of accredited supply from regulated utilities** ("the market is no longer constrained by demand — it is constrained by where power can actually be delivered"), with Texas = 61% of net additions and ~221GW of *nameplate* additions netting to just ~62GW accredited after intermittency haircuts (solar 24.4% capacity factor, wind 34.2%) + 43GW of retirements. Gas turbines are the preferred fill but **mostly sold out through 2030** (commissioning lags shipment ~2 yrs; global turbine capacity 55GW 2025 → 94GW 2030), pushing demand toward **gas reciprocating engines** (Caterpillar/INNIO/Rolls-Royce/Wärtsilä capacity expansions), coal-retirement delays (9 plants already extended), batteries, and demand destruction (US power prices +7% YTD; Ohio +21%, Virginia +18%, PA +17%). Named Buy beneficiaries: **GE Vernova (GEV), Eaton (ETN), Emerson (EMR), INNIO (INIO)** — GEV gas-turbine capacity 15GW 2025 → 24GW 2030, the largest OEM (see [GEV](../GEV.md)). This 125GW-DC / 93GW-supply gap is the cleanest bottoms-up quantification yet of the "grid is the bottleneck" thesis. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_on_eletricity.html)
  - **Supplement (re-ingested 2026-07-08 as `Bofa_on_grid.html`) — the load-growth-in-historical-context framing + the 75%-US-mix upside case (BofA · Obin/Arya "Power gap", 2026-07-08).** Adds detail to the note above: BofA's decade CAGR is **~2.8% (2020-30E)** — the *implied 2026-30E* rate is **~4.1% at a 50% US DC mix, rising to ~5.1% at a 75% US mix** (the 75% case lifts US DC load ~125GW → ~187GW). The key context: **2010-20 US load growth was flat (~0.0% CAGR)** — an *exception* driven by LED adoption, appliance-efficiency (EnergyStar) and residential-solar (a combined ~150bp drag) — vs 2.3% (1990-2000), 2.9% (1980-90), 4.1% (1970-80) and 7.3% (1960-70); with those drags now exhausted (incandescent <10% of bulbs, resi-solar tax credit ended 2026), the AI-DC era (~2.8%/decade) reads as a return to the historical norm, not an anomaly. Named beneficiaries carry BofA prices GEV **$1,077.08** (PO $1,310), ETN $395.68 (PO $490), EMR $137.91 (PO $191), INIO $34.55 (PO $46). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_on_grid.html)
- **NEW (2026-05-25 note; ingested 2026-07-08) — Rack power up 100x to 1.5MW at Feynman; ~233GW deployed through CY30; AI analog $7.9bn → $27bn inside a $1.7tn AI-DC-systems TAM (BofA "Watts to Tokens", Vivek Arya, 2026-05-25).** BofA's bottoms-up power-semis model: rising compute density drives **rack power capacity up ~100x, from 10-15kW in traditional cloud servers to ~1.5MW for Nvidia's Feynman platform (CY29/30)** — the GPU/XPU roadmap (Hopper→Blackwell→Rubin→Rubin Ultra→Feynman) is the demand driver behind the wholesale grid-to-rack power-delivery revamp (the 800V transition). Sizing: **~233GW of accelerator capacity deployed through CY30** (annual GW deployed 4x from ~15GW CY25 to ~60GW CY30) expands the AI analog-semi market from **$7.9bn (CY25) to $27bn (CY30, 28% CAGR)** — the rack-to-core content TAM alone goes $7.6bn → **$25bn** (rack content ~25x from $36K today to ~$300K per 600kW rack, approaching $1mn in the MW-class era) — all inside a **$1.7tn AI-DC-systems long-term TAM**. This corroborates (and is the raised-forecast source behind) the F3 "Power gap" note above, and the BofA $1.7tn figure already tracked below (AI 2030). The per-platform GW table is folded onto [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md) (the Google-TPU/AWS-Trainium/Broadcom/MediaTek/Marvell ASIC ramp). Read-throughs: [NVDA](../NVDA.md) (Feynman rack power the demand root), [AMD](../AMD.md), [MSFT](../MSFT.md), [MEDIATEK](../MEDIATEK.md). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_Watt_to_tokens.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-07) — Big-3 datacenter capacity 27→39→57 GW (2025→27); cost/GW spread $25-45bn; capacity deals >$40bn/GW (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07).** BofA (Justin Post) translates raised hyperscaler capex into installed GW capacity: the **Big-3 (Alphabet + Meta + Amazon AWS) had ~27GW exiting 2025, growing to ~39GW in 2026 → ~57GW in 2027.** **Amazon adds the most (~15GW over 2026-27), then Google (+9GW) and Meta (+~6GW).** Cost per incremental GW (2026) spans a wide $25-45bn range by operator — **Amazon lowest at ~$25bn/GW** (scale + internal Trainium/Graviton chips + core-cloud-vs-AI mix), **Google ~$37bn/GW** (TPU-differentiated, premium-cloud mix), **Meta highest at ~$45bn/GW** (upfront land/building ahead of GPU deployment + external-GPU reliance + specialized AI capacity). On monetization, BofA models **Cloud revenue-per-GW of $10-17bn (AWS ~$10.6bn 2026, Google Cloud ~$15.7bn)** — well below **recent Anthropic & Google–SpaceX capacity deals struck at >$40bn/GW (up to ~$50bn/GW for specialized AI capacity)**, which BofA reads as upside to Cloud-revenue estimates. Cross-company read-through: this GW/cost/monetization framing underpins BofA's per-name valuation calls — implied Street value ~$110bn per Google 2028 Cloud GW, ~$59bn per AWS GW, and only ~$4bn per Meta AI GW (see [AMZN](../AMZN.md), [GOOG](../GOOG.md), [META](../META.md)) (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07). _(General cost-per-GW cross-check: JPM's hyperscaler-recap benchmark of ~$35B capex/GW and ~$12B cloud rev/GW, 2026-04-30, sits inside BofA's ranges.)_
- **NEW (2026-07-01) — Korea's "Three Mega Investment Projects" put ~$1.5tn behind semis + AI data centers + physical AI; SK hynix alone adds KRW1,000tn for AI DCs (Morgan Stanley "South Korea: A Landmark Industrial Strategy", 2026-07-01).** President Lee unveiled (June 29) a landmark industrial strategy — **"Three Mega Investment Projects" sized at ≥KRW2,000tn (~US$1.5tn)** across (1) semiconductor clusters, (2) AI data centers, and (3) physical AI & robotics, **led by Samsung and SK Group**, with the government providing power, water, land, infrastructure, workforce and housing support. Samsung + SK CEOs announced an *additional* KRW1,500tn on top of committed plans over 10-15 years; **SK hynix nearly doubled its commitment, adding another KRW1,000tn to capex for AI data centers** (KRW1,100tn total AI-memory network: KRW600tn Yongin, KRW100tn Chungju, KRW400tn southwestern cluster). On the AI-DC leg specifically: a public-private initiative (SK Telecom, GS Group, Naver + government) targets **8.4 GW of AI-DC capacity by 2029 (Phase 1) → 15 GW by 2035 (Phase 2)**, ~KRW550tn of DC construction — plus an **Ulsan AI DC with AWS (operations from 2027)** and a next-gen DC with Nvidia. MS sees **GDP growth upside of ≥50-70bp through 2030** from construction/production activity, and **100-150bp beyond 2031** on enhanced AI productivity — "execution is key" (limited southwestern-region power/water/workforce infrastructure the named risk) (Morgan Stanley "South Korea: A Landmark Industrial Strategy Unveiled", 2026-07-01). _(Corroborates the memory-capacity leg tracked on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md); the DRAM-capacity-doubling read sits there.)_
- **NEW (2026-07-02) — Meta contracted >5GW across Cloud & Colo in H1'26; two largest campuses = 2.5GW under construction (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute", 2026-07-02).** SemiAnalysis argues Meta's DC & compute procurement will *accelerate*, not slow — "in just the first six months of the year, Meta has contracted **over 5GW of capacity across Cloud & Colo**, and that doesn't even include all their accelerating self-build activity." It shows Meta's **two largest campuses = 2.5GW of capacity under construction**, and directly rebuts the "half of US datacenters are delayed and only 5GW are under construction" headline ("just two datacenters are half of this"). After ~10GW of deals signed since early 2024, the bulk of Meta's additions now run through **third parties** (a large RPO-growth source for CoreWeave, Nebius, others). Meta's fast "tent" DC design brings capacity online quickly to enable SpaceX-style compute monetization (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud", 2026-07-02). _(The hyperscaler-capex read-through — Meta 2027 capex "shockingly high" + the four monetization use-cases — sits on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md) and [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md).)_
- **NEW (2026-06-08) — Delora/UBS DC-infrastructure expert: the frontier is "stranded power" + load-flattening to free electrons for IT; power-electronics displacing electromechanical gear; a single 1GW contract can make-or-break a vendor's order book (UBS · Amit Mehta w/ Alex Cordovil, Delora Group / ex-Vertiv, 2026-06-08).** UBS's Industrials desk re-hosted Delora's Cordovil: **power is "the biggest bottleneck of the industry"** and the next leg of novelty is accessing **stranded power** — the grid is built for peak capacity used only a few hours/year, so operators are chasing the spare capacity plus **flattening their own DC load** (gas/steam-powered chillers, thermal storage) to "take that peak power and say, hey, IT, now you can use that power" — i.e. maximize IT-provisioned power out of whatever they've secured. Behind-the-meter/onsite is "already the talk of the town." On architecture: **800V DC is "definitely the topic on the power side," with SSTs starting to pick up from ~2028** (DC breakers are power-electronic, not electromechanical). Structural share shift: **power-electronics players stand to gain while traditional electrical-equipment makers lose out** (Delta + Murata/TI/Monolithic on-board content becoming integral to the tray); a single **1 GW deployment/contract can make or break an order book for 3–4 years** (Modine cited). **Flex "one to watch"** on its DC-power-focused spin-off. Read-through: reinforces the grid-is-the-constraint thesis and adds the load-flattening/stranded-power operational lever the earlier bullets don't capture. (See [../VRT.md](../VRT.md), [../FLEX.md](../FLEX.md); the 800V/SST leg sits on [800v-dc-power.md](800v-dc-power.md).) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_08_08_06_26_ubs_expert_call_data_center_check_in_w_de.html)
- **NEW (2026-05-07) — Capstone house deck (GEV): AI/data centers = ~80% of incremental US GW 2024–2030; GEV equipment generates ~55% of US electricity; US is 40% of revenue but 60% of backlog (Capstone · Fernanda Watkins "Case GE Vernova", 2026-05-07).** Capstone's internal GEV thesis deck frames the firm-power scarcity this page tracks from the OEM side: **AI/data centers drive ~80% of incremental US GW 2024–2030**; GEV holds ~half the world's installed gas-turbine base (**+800 GW, ~2x the #2**) and its equipment generates **~55% of US electricity**; the US is **40% of revenue but 60% of backlog**, and **AI demands disproportionately more gas turbine than the consolidated market** — more constructive for GEV. The exceptional-demand/severe-supply-constraint combination is generating unprecedented pricing power: **+10–15%/yr pass-throughs on new orders, rising down-payments to secure a queue slot, an order book full through 2028–2029, and new orders carrying 10–20pp of margin above the existing backlog** — feeding an accelerating FCF ramp; SMR is a post-2030 optionality leg. An internal house view corroborating the GEV firm-power thesis (see [../GEV.md](../GEV.md)); numbers are Capstone's synthesis, not a sell-side mark. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_07_VF_Apresenta%C3%A7%C3%A3o_GEV_Maio26_-_Fernanda_Watkins.html)
- **NEW (2026-05-27; historical/superseded) — MS's original bottom-up cost-per-GW-across-9-chip-generations model: hyperscalers add ~14 GW ('26) / ~20 GW ('27) of incremental capacity; AMZN +3.9 GW of new power capacity in 2025 (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak "How Much Capacity Will $2T of Hyperscaler Capex Bring By '27?", 2026-05-27).** This is the earlier vintage of the MS bottom-up capacity work that the 07-12 "$1.4trln / 120 GW by '28" note (top of page) later updated: MS built **cost-per-GW frameworks across 9 chip generations (NVDA + custom silicon)** and modeled hyperscalers adding **~14 GW ('26) → ~20 GW ('27)** of incremental capacity (ex-internal, **~6 GW / ~8 GW** goes to the AWS + Google public-cloud businesses), noting **AMZN's ~3.9 GW of new power capacity in 2025**. Logged for lineage only — do NOT treat as current (superseded by the 07-12 MS numbers). (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak, 2026-05-27; capacity/capex detail on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md)) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260527_2032.html)
- **Demand is staggering and accelerating, not peaking.** Microsoft backlog +99% y/y to $627B; Oracle "half a trillion"; CoreWeave "pulled a Wayne Gretzky" at $99B backlog and declared itself sold out; 2026 hyperscaler capex is set to *accelerate* across the board (Jefferies, 2026-05-08). VST cites hyperscaler capex **>$700B in 2026, ~+50% y/y** (VST Q4 FY25, 2026-02-26); CEG calls hyperscaler 2026 capex "almost 75% higher than last year" (CEG Q1 FY26, 2026-05-11).
- **JPM's "AI Capex 2.0" sizes the wall: $5.5T through 2030.** JPM lifts total AI capex to **$5.5T through 2030** (from $5.1T), of which **$4.1T debt-financed** (LTCs averaging >85%, some >90% — "90% Loan-to-Cost ≈ 60% Loan-to-Value"). Top-4 US hyperscalers (GOOG/AMZN/MSFT/META) guided ~**$700-725B total 2026 capex, +~75% y/y** off ~$410B in 2025 (3rd straight 60%+ year); combined five-name FY26 capex >$750B, **approaching/exceeding $1.1T in FY27**, vs combined FY27 OCF ~$900B+ — so debt/equity issuance stays structural. Capex mix shifts: DC "box" capex ~$280B (2026) plateaus ~$580-590B (2028-30) while GPU/ASIC capex climbs $340B (2026) → >$800B (2030); >$3T of accelerator financings over 5 yrs (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16). Base-case DC capacity now **138 GW through 2030** (up from 122 GW Nov), power still the binding constraint; ASICs ~42% of AI chip units 2026 → ~53% 2027 (ASIC units +109% y/y vs GPU +39%) (JPM, 2026-06-16). Corroborated by Redburn: AI-11 capex +69% (2026) → $869bn (2027, +23%), with YTD upward revisions of +39%/+55% to the 2026/2027 numbers (Redburn, 2026-06-17).
- **800V power-architecture spend layers on top.** The grid-to-rack 800V transition itself adds a multi-billion content layer: JPM sizes DC power-management TAM ~$20B (2025) → ~$45B (2028) at ~+30% CAGR, and notes DC IT load on track to **triple to ~300 GW by 2030** (~+25% CAGR, >35 GW/yr new capacity, Omdia) (JPM "800V", 2026-06-09). Citi models ~19% CAGR in AI DC deployments ~9 GW → ~21 GW (2025-30), DC analog/power-semi TAM ~$9B → ~$35B (30% CAGR) (Citi "800V power", 2026-06-15). See [800V DC Data-Center Power](800v-dc-power.md) for the architecture detail.
- **The grid — not generation — is the bottleneck.** Bernstein: "generation we don't see as the constraint, we see the grid as more of the bottleneck," with **>2,000 GW of projects in the FERC interconnection queue** and a **4–5 year wait** to connect anywhere in the US (Bernstein power names, 2026-06-18). Jefferies' real-world read is sharper: frustration with utility interconnection has "reached a boil," pushing customers to behind-the-meter generation, batteries, and "bring-your-own-generation" (Jefferies, 2026-05-08).
- **The transformer sub-bottleneck — a US power-transformer "super-cycle" (Morgan Stanley Global Industrials "Global Guide to Ride US Transformer", 2026-03-29).** Inside the grid bottleneck sits the transformer: MS sizes a multi-year US super-cycle in which the equivalent of **~4.3k large power transformers (LPTs, >100 MVA) are needed in the US 2025-30 — ~2-3x domestic production** (vs only ~200-300 LPTs produced domestically in 2024), with **US LPT TAM growing ~14% CAGR through 2030** and **import reliance staying >80%** — a "seller's market until at least 2030." Distribution-transformer lead times have stretched **~4x (a few months in 2020 → 2-4 years)** and prices are **up 4-9x in 3 years**; new North-American capacity mostly doesn't arrive until 2027-29. MS models **data centers reaching ~20% of US energy by 2035 (from ~6% in 2024)** and **~74 GW of incremental US DC power demand 2025-28e → a ~9-18 GW power-access shortfall** (already netting time-to-power solutions) — consistent with the Byrd 10-20% figure below. Preferred names to play it: **GE Vernova (OW)**, HD Hyundai Electric, Eaton, Siemens Energy; ~6% upside to GEV 2027E EPS in the transformer bull case (see [GEV](../GEV.md)). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Morgan_Stanley_Global_Industrials_Global_Guide_to_Ride_US_Transformer.html)
- **The power-access gap + the politics — MS "10 Thematic Predictions" (Morgan Stanley · Stephen Byrd, 2026-04-06).** Byrd's utilities/power team models **US data-center developers running ~10-20% (midpoint) to ~20-30% short of power access through 2028** even after crediting a probability-weighted "time-to-power" solution stack — **nat-gas turbines (~90% success), Bloom fuel cells (~90%), siting DCs at operating nuclear plants (~75%), and converting Bitcoin-mining sites (~90%)**. Concrete premium mark: **Google's guarantee of a Hut8 lease for a data center ultimately used by Anthropic translated to a ~300% power-price premium** — evidence AI players "will pay significant premia" not to be short power. Byrd flags the emerging **political/regulatory backlash on consumer power bills**: the **GRID Act** (Hawley/Blumenthal — no DC-driven consumer-price increases, consumers first on the grid, new DCs on separate generation), the **AI Data Center Moratorium Act** (Sanders/Ocasio-Cortez), and a likely **Maine ban on DC projects >20 MW through 2027** — with the read that **merchant power companies whose margins track power prices "may face challenges given greater policy efforts to reduce customer energy bills"** (a risk to the merchant-IPP names CEG/VST). Byrd's OW "AI Infrastructure + Powering AI" screen names include CEG (+38% to PT), VST (+39%) and WMB (+25%). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MSR_000333CH_040626_2536.html)
- **A lower-than-consensus $/GW build cost — Bernstein AI-DC value-chain framework (Bernstein "AI DC value chain", 2026-03-26).** Bernstein models AI data-center capex at **~$36Bn per GW (~$6Mn per rack, ~5,929 racks/GW, ~$21Bn of that in rack cost)** — **notably below the $50-60Bn/GW commonly cited** — and drives per-company revenue off a simple GWs-online × TAM/GW × market-share framework (BloombergNEF ~16 GW of 2027E capacity the base). Component-of-GW split: **foundry ~2.5-3% (~$1.1Bn/GW), HBM ~3-3.5% (~$1.1Bn/GW), WFE ~3-4% (~$1.2Bn/GW), power supplies ~4%, transformers ~5%**; ~**$1.3Bn of electricity to run a GW for a year** at $0.15/kWh (personnel negligible). Favors electrical names (Eaton), Delta (in-rack power), and the AMD/MediaTek/Marvell ASIC leg. A useful low-end bookend to the Mizuho ~$50B/GW and MS ~$11-19mn/MW marks already on this page. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_AI_DC_value_chain_2026.html)
- **Power demand inflecting after two flat decades.** Bernstein models **+3% US power-demand CAGR to 2030** (range heard 2.5–4.5%), with data-center electricity roughly doubling from **~250 TWh to ~500 TWh** by 2030 on ~40 GW of incremental DC capacity (Bernstein power names, 2026-06-18). VST sees ERCOT load +5–6% and PJM +2–3% to 2030 (VST Q1 FY26, 2026-05-07).
- **Gas is the bridge; nuclear/geothermal the only long-run baseload displacement.** Survey of 50+ DC operators: **98% prefer grid (or grid + on-site backup)**, with reliability/uptime and speed-to-power the deciding factors — and "three years out is effectively unacceptable" (Bernstein, 2026-06-18). Behind-the-meter stays a **5–10% niche** (more expensive than grid), not the 50% some bulls model (Bernstein, 2026-06-18).
- **Nuclear is the scarce premium product.** CEG runs the largest US nuclear fleet (~22 GW, 96.8% capacity factor) with ~900–1,000 MW of uprates identified and the Crane (ex-Three Mile Island) restart progressing (CEG Q1 FY26, 2026-05-11). VST has **~3.8 GW of nuclear under 20-yr PPAs** (Amazon at Comanche Peak; Meta across Perry/Davis-Besse/Beaver Valley) (VST Q1 FY26). TLN's anchor is the **~1.9 GW front-of-the-meter AWS PPA at Susquehanna** (TLN Q1 FY26, 2026-05-05).
- **Gas-turbine OEMs sold out years forward with pricing power.** GEV ended Q1 with **100 GW of gas contracts** (44 GW firm + 56 GW SRAs), order book filled through 2027 with 2030 slots filling ahead of 2029 (permitting-gated); H1'26 orders priced **10–20 pts higher $/kW** y/y, plus a **~20–30% US price premium vs. Siemens** (GEV Q1 FY26, 2026-04-22; GEVernova preview, 2026-04-17). Industry-wide turbine capacity ramps 55 GW (2025) → 87 GW (2030), yet the US is still "~10 GW of gas short every year" (GEVernova preview, 2026-04-17).
- **Labor is now the #1 constraint.** Both Jefferies desks: labor — not turbines — is the gating factor; projects booked "three years in the future"; construction EPC names (Quanta, MasTec) seeing "shocking positive revisions," with Quanta's near-term growth talked up toward ~30% (Jefferies, 2026-05-08).
- **MS corroborates the ~$1.2T/2027 capex bogey with a per-company breakdown.** Morgan Stanley's hyperscaler+neocloud capex chart (AWS, GOOGL, META, MSFT, ORCL, CRWV, NBIS) sums to **~$1,207bn in 2027** — matching the Jefferies "could be 1.3, could be 1.4" bogey above — with steep '24-'27 CAGRs disclosed per name (ORCL +118%, CRWV +68%, NBIS +63%, AWS +53%). Context: the same note frames META adding **~2GW/~3.5GW owned-operated IT capacity in '26/'27** (vs AMZN ~5GW and GOOGL ~9GW in 2027) as the basis for META's "Meta Compute" neocloud-optionality sizing (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak, 2026-07-01). No new company-page action — this is confirmatory of the existing capex-wall framing, and the META/AMZN/GOOGL capacity figures are already logged on those tickers' pages.
- **Satellite build-tracking: ~14–16 GW (2026), ~20 GW (2027) — far below the announced pipeline.** SinMax/Industrial Info's "Vulcan" platform (weekly satellite imagery + 500-analyst phone verification) tracks **11 GW built in 2025 → ~14–16 GW in 2026 → ~20 GW in 2027** (vs. ~40–45 GW *proposed* for 2027 — a **~50% haircut**, ~the same ~60% realization rate seen in renewables). The gate is **labor** (1–10 electricians/MW; ~1M total US electricians, 5 retiring per 1 entrant) plus permitting/policy (Maine/Michigan/Minnesota moratoria; only ~29% public support). Crucially **the misses are delays, not cancellations** (no peak signal); once construction starts, 2025 builds ran **~5 months faster than history**; ~74% are new (greenfield) sites that compound into later years. Behind-the-meter tracked at **~54 GW total**, 248 plants; natural-gas BTM ~25 GW starting construction in 2026 (~8 GW online '26, ~11 GW '27); turbine lead times 3–5 yrs. Top regions: Texas (~$1T proposed), Virginia, Ohio, Georgia, Utah; regulated markets now *faster* than deregulated (single approver vs. long queue) (Jefferies "DC build / Vulcan-IIR", 2026-06-22).
- **The behind-the-meter power-architecture expert view (Aman Chakri / ex-GE Vernova).** Gas "will be king" five years out; BTM is a **permanent** solution (not just a bridge) because connecting hyperscalers to the grid raises rates on the public and ISOs "pick their battles." Cost is ~the same BTM vs front-of-meter — **the variance is *time*, not cost** (interconnection takes 4–6 yrs). Installed all-in cost **~$2,900–3,200/kW (BTM), ~$3,000–4,000/kW** for aero/recip; technology cost alone >$2,000/kW (aero ~$2,200/kW). AI loads need **~10-millisecond response** that **no turbine/engine can meet** — so every architecture pairs gas with **UPS + battery storage** as a buffer (the developer claim of "no batteries needed" is a "flawed argument"). DCs spec **simple-cycle (not combined-cycle)** for fast start; **N+5 / ~40% overbuild** (install ~140 MW for 100 MW of demand) for 99.999% uptime; one large frame turbine beats 10 small aeros on $/kW except at high elevation (Wells Fargo "Aman Chakri BTM expert", 2026-05-27).
- **DC broker on the ground (Chris, DC broker/advisor).** ~**16 GW** of tracked demand, of which **~40% is firm for 2026–28** (rest 2029–31); **>50% of new AI-DC development goes international past 2028** (US lacks power/capacity); SE Asia/Canada/South America the beneficiaries. **~30% of his pipeline is true grid-islanded BTM**, never intending to connect. Build cost **~$9–10M/MW for CPU/cloud, ~$12–13M/MW for AI** DCs. The "big four" (AMZN/MSFT/GOOG/META) "get whatever they want"; **Oracle is the IG-credit swing** (3 colos dropped Oracle sales coverage); neoclouds need backstops/creative financing. Power-study feasibility cost went **$500 → $300,000** as utilities vet "tire-kickers" (185 GW in queue). xAI Colossus 122-day build is "not repeatable" (Chris was on the JPM DC call, 2026-05-04).
- **Gas-turbine vs. genset economics (Norbert Neuster, ex-Cummins Power Systems).** Gas is the only non-grid option that works for 26–28 builds (SMR/geothermal "not ready"). Diesel gensets are remarkably cheap/commoditized at **~$550–600/kW today (from ~$400–450 historically), ~$1,000+/kW fully enclosed** — and prices likely **fall back** as supply catches up (peak imbalance was 24–25; diesel lead times ~18 mos heading to 12–15). **CAT is shifting diesel build slots to gas** (gas accrues aftermarket hours/revenue), which could *extend* CAT diesel lead times. Generac (w/ Weichai engines) entering hard but channel/deployment capability — not product — gates share (cf. Kohler at only ~6–8% after years). On Bloom fuel cells: skeptical without a 30–50% cost cut from ~$2,700/kW (~3x gas); much of Bloom's 12-mo demand was "desperation." Batteries don't replace gensets but let you cut ~10–20% of them (Wells Fargo "Norbert Neuster turbine/genset expert", 2026-04-10).
- **Barclays: the capex peak gets pushed *beyond* 2028 — ~36 GW of next-gen compute, demand outstripping supply (Barclays "AI's S-Curve Is Steepening", 2026-06-01).** Barclays' AI-lab-demand-driven framework now has the **capex peak pushed farther out beyond 2028 with ~36 GW of next-gen compute infrastructure**, leveling thereafter — the near-term revenue ramp is driving inference demand and **putting stress on the capacity picture (demand outstripping supply)**, expected to balance more evenly next year. Their AI-XPU-specific capex grows **+90%/+65%/+21% CY26/27/28** (vs Street total hyperscaler capex +85%/+24%/+8%), GW added 8.8 (2025) → 16.6 (2026) → 27.5 (2027) → 33.3 (2028); Barclays hyperscaler capex >$1.1T in 2028 (~25%+ above Street). The most common question they hear is no longer ROI but **"what is your ability to upside supply to meet demand"** (Barclays "AI's S-Curve Is Steepening", 2026-06-01).
- **BofA: AI-DC-systems TAM ~$1.7Tn by CY30, +45% CAGR (BofA "AI 2030: Stronger for Longer", 2026-05-13).** BofA lifts CY30 **AI-DC-systems TAM to ~$1.7Tn from ~$1.4Tn prior** (+45% CAGR off $264bn in CY25), with AI servers ~75% ($1.3Tn), networking ~20% ($316bn) and storage ~5% ($81bn); accelerators ~$1.2Tn, **standalone server-CPU racks ~$110bn** (AI CPUs $88bn) and **SRAM-based ultra-low-latency racks coexisting with HBM-GPU racks** as workload tails elongate — an additive, more-diversified compute build, with **power/packaging/capital the named constraints** (BofA "AI 2030: Stronger for Longer", 2026-05-13).
- **Hyperscaler capex-per-gigawatt benchmark (JPM hyperscaler recap).** JPM frames DC build economics at **~$35B of capex per gigawatt** and **~$12B of cloud revenue per gigawatt per year**, with Google needing **~2.5–3 GW/yr of incremental capacity** to service its 1Q26 cloud-backlog surge (driving 27 capex to ~$290–295B from ~$200B in 26). Amazon flagged **memory/component cost inflation likely adding ~20%+ per-unit to 2027 capex** (same units, higher cost) — a read-through to rising $/MW build costs across the cohort (JPM hyperscaler recap, 2026-04-30).
- **SemiAnalysis: BTM power build accelerating to 40+ GW by 2028 as grid constraints force hyperscalers off-grid (SemiAnalysis, 2026-06-24).** SemiAnalysis published analysis on power grid constraints in the AI buildout, arguing that utility grid interconnection is so constrained that the **behind-the-meter (BTM) generation pipeline is on track to exceed 40 GW by 2028**. The analysis frames BTM not as a premium-for-speed solution but increasingly as the *default* path for large AI campus builds in constrained markets. Key structural read: if BTM approaches 40 GW (vs the Jefferies/SinMax satellite-tracked ~54 GW total BTM at various stages), it implies a step-change in gas-turbine demand (GEV, Caterpillar, Cummins) and generator/UPS content (Eaton, Vertiv) that is durable through at least 2030. The analysis dovetails with Jefferies' "bring-your-own-generation" framing (2026-05-08) and the Aman Chakri BTM architecture expert (interconnection 4–6 yrs → BTM is permanent, not a bridge). For the nuclear/power names: the BTM wave is not an alternative to nuclear — it is gas-turbine-led and validates CEG/VST's view that "we don't see generation as the constraint, we see the grid as more of the bottleneck" (Bernstein, 2026-06-18). (SemiAnalysis / Dylan Patel, 2026-06-24)

## Sizing & sequential growth (the numbers)

JPM's "AI Capex 2.0" sizes the wall: top-4 US hyperscaler capex **~$410B (2025) → ~$700-725B (2026) → ~$1.1T (2027)**, part of **$5.5T total AI capex through 2030 (~$4.1T debt-financed)**. Power — not chips — is the binding constraint, and the funding gap (capex > operating cash flow from 2027) is what makes the firm-power owners scarce.

## Key debates

- **Bull / where the money is:** Demand is real, supply is static, and the scarce assets re-rate. CEG: ~$0.75T of data-center capital projected — "twice the size of the 3 largest listed US utilities" — and CEG sells "the product no one else has ready: firm, clean, 24/7 power, available now" (CEG Q3 FY25 / Q1 FY26). GEV is "an earnings-revisions machine," up off pace of EBITDA growth, with Electrification flipping from "peaking" to accelerating — TAM doubled $150B→$300B in a month and the $200B total-backlog target pulled forward to '27 (GEVernova callback, 2026-04-22). WMB: pipelines lag gas-gen, and "Williams is the ultimate manifestation of this thesis," compounding via 5x-build-multiple behind-the-meter gas for data centers (JSD via WMB page, 2026-05-06). PWR is the "pick-and-shovel" labor layer targeting >2x earnings power by 2030 (15–20% EPS CAGR) (PWR Q4 FY25, 2026-02-19).
- **Bear / what could break it:** Timing and slippage. Data-center delays are real — EIA, ERCOT and PJM all cut 2026 demand forecasts; "~50% of data centers delayed / canceled" headlines, though Jefferies calls this "normal course" (Jefferies, 2026-05-08). The big hyperscaler nuclear deals keep slipping (CEG promised announcements that haven't landed; some conversations "paused" on PJM regulatory clarity) (CEG Q1 FY26). Management teams are more cautious than the tape: VST says meaningful DC tightening doesn't begin until "late 2027 or early 2028" and load growth is "not at the extremely elevated levels… forecast by many third parties" (VST Q4 FY25). GEV order-peak risk is live — Q4'25's 24 GW / $22.2B order quarter is the comp to beat (GEVernova callback). Bernstein/Cantor procurement expert thinks the build pace is "plateauing" — "I think last year was the peak," with power and people the two gates (Bernstein DC buildout, 2026-06-11). And policy/rate-payer politics bite in an election year: PJM bills rose ~$20/mo, IPPs that signed rate-payer-protection pledges can't contract existing resources, and IPPs "are not participating in the rally" (Jefferies, 2026-05-08).
- **Timeline / inflection points:** Behind-the-meter and gas-gen scramble *now* (band-aid, 5–10% of market); large H-class turbines stay under-supplied through 2030 (relative positive), while diesel gensets and small on-site gen move to over-supply by 2027–2029 (Bernstein power names, 2026-06-18). Nuclear restarts/uprates land 2027–2034. Grid/electrification is the multi-decade compounder beyond 2030 (Bernstein, 2026-06-18). The cleanest near-term tell on supply: track the chip ramp and the order/backlog cadence at GEV (Q2 FY26 print, ~late July).

## Who's exposed (companies)

| Ticker | Angle | Read |
|---|---|---|
| [NVDA](../NVDA.md) | **Demand driver** — accelerator shipments are the root of the power scramble | The cause of the scarcity: ~40 GW of chips into the US 2025–2030 vs. ~21 GW DC built over prior 25 yrs (Cantor, 2026-06-18); DC ~90%+ of revenue, Q2 FY27 guide $91B (NVDA Q1 FY27, 2026-05-20) |
| [CEG](../CEG.md) | Largest US nuclear fleet; firm 24/7 carbon-free PPAs to hyperscalers | Net positive, timing-dependent. ~22 GW nuclear @96.8% CF; ~900–1,000 MW uprates; Crane restart; **hyperscaler deals keep slipping** on PJM clarity (CEG Q1 FY26, 2026-05-11) |
| [VST](../VST.md) | Largest merchant IPP; ~3.8 GW nuclear under 20-yr PPAs (AMZN/META) + 26 GW CCGT at ~60% util | Net positive operating leverage; mgmt cautious on *pace* — tightening "late 2027/early 2028" (VST Q4 FY25; Q1 FY26). JPM logs the **1/9/26 Vistra–Meta 20-yr PPA, 2.6 GW** across Beaver Valley/Davis-Besse/Perry nuclear plants (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16) |
| [TLN](../TLN.md) | Merchant IPP "powering-AI" play; ~1.9 GW FOTM AWS PPA at Susquehanna | Positive on FCF/sh "flywheel"; single-plant nuclear concentration + PJM co-location rules unresolved (TLN Q1 FY26, 2026-05-05). JPM recap: FERC rejected the original Talen–AWS Susquehanna BTM co-location in 2024, forcing the pivot to a **front-of-meter 1.92 GW 20-yr PPA (ending 2042)** — the canonical example of regulatory risk pushing hyperscalers toward build-your-own/build-to-transfer generation (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16) |
| [GEV](../GEV.md) | Gas turbines + grid Electrification — the pure-play on generation *and* the grid | Net positive, order-peak risk live. 100 GW contracted, +10–20pts $/kW, ~20–30% premium; Electrification TAM $150B→$300B (GEV Q1 FY26, 2026-04-22; preview 2026-04-17) |
| [PWR](../PWR.md) | EPC / labor layer — transmission, gas backup, DC interconnect; DC ~10% of work | Net positive secular. Record $48.5B backlog; targeting >2x earnings by 2030; **labor is the moat and the constraint** (PWR Q1 FY26, 2026-04-30; Jefferies 2026-05-08) |
| [AGX](../AGX.md) | Small-cap EPC for gigawatt-plus combined-cycle gas plants (Gemma) | Positive niche; "handful of firms" can do fixed-price GW-scale; >4.1 GW US gas in backlog, but backlog is lumpy (AGX Q1 FY27, 2026-06-04) |
| [WMB](../WMB.md) | Gas midstream (Transco) + behind-the-meter Power Innovation gas-gen for DCs | Net positive; ~5x-build-multiple BTM gas, 10%+ EBITDA CAGR to 2030; bear = PI terminal-value/earnings-quality after 10–12.5 yr terms (WMB Q1 FY26, 2026-05-05) |

**The bitcoin-miner / emerging-AI-infra cohort — ⚠️ plain-text tickers, NONE of these names has a wiki page (do not wikilink).** Added 2026-08-04 off the Texas/ERCOT audit note; the cohort is the "converting Bitcoin sites" leg of MS's time-to-power stack (**10/14/19 GW low/mid/high at ~90% probability**, 07-27) now covered directly. Cohort-level marks: **planned ~30 GW power portfolio**, and **8 GW of power capacity contracted to hyperscalers, neoclouds and AI-chip makers over the past two years across 20+ deals worth over $160bn** (contracted-GW basis) (Bernstein · Gautam Chhugani, "AI Infra/miners: Texas data center audit to drive more MW scarcity", 2026-08-04).

| Name (no wiki page) | Angle | Read — post-audit |
|---|---|---|
| **WULF** (TeraWulf) | Powered-shell developer, **zero Texas exposure** | **Cleanest expression of the scarcity thesis** — 3.6 GW of power access across Kentucky/Maryland/New York, **3,630 MW planned pipeline, no Texas presence**, so no Batch Zero risk. Outperform, PT $36 (px $18.83). Also in MS's PSP cohort on EV/Watt (07-27) |
| **IREN** | **Vertically integrated neocloud** — owns power *and* sells compute | Highest relative PT in the cohort (Outperform, **PT $100** vs px $39.75) precisely because it captures the rising $/MW on both sides. **5,800 MW planned pipeline / 2,750 MW Texas concentration, fully grid approved**; **~2 GW ENERGIZED in Texas** (650 MW Childress + 1.4 GW Sweetwater 1, 200 IT MW DC under construction). **The $/MW monetization series above is its exhibit** |
| **CIFR** (Cipher Mining) | Powered-shell developer with the **largest Batch Zero exposure** | **Bifurcated: approved 1.2 GW unaffected** (700 MW under construction for AWS/Fluidstack + 270 MW grid-approved + 207 MW operational Odessa BTC site) but **2 GW of the 3.2 GW pipeline needs Batch Zero approval to energize** — the audit gates ~two-thirds of growth. **3,177 MW planned / 2,977 MW Texas**. Outperform, PT $32 (px $24.16) |
| **CORZ** (Core Scientific) | **CoreWeave's host** — leasable IT load, not gross MW | **Execution timelines NOT impacted: 437 MW already delivered to CoreWeave, on track to 590 MW by early 2027.** Exposure is only the **300 MW gross Pecos expansion**. ⚠️ **1,975 MW planned pipeline is LEASABLE IT LOAD — not comparable to the gross-MW rows here.** Outperform, PT $32 (px $22.83). Covered by Madison Rezaei. See [CRWV](../CRWV.md) |
| **RIOT** (Riot Platforms) | Co-location lease cohort; **fully grid approved** | ⚠️ **The note contradicts itself: p.1 says ~2 GW grid-connected incl. ~1 GW ENERGIZED in Texas (700 MW Rockdale + 400 MW Corsicana); p.2 says "ready operational 1.7 GW capacity in Texas."** Exhibit 1's 1,700 MW is **Texas concentration of the PLANNED pipeline** (of 2,000 MW total). **Both carried, neither adopted.** Outperform, PT $30 (px $21.42) |
| **CLSK** (CleanSpark) | Existing-site expansion dependent on the Texas grid | **300 MW Brazoria expansion pending ERCOT approval**; 2,110 MW planned / 885 MW Texas. Outperform, PT $24 (px $14.65) |
| **MARA** | The cohort laggard | **Only Market-Perform in the slate**, PT $17 (px $11.75); not named in the Texas-exposure exhibit at all |

**Read-through — Forgen FPS (off-coverage electrical-distribution EPC; no wiki page).** Jefferies hosted Forgen (ex-Vertiv CEO Gary Deaterfruit) and ran a bull/bear desk call. The genuine sector datapoint: **modular "e-houses"/skids/power-train solutions are the fastest-growing electrical-distribution product** as DC customers shift labor from field to factory — Forgen's content is ~**40–90% (avg ~70–75%) of a typical e-house** (transformers, LV/MV switchgear), and the default customer ask is now "can I modularize this?" Forgen added **1.8M sq ft (→2.3M total, ~$200M)** of US capacity, taking shippable revenue from ~$1B to **$4.5–5B**, with electrical-distribution lead times **~12–15 mos vs. peers' 18–24** and data-center order growth running with the cohort (GE/ABB peers put up ~300%+ DC order growth in 1Q). BTM is a tailwind (content rises stepping voltage up/down; a "second bite" when the utility later interconnects). On 800V: e-houses fuel-cell-fed DC distribution is already shipping, but the LV product set must convert AC→DC (roadmap, not done) — the Street's bear is whether a non-hyperscaler-levered, customization-led vendor nails the 800V transition without the hyperscaler design reps (Jefferies "Forgen FPS call", 2026-06-18; "Forgen FPS bull/bear", 2026-06-22). _Read as corroboration of the e-house/modular + BTM electrical-content theme, not a coverage name._
- **Entergy** — vertically integrated regulated winner building Meta's Hyperion (Louisiana, +5 GW added), called "one of the biggest winners" alongside NiSource (Jefferies, 2026-05-08). **Bloom Energy (BE)** — fast-to-connect on-site fuel cells; ~5 deals @25 MW, scaling to ~2 GW by YE26, no-water/modular, but valuation-driven and announcement-led (Bernstein power names, 2026-06-18). **CoreWeave / Nebius / IREN** — neoclouds renting GPU capacity into the same power-scarce world; IREN has "a ton of power" and undisclosed ERCOT batch capacity (Cantor, 2026-06-18). **Caterpillar / Cummins / Baker Hughes (TurbineX)** — gas/mobile gensets doubling order books (Jefferies, 2026-05-08; Bernstein DC buildout, 2026-06-11). **Fluence (FLNC)** — grid-scale batteries, +~100% off lows on DC-storage interest (Jefferies, 2026-05-08). **Siemens Energy / Mitsubishi Heavy / Doosan** — the rest of the turbine oligopoly under GEV's pricing umbrella. **Vistra's Helix Digital Infrastructure** ($10B w/ KKR/NVDA/KIA) flagged as emerging theme (MS, briefing 2026-06-12).

**Read-through — orbital data centers (not a near-term escape valve):** with terrestrial DCs power/water/permitting-constrained, "space compute" has moved from sci-fi into US-equity discussion (cheaper launch via Starship/reusables). FundaAI's expert work argues the binding constraint is *not* launch cost but **heat rejection**: in vacuum there's no convection, so cooling is radiation-only — a 1 GW orbital DC implies ~0.5–1.5 km² of radiator + ~2.9 km² of solar + ~2,000–3,000 kg/s coolant flow, dozens of launches, in-orbit assembly, and finite (~7–10 yr) asset life. Verdict: a long-dated *option* (1 MW demo plausible soon; GW-class far off), to be de-risked by engineering milestones, not modeled as near-term capacity. The one vertically-integrated beneficiary — **SpaceX** — is now the listed read-through ([SPCX](../SPCX.md), IPO'd June 2026) (FundaAI "Deep|Space: Orbital Data Centers", 2026-06-22).

- **NEW (2026-07-07) — JPM's SpaceX initiation prices the higher-$/W third-party-compute monetization and models the terrestrial→orbital power ramp (JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07).** JPM's SpaceX initiation folds a full AI-DC power/cooling model into the name: COLOSSUS + COLOSSUS II provide **~1 GW of terrestrial compute today** (~325k NVIDIA GPUs), ramping **~8x to ~8 GW by end-2028**, with **orbital compute scaling from 2029 to ~75 GW by 2031** at capex/orbital-GW <half of terrestrial cost. The monetization tell for the theme: **third-party neocloud compute is priced at a materially higher $/W than internal use** — JPM estimates SpaceX renting to **Anthropic ~$29/W (Hopper+Blackwell), Google ~$50/W (GB300), Reflection AI ~$50/W** vs an **industry-average neocloud rate of ~$10-20/W** and only **~$3-5/W for internal Grok** — the same ">$40-50/W specialized-AI-capacity deals" signal BofA flags above (the SpaceX/Google $50/W deal is the marquee mark). Microsoft's **Fairwater Atlanta** is among the largest AI facilities JPM benchmarks. Read-through: reinforces that the marginal $/W on scarce, contractable compute keeps rising, and that space compute has a listed vehicle (see [SPCX](../SPCX.md); Nvidia-GPU substrate [NVDA](../NVDA.md); [MSFT](../MSFT.md)). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_SpaceX_The_Next_Fron_2026-07-07_5359413.html)

## Management commentary — cross-company evolution (recent quarters)

| Company | DC / hyperscaler demand | Contracted asset / PPA arc | Pricing & margin | Capital returns / leverage | Constraint flagged | Tone on pace |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [CEG](../CEG.md) | "Hotter than ever" → capex ~75% higher y/y ↑ | Meta Clinton 1.1GW + Crane 835MW; none new since → | CF 94.8%→96.8%, uprate IRRs >10% ↑ | FY26 $11–12; $5B buyback; lockup overhang → | PJM/FERC regulatory clarity ↑ | Cautious — deals keep slipping → |
| [VST](../VST.md) | ERCOT load 5–6%, PJM 2–3% to 2030 ↑ | 0 → ~3.8GW nuclear PPAs (AMZN+Meta); 3.2GW open ↑ | EBITDA Q1 record +20%; gen leverage @60% util ↑ | 2.6x→2.3x by YE27; Fitch IG; ~$600M YTD ↓lev | BTM/regulated peers winning DC share → | Cautious — tightening "late 2027/28" → |
| [TLN](../TLN.md) | PPL peak +70%/5yr; +3% weather-adj ↑ | ~1.9GW AWS FOTM; 35% GM contracted ↑ | Spark spreads +$5/MWh since 3/31; EBITDA 2x ↑ | 3.1x; $4bn @6.25%; $2bn buyback ↑lev | Single-plant; PJM co-location rules → | Constructive on FCF/sh flywheel ↑ |
| [GEV](../GEV.md) | 13GW US gas '25 vs ~18GW signed/2Q "dramatic" ↑ | 55→100GW contracted; $163B backlog ↑ | $/kW +10–20pts; ~20–30% premium vs ENR ↑ | <1x lev; $4.8B Q1 FCF; $10B buyback ↑ | Permitting/EPC/interconnect gates orders ↑ | Beat-and-raise; order-peak risk live ↑ |
| [PWR](../PWR.md) | DC ~10% of biz, fastest-growing slice ↑ | Backlog $34.5→$48.5B record; >2x EPS by 2030 ↑ | Steers to compounding over margin-% → | FCF $1.7B; M&A-funded; 15–20% EPS CAGR ↑ | Labor — "the moat and the constraint" ↑ | Multi-decade, "can't double overnight" ↑ |
| [WMB](../WMB.md) | PI BTM gas-for-DC; 5GW Siemens reserve ↑ | $1.6→$5.1bn PI sanctioned; NEO 682MW ↑ | ~5x build multiple; capex/kW falling ↑ | 4.1x (timing); 10%+ EBITDA CAGR to 2030 ↑lev | Grid/pipeline lag; PI terminal value (bear) → | Compounder; contracted CAGR 8%→9% ↑ |
| [AGX](../AGX.md) | >4.1GW US gas in backlog; DC tanks via Industrial ↑ | Backlog $1.4→$2.8B (off $3.0B peak); lumpy → | GM 11–25% range; Power 23.6%, no guidance → | $421M net cash, no debt; $200M buyback ↑ | Permits/turbines/financing gate new jobs → | Constructive niche; "handful of firms" → |

**Read:** Across the cohort the demand signal is unanimous and rising (↑), but the gating factor has migrated from generation to the *grid, permitting and labor* — GEV (turbines), PWR (labor), CEG/VST/TLN (PJM-FERC regulatory clarity) — and the merchant nuclear IPPs are notably more cautious on *pace/timing* than the equipment and EPC names, where backlog and pricing power are inflecting hardest. The scarce-supply re-rate is real, but the spread between "demand ↑" and management's hedged tone on timing is where the debate lives.

_Source: member-company earnings calls; see each company page for quarter-by-quarter detail._

Key finding: the transformer is the gating item upstream of the Kyber rack's 800V sidecar, with IEEE Spectrum cited for lead times "up to seven years" on large power transformers. GEV named as the primary gating OEM for transformers; frames the bottleneck as upstream of the "first 1 meter" of rack-level power.
  - **Nutty "Transformer Bottleneck" (nuttycld.substack.com, 2026-05-06):** Covers the industrial transformer supply chain under AI-deployment cadence pressure. AI is pulling transformer supply into cloud-deployment timelines, breaking the historical power-grid pattern. xAI Colossus built in 122 days site-to-training; Stargate Abilene Phase 1 took ~12 months; IEEE Spectrum cites "up to seven years" for large power transformers. GEV named as the primary gating OEM — transformer bottleneck is upstream of both the "first 1 meter" of power delivery and the 800V rack architecture. CEG and TLN are named as nuclear/gas-generation assets that are disproportionately valuable precisely because new-build timelines are so extended.

  🔴🔴 **NET-NEW ③ — THE TIME-VALUE OF A MEGAWATT, STATED AS A MARKET MISPRICING: *"they seem to equate about a megawatt of power in a year with a megawatt of power in three years… **It is worth fundamentally less and it is far less scarce** than a megawatt of power a year from now."* ➤ Every multi-year GW anchor on this page (NSR 275GW 2030, Jefferies ~111GW NA 2030, Bernstein 404GW pipeline / ~170GW credible) is a STOCK number with no time-discount attached; this is an operator saying the DATE is separately priced and the market is not pricing it.**

NOTHING SUPERSEDED, confirmed or moved).** 🔴🔴 **NET-NEW: the constraint on frontier AI *research* is named from inside a lab as **GPUs and SERIAL experiment time**, explicitly *“not bottlenecks of intelligence”* — a constraint one level ABOVE the power/shells/permitting stack this page tracks, and one that more gigawatts do not relieve, because it is wall-clock. RSI priced at ~3x, not 100x.** 🔴 **Compute elasticity stated: 100x less compute → *“not 100x less”* progress but *“definitely”* less — sublinear, strictly positive, and unlike serving it does NOT scale down when product usage does.   🔴🔴🔴 **NET-NEW ① — THE GATEKEEPING THESIS, WHICH IS THE FINANCING COMPLEMENT TO THIS PAGE'S POWER THESIS.** The five Gigascalers *"have been GATEKEEPING THE INVESTMENT GRADE CAPITAL that the AI buildout runs on"* and earn the spread between the offtake price they are charged and the spot price they sell at; [[NVDA]]'s backstops are a deliberate counter — *"manufacturing alternative credit anchors so Neoclouds and Neolabs can raise money WITHOUT the Gigascalers"* so non-IG operators *"can effectively borrow at IG rated pricing"*, with the objective read as *"to have TWENTY BUYERS AT SCALE RATHER THAN JUST FOUR."* ➤ **Explains the 08-29 finding already on this page that the same gigawatt earns 10% or 45% IRR depending on the customer: the offtaker's rating is the variable.**
  🔴🔴 **NET-NEW ② — THE PROPORTION, AND IT CUTS AGAINST THE CIRCULAR-FINANCING PANIC.** Gigascaler third-party leasing ~15GW (2026) → >35GW (2028), ~39GW incl. - **2026-07-30 — folded SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters" (2026-07-29, slug `Ssemianalysis.html`). 1 (large, 10-part) State-of-play bullet + 1 Sources entry. Nothing superseded.** This is the first work on this page that treats **construction method** as a driver of GW delivered, rather than generation, the grid or the interconnect queue — so it is additive by construction and does not contest any existing number. What is genuinely net-new and worth a reader's time: **(a) the labor bottleneck is now sized trade-by-trade** (electricians 30-40% of construction man-hours, shortage emerging 2027 worst in TX/OH, ~12,000 field man-hours/MW of MEP falling ~63% to ~4,500/MW in the factory with licensed-electrician hours −85%, and the wage gap explicitly *not* the lever — $34/hr field vs $33/hr factory, but ~$63/hr effective with OT/site premiums); **(b) a four-block schedule with permitting placed outside the clock everyone quotes** (18-24 months groundbreaking→IT-ready on a conventional 50 MW hall, plus 12-13 non-overlapping months of permitting → ~30-35+ months stick vs ~24-30 modular; full modular construction window 12-18 months, ~36% faster / ~30% faster than today's partly-modular baseline); **(c) the $/MW/month value of schedule, which is the number this page can actually use** — ~$500k/MW/month to an owner-operator on ~$30mn/MW of GPU depreciation over five years vs ~$190k/MW/month to a wholesale colo, so an ~8-month lead is ~$200mn / ~$4mn/MW on a 50 MW hall, **explicitly conditional on the building having been the binding date rather than power availability or GPU delivery**; **(d) ⚠️ a hard federal ceiling on module size that nobody on this page had logged — 102 inches wide and 80,000 lb gross, leaving ~24 tons on a standard deck, with the 40-ft ISO container at 96 in the permit-free/C-17-shippable envelope, superloads past ~16 ft triggering 7-21 days of bridge review per state that stack toward months, and non-uniform state thresholds (Virginia 18 ft/250,000 lb vs Ohio 14 ft/120,000 lb)**; **(e) commissioning as the unclosed gap** (3-8 months, on-site from L2 onward because the utility feed, generators and BESS only exist there, with L3 point-to-point the real bottleneck); and **(f) capacity rationing at the OEMs** — Vertiv modular lead times >12 months, Vertiv/Schneider/Siemens all selective on capacity slots with minimums and a bias to bigger projects, which is what is creating room for private challengers. Also folded, because they are the most-mentioned vendor in the note and have no wiki page: the **Schneider Electric** marks (Secure Power ~32% of the Data Center & Networks portfolio, $3bn Compass agreement + 105,000 sq ft Red Oak integration facility, addressable content ~$1.2-3.3mn/MW, group backlog >€25bn with 18-24 months of DC visibility, EcoStruxure Pod up to 40 racks against 30+ NVIDIA reference designs), plus sized marks on Comfort Systems, Sterling, EMCOR, ABB, Siemens, Modine, Jacobs and Foxconn, and the operator playbooks (Compass, QTS — including **QTS's estimate that labor pressure has raised DC construction costs ~20-30% per MW** — Aligned, Crusoe, EdgeConneX). The **NVIDIA DSX** facility-level reference design (Omniverse blueprint at GTC Washington Oct-2025 → Vera Rubin DSX formalized March 2026; **DSX Max-Q** for tokens/watt in a fixed power budget and **DSX Flex** for grid-service connection and dynamic power-draw orchestration with hybrid onsite generation) is logged here and cross-filed to [800v-dc-power](800v-dc-power.md). Nothing superseded — the ~120 GW-by-'28 mark was already on this page from MS's 12 Jul note and is logged here only as confirmation the ROIC framework uses the same base.** ⚠️ **Deliberately kept PROPORTIONATE: this is a ROIC note with no grid, generation, interconnect-queue or cooling content, and only three items were taken from it.** **The net-new item is the per-GW cost split, which is the first sourced sizing on this page of the NON-IT half of a gigawatt: MS anchors to $39bn/GW all-in for GB300 — IT capex $23bn on a 5-YEAR useful life plus NON-IT (powered shell) capex $16bn on a 15-YEAR useful life — at ~$4mn per GB300 rack and 410,256 chips per GW, producing NON-IT DEPRECIATION of ~$1bn/yr per GW and ENERGY + OTHER OPEX (energy, labor, maintenance) of ~$2bn/yr per GW inside $7.6bn/GW of total opex.** **The structural read logged with it: the shell is 41% of the capital but only ~13% of the annual depreciation charge, because it amortises over three times the life of the silicon — so powered-shell demand modelled off capex dollars and off P&L depreciation give very different answers.** **Third item: MS's utilisation assumption is 75%, explicitly labelled "in line with industry average for a well-optimized AI data center" — an industry-average-for-a-good-site figure, not a physical ceiling and not a fleet average — applied to owned infrastructure but NOT to MS's third-party/rented framework, which is why that case shows higher revenue per watt.** The three ROIC models themselves are filed on [tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md) and the capex aggregation on [hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md); nothing further from this note was stretched onto this page.
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch). 3 State-of-play bullets. Nothing superseded.** **BofA restates its US supply-gap framework with a larger, more precisely bounded top line: "US electricity demand requires the equivalent of 177GW of new capacity between 2026-30, but utilities are likely to supply just 93 gigawatts, leaving a gap exceeding 80GW."** Note this is framed differently from the ">100GW shortfall" already on the page (07-08) — capacity-equivalent gap vs generation shortfall — so they are complements, not a supersession. **Two net-new sizing lines: "hyperscalers plan to spend over $5trn in the next five years, yet only ~9% of US data centers have onsite generation" (the 9% is the penetration denominator for the whole BTM thesis), and "if 100% of BTM is met by gas, we estimate the opportunity is 84GW."** Salisbury's **158GW announced / 30-50% achievable by 2030 / 40-80GW gap** is unchanged from 07-25. ⚠️ **Recorded with the source's interest attached: this is BofA's oil-and-gas team making a Buy case for WMB/CVX/KGS/PUMP/LBRT, so it is simultaneously a demand argument for onsite generation AND a competitive argument against the grid/interconnect route — partly substitution away from grid-scale CCGT.** Both readings kept. **Second, the constraint nobody models, quantified: "cooling infrastructure should not exceed 9-10% of total data center operating or CapEx costs"** — against **next-gen GPUs at up to 2,400W TDP**, direct-to-chip liquid cooling as the standard, and the advanced alternatives **"two to three years away"** from commercial deployment (AMD principal PM, expert call). Separately, a **Microsoft program manager puts widespread 800V/SST adoption at 2030-2032**, ⚠️ **materially later than [[GEV]]'s "'27 and beyond" commercial framing** — see [800v-dc-power](800v-dc-power.md). **Third, the financing constraint has moved from "will it be funded" to "can the market digest it": Goldman's John Greenwood is "looking for capital in every nook and cranny" for ~$7.5tn of chips + data centers + power over five years — recurring, because "much of that spending is on GPUs… that need replacing every few years" — with the panel flagging "bond markets show 'digestion issues' after massive AI-related debt issuances," and the equity-side signal that "the tumble in SPCX shares… is a big red flag for the prospects of AI-linked IPOs… and without the money from those offerings, capex will inevitably slow."** Cross-filed [[GEV]], [[CEG]], [[TLN]], [[SPCX]], [hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md).
- 2026-07-25 — /wiki-ingest (Outlook). Added 4 State-of-play bullets; additive, no prior number superseded. All four sharpen the same conclusion — **deliverability, not appetite, is the binding constraint on AI power.** **(1) BofA's cross-sector power team (via Villani, 07-24)** sizes the behind-the-meter answer to its own **100GW demand-supply gap through 2030**: BTM is "the only option" since in-front-of-the-meter is spoken for and the grid is maxxed out; they identified **158GW of announced BTM projects but expect only 30-50% to get through, leaving a >50GW residual gap**, with **Chevron and Williams the main deliverers**. First quantified pipeline-vs-conversion estimate here — the working assumption for any "announced GW" figure quoted across the wiki should now be **announced ≠ delivered, haircut ~half**. **(2) BofA · Ross Fowler's NARUC 2026 takeaways** — regulators moving from "**demand only**" to "**show me they're real**," gating approved investment on transparency around customer commitments, infrastructure requirements, cost allocation, interconnection timing and local impacts; "**speed-to-market has become the critical bottleneck for AI-driven demand, and execution matters more than opportunity.**" **(3) BofA · Alex Jones (Siemens Energy note)** — the first quantified turbine capacity-vs-demand balance on this page: **GE Vernova to 30GW of gas-turbine capacity by 2030** against BofA's **industry demand of 115-120GW/yr in 2026-28 vs expected capacity of ~102GW**, plus **servicing consuming up to 25% of peak OEM capacity by 2035**; oversupply fears called "misplaced," Siemens Energy PO **€260 (~70% upside)**. Implication carried onto [../GEV.md](../GEV.md): if capacity is the constraint, an OEM order-book deceleration is a rationing artefact rather than a demand signal. **(4) New York becomes the first US state to impose a data-centre moratorium** (DataCenterDynamics via Susquehanna/Hosseini weekly) — first state-level moratorium logged; small in isolation but the concrete instance of the "local impacts" gate Fowler flags, and a possible template.

(2026-07-24)** — the hard rack-power comparison: **MI455X Helios ~240kW server / ~257kW all-in per rack vs GB300 NVL72's ~142kW / ~159kW**, i.e. ~1.6x the facility power per rack. **This contradicts the prevailing sell-side view** — BofA "Watts to Tokens" (Arya, 2026-05-25, logged on [../AMD.md](../AMD.md)) concluded AMD's platform is **"less power-hungry than Nvidia's."** Both are now carried with attribution and the disagreement **flagged, not resolved**: BofA works from marketed platform power budgets, SemiAnalysis from a bottoms-up Helios rack build; SemiAnalysis separately flags that AMD "has historically overstated TFLOPs" and suspects low MFU, so marketed TFLOPs/W parity likely overstates effective efficiency. (2) **S7 "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop" (2026-07-06)** — the energy-procurement leg now financed alongside compute: Firmus's **360MW Batam** cluster paired with a **600MW firm-energy deal with Gunvor underwriting 1.2GW of renewables + 1.5GWh of storage in South Australia by 2032**; prior 18,000-GB300 / 42MW Melbourne cluster on a **$10B Blackstone-led facility** with Coatue; SemiAnalysis caveat that Firmus still needs a DC provider or self-build before GPUs deploy. (3) **S2 "Amazon's AI Resurgence" (2025-09-03) — flagged HISTORICAL BACKFILL** (~11 months old): **>1.3GW of AWS IT capacity across three campuses in final construction for Anthropic training alone**, **just under 1 million Trainium2 in the largest campus**, in buildings *"hyper-optimized for air-cooling… identical to 5-year-old traditional AWS Cloud datacenters"* — the counter-example to the liquid-cooled-GPU norm, and a reminder that GW-of-IT-capacity ≠ GW-of-liquid-cooled-GPU-capacity. (4) **S1 "H100 vs GB200 NVL72 Training Benchmarks" (2025-08-20) — flagged HISTORICAL BACKFILL**: the measured power-efficiency ladder — **GB200 1200W/chip vs H100 700W**, yet **2.2x more power-efficient per token by Jul-2025** and a projected **4x lower Joules/token by Dec-2025**, so *"a datacenter with 100MW of utility power can train 4x more tokens"*; DLC unlocking lower PUE where chillers are minimised; the household anchor (10,791 kWh/yr = 1,232W continuous vs a single GB200's 1,200W). **DEDUP — verified already on the page, nothing re-added:** S8 "The Future of Meta Superintelligence" 07-09 (five 1GW+ titans; Prometheus ~1GW→>3GW; Hyperion ~1.5GW = 3x400MW + 3x100MW; Iowa 1GW lease; El Paso and Indiana; AWS's 800MW Project Rainier as the closest historical comparison; the "tent" fast-build design; BTM power; the 27-DC/6-campus constellation and ~22 Pbit/s AIBB) — all present in the 2026-07-09 bullet; only the canonical SemiAnalysis email slug was added to Sources.
